# X module (`odyssey.so`) functions read in full, one entry each

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so` (N32, `$gp` resolved with `ghidra/scripts/IrixGp.java` before reading). Each entry names the function, its address in the module and its size in bytes. Labels are **established** for functions whose whole listing was read next to the decompilation, and **inferred** for anything less. The role lists of the whole module are in [odyssey-so-functions.md](odyssey-so-functions.md). Offsets such as +0x1b4 in a screen record are the X server's own `ScreenRec` and window-record fields; the module keeps two screen-private slots, the board record (`odsyScreenPrivateIndex`) and the video-control record (`vcScreenPrivateIndex`).

## Stub and one-line routines

- `odsyInitAllocator` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1694d40, 8 bytes): **established.**
  Reports success without doing any work: the listing is two instructions, `jr ra` at 0x1694d40 and `li v0,1` in its delay slot at 0x1694d44, and no state is read or written.
- `odsyQueryMaxSwapBarrier` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1636cf0, 8 bytes): **established.**
  Also returns 1 and nothing else, through `li v0,1` at 0x1636cf4 behind the return at 0x1636cf0; it does not read its arguments or the board record, and no limit is written for the caller.
- `odsyInstallFakeColormap` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x163f030, 8 bytes): **established.**
  Does nothing: `jr ra` at 0x163f030 and a no-op at 0x163f034, and no argument is read.
- `odsyStoreFakeColors` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1623c70, 8 bytes): **established.**
  Returns at once: the return is at 0x1623c70 with a no-op delay slot at 0x1623c74, and no argument or global is read or written.
- `odsyWakeupHandler..M` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x158f8f0, 8 bytes): **established.**
  Returns at once: the whole routine is the return at 0x158f8f0 and the no-op after it at 0x158f8f4, so it reads and writes nothing.
- `rrmUndoubleBufferWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x161bda0, 8 bytes): **established.**
  Returns at once: the routine is `jr ra` at 0x161bda0 followed by a no-op at 0x161bda4 and reads or changes no window or board state.
- `odsyScanAudioInventory..K` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15931e0, 36 bytes): **established.**
  Tests one record: when the word at +4 of the first argument is 10 it stores 0 through the second argument and returns 1 (`li v0,1` at 0x1593200, the delay slot of the `jr ra` at 0x15931fc), otherwise it returns 0 at 0x15931f4 and stores nothing.
- `odsySetColor` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16ffb80, 56 bytes): **established.**
  Guard against a call that must not happen: it prints "odsySetColor: should not be called." with `ErrorF` (the call at 0x16ffba0) and returns; it sets no colour and touches no board register.
- `odsyInitWindowType` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1631730, 80 bytes): **established.**
  Stores the window type halfword of the window's resource-manager private (at +2): it is 1 when the window's frame-buffer-layer private has a nonzero word at +0x18 (branch at 0x1631768, store at 0x1631774) and 0 otherwise (store at 0x163177c). The two private records are found through `rrmWindowPrivateIndex` and `nfbWindowPrivateIndex` in the window's private array at +0x84.
- `rrmNeedCID` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1586c00, 96 bytes): **established.**
  Returns 0 or 1 from two stored numbers. It loads the screen's resource-manager record word at +8 and the object at +0x34 of the first argument; with no object it answers 0 when that screen word is nonzero and 1 when it is zero (branch at 0x1586c50), and with an object it answers 1 when the screen word is below the object's word at +4 (the compare at 0x1586c3c) and 0 otherwise.

## Screen controls, genlock and timing-file output

- `odsySetScrnGlock` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x169c100, 120 bytes): **established.**
  Records the screen's genlock source. The third argument must be -1 or -2 (the two values `setmon` passes to the input-sync call, -2 for its external source); any other value prints "odsySetScrnGlock: Invalid sync source: %d" with `ErrorF` (the call at 0x169c134) and the value is not stored, while a valid one is stored at +0x18 of the video-control screen record by the delay-slot store at 0x169c168.
- `odsySetExternalLock..GD` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x165a130, 72 bytes): **established.**
  Selects which external lock input the screen follows. The second argument is stored at +0x30 of the video-control screen record only when it is not negative and is below the record's count at +0x2c (the branches at 0x165a150 and 0x165a160, the store at 0x165a16c); an out-of-range number is ignored without a message.
- `odsyGenlockHandler..FD` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15ff550, 92 bytes): **established.**
  Handler run when genlock status changes. It stores its second argument at +0x1c of the video-control screen record (the store at 0x15ff58c), calls `odsyBlankScreen` with mode 0 and the screen at 0x15ff590, and returns 0.
- `odsyQryCntrl..JD` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16de370, 140 bytes): **established.**
  Answers a control query for one screen. For control 10 it writes 0 at +0x20 of the reply block; for control 5 it converts the integer at +0x7c of the board's screen record to a float stored at +0x10 of the reply (the `cvt.s.W` at 0x16de3e8); for control 11 it tests the bit selected by the second argument in the mask at +0x134 of the board record and writes 1 or 0 at +0x28 of the reply (the stores at 0x16de3cc and 0x16de3f8); every other control returns with the reply untouched.
- `odsyWriteTTfilename` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x169c180, 176 bytes): **established.**
  Writes the three-line timing-table file that `setmon` also writes: it opens the path in the first argument for writing (the call to `fopen` at 0x169c1a0; a failure returns 10), then prints the format name with "%s" and the two integers (the colour depth and the accumulation type) each with "%d" and a newline, closes the file and returns 0.

## Timing and format-file routines

- `odsyLoadTimingTable..CC` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x170a7f0, 5308 bytes): **established.**
  Builds the 320-byte video timing structure of the kernel's timing-table request from a video format file and loads it (arguments: screen, format-file handle, format record, a flag to enable the timing generator afterwards, genlock source; result 1 on success, 0 on any failure). Read as the whole 1327-instruction listing next to the decompilation, `$gp` = 0x173a9c0. The names below are resolved through the module's constant-pool slot at 0x17339b8 (base 0x172acc0); each is read from the file with `SDBgetSymbolValueScalar` into a stack word and every miss prints "odsyGetScalar: unable to find %s" with `ErrorF` and returns 0, after freeing the two table buffers. The structure is the stack block at sp+0x28, offsets below are from its start.
  Set-up. The routine keeps the file descriptor from the screen's second-level record (the pointer at +0x74, word +100) and the board record from the screen private slot `odsyScreenPrivateIndex`. It clears the structure with `bzero(…, 0x140)`, stores the caller's genlock source at +220, the refresh rate (the float at format-record +0x110, truncated) at +184 and the fields-per-frame word (format-record +0x12c) into +172, and clears the flag word at +200. The format record is the 312-byte block that `odsyBuildFmtInfoArray..HB` copies out of each format-list node, with the format name in its first 255 bytes. First it reads `AchievedFrequency` into +192, then `ODY_I2CClockSpeed` into +216, `ODY_DDCClockSpeed` into +212 and `ODY_DCBClockSpeed` into +208.
  Dual-head fields. `TTL_Sync` is read into a local and +264 becomes 0xf0 when it equals 3 and 0 otherwise. `DCDHorizontalFrontPorch`, `DCDHorizontalSync`, `DCDHorizontalBackPorch` and `ActivePixelsPerLine` are read in that order into locals; +268, +272, +276 and +280 receive each one halved (round toward zero, the `srl 31`, add, `sra 1` idiom at 0x170a998 to 0x170aa14). Before `DCDTotalPixelsPerLine` is read, its slot is preset to the sum of the four values; the file's value replaces it when the read succeeds. `TotalLinesPerFrame` and `FramesPerSecond` (a double) follow.
  Clock divider search. The pixel clock is lines times total pixels times frames per second divided by 1,000,000 (the double at pool offset 0x2560). A word at +260 is 0xbb when that exceeds 165 MHz and 0xff otherwise (the doubles 165.0 and the codes at 0x170aafc to 0x170ab1c). The search then walks three four-entry tables in the module's data at 0x1731258, 0x1731268 and 0x1731278 (values 1, 2, 4, 8; 1, 3, 5, 4; 1, 3, 5, 4), outermost loop over the third table, innermost over the first, and takes the first combination whose product times the pixel clock lies strictly between 69.0 and 230.0. The result is the code (outer index << 4) | (middle index << 2) | inner index at +292. The low byte of the total pixels per line goes to +296 and bits 13:8 of it to +300. The product's range selects +304: 3 at 140 or above, 0x13 from 80 up to 140 and 0x12 below 80. Words +308 (0x8e) and +312 (0) are constants. +252 is 1 when the halfword at +0x3a of the board's own record (the pointer at +0x5c of the private record) equals 3, else 0. When no combination fits, `ErrorF` prints "Timing invalid for DCD board" and the routine goes on: +292 stays 0, and +296 to +312 are computed from the last product tried (the range test reads the double saved at sp+0x350).
  Plain fields. `ODY_PLL_DPAControl`, `DPAOffset`, `FdBkDiv0`, `FdBkDiv1`, `InputControl`, `LoopControl`, `Osc_Div` and `OutputEnables` fill +96 to +124, `ODY_AMI_PLL_0x0` to `0x7` fill +128 to +156 and `GEN_AMI_REG1`, `REG2`, `REG5` and `REG6` fill +236 to +248. `FormatInfo_FullScreenStereo`, `Stereo`, `FieldSequential` and `Interlaced` set bits 3, 2, 5 and 0 of the flag word at +200; when interlaced is set and the first word of the field table that the pointer at format-record +0x130 names (the first field's line offset) is nonzero, bit 1 is set as well. `FormatInfo_DualHead` sets bit 4 and stores 1 in the halfword at +0x3e of the board record, or stores 0 there when the value is zero. Then `ODY_VTG_Control`, `ODY_VTG_InitState` and `ODY_VTG_ChanEn` fill +28, +32 and +36, `ODY_DAC_Cntrl` fills +160 and `ODY_DP_Cntrl` +164, and bit 1 of the DAC control value is stored in the screen private record at +0x7c (the word `odsyQryCntrl..JD` returns for control 5). `ODY_SM_Cntrl` fills +168, `FrameBufferHeight` +180 and `ActivePixelsPerLine` +176.
  Tables. For `FrameTab` and then `LineSeqTab` it asks `SDBgetSymbolElementCount`, allocates that many halfwords with `calloc(count, 2)` and fills them with `SDBgetSymbolValueArray`; the frame table pointer and count go to +0 and +8 and the line table pointer and count to +16 and +24. A failed allocation or read prints "Couldn't allocate frametable", "Couldn't get frametable", "Couldn't allocate linetable" or "Couldn't get linetable" with the routine's name and returns 0.
  Generator words. The screen private record's word at +0x88 is copied into +224 first and then chooses the source. When it is nonzero, the ten words at private +0x10c to +0x130 are copied into +40 to +76, bit 1 is ORed into +28, `ODY_GEN_CONTROL` fills +88 (a miss returns 0 at once) and `GEN_Control_mask` is read and ORed into +88; a miss of the mask is recorded and the routine returns 0 before the two reads below. When it is zero, +40 to +76 come from `ODY_GEN_lPFD`, `hPFD`, `lPFD_HI`, `hPFD_HI`, `lPFD_LO`, `hPFD_LO`, `lPSD`, `hPSD`, `lHMASK` and `hHMASK`, and `ODY_GEN_CONTROL` fills +88; a miss there is recorded and the routine returns 0 before the two reads below. In both cases `ODY_GEN_BP_CLAMP` fills +84 and `ODY_GENLOCK` +80 afterwards.
  External genlock. When the genlock source argument is -2 the routine reads `VTG_Control_mask`, `VTG_ChanEn_mask`, `GEN_Control_mask` and `VTG_ChanDisable_mask`, ORs the first into +28, ORs the third into +88, and sets +36 to (+36 OR the second) AND the fourth. It then reads `GEN_PLL_InputControl` into +228 and `GEN_PLL_Osc_Div` into +232; a miss of the latter prints the message and returns 0 after the reads.
  Load. `ioctl(fd, 0x6f64001f, structure)` loads the timing; a negative result prints "ODSY_LOAD_TIMING_TABLE Failed '%s'" with `strerror(errno)` and returns 0. When the enable flag is nonzero, `ioctl(fd, 0x6f640020, {1})` enables the timing generator; its failure is reported ("odsyEnableVTG: ODSY_BRDMGR_VTG_CONTROL Failed") and ignored. Finally the byte at +0x138 of the private record decides on a Denali reset: a value of 3 or more is reduced by 2 and the routine returns 1; 0 returns 1 at once; 1 or 2 sends a 48-byte two-wire request with `ioctl(fd, 0x6f640021, …)` (command 16, a write to the DM5 chip, port 1, 8 bytes of value 0xa4 flagged at +6, acknowledge mode 1) whose payload words are 3 and 10 when the byte is 1 and 1 and 9 when it is 2. A failure prints "Error resetting dm5". The byte is cleared and the routine returns 1.
  The kernel source's timing-information structure names its fields for the clock-speed, width, height and refresh words, which fits +208 to +216, +176, +180 and +184. The dual-head and divider fields have no counterpart there. Callers: `odsyAttachXvc` (enable 0, genlock -1), `odsyLdFmtByName..OC` and `odsyLoadAndFree` (enable 1, genlock source from the video-control record at +0x18).

- `odsyReadVideoFormat..MB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16b3220, 6292 bytes): **established.**
  Opens a `.vfo` video format file, fills a 600-byte format node from it and answers whether the format can be shown on this screen (arguments: screen, file path, node, mode 0, 1 or 2; result 1 when usable, 0 when not or on any error). Read as the whole listing next to the decompilation (the stack saves and restores were skimmed). `SDBreadOpenDatabase` failing prints "Failed to open %s", frees the node's field table (the pointer at node +0x234) and returns 0; the database is closed with `SDBcloseReadDatabase` on a success.
  Reads. The names come from the file with `SDBgetSymbolValueScalar`; a miss prints "odsyGetScalar: unable to find %s" and returns 0. `FormatName` is copied (255 bytes) to node +4 with a terminator at +0x102, or +4 is cleared when it is absent. `VfoVersion` zero prints "Illegal timing file '%s' specified" and returns 0. The format block at node +0x104 (312 bytes) is cleared; then `FieldsPerFrame` goes to +0x230, `FormatInfo_Interlaced`, `FormatInfo_FullScreenStereo`, `FormatInfo_Stereo` and `FormatInfo_FieldSequential` to locals, `AchievedFrequency` (a double) and `VerticalRetraceRate` (double) to locals. `VerticalRetraceRate` is stored as a float at +0x218; the float at +0x214 (the refresh rate) is the same value when the format is interlaced and fields per frame times it otherwise, and +0x240 is 1 for interlaced and 0 for progressive. The stereo word at +0x238 is 0 without stereo, 1 with stereo and 4 with stereo plus full-screen stereo, with bit 1 added for a field-sequential format. `ScreenHeight`, `ActivePixelsPerLine`, `TotalLinesPerFrame`, `TotalPixelsPerLine`, `FormatInfo_PixelClock`, `FormatInfo_H_BackPorch`, `H_Sync`, `H_FrontPorch` and `H_Active` fill +0x204, +0x208, +0x20c, +0x210, +0x21c, +0x220, +0x224, +0x228 and +0x22c.
  Dual-head files. `FormatInfo_DualHead` (a local), `FormatInfo_DigitalSkew` (stored at +0x254, the read's own failure is ignored) and `DCDSupport` (+0x250) follow. With dual-head set, +0x23c is 1, total lines (+0x20c) is halved, `DCDHorizontalBackPorch`, `DCDHorizontalFrontPorch` and `DCDHorizontalSync` replace +0x220, +0x228 and +0x224, the original total pixels per line is kept in +0x248 and `DCDTotalPixelsPerLine` replaces +0x210 and is copied to +0x24c. With dual-head clear but digital skew set, +0x23c is 0, +0x254 is forced to 1 and the same four reads are made. With both clear, +0x23c is 0 and +0x248 and +0x24c copy +0x210. Failures in the DCD reads are collected and the routine returns 0 after the last of them. The horizontal blanking sum (sync plus back plus front porch) goes to +0x244.
  Field table. With zero fields per frame the table pointer is cleared; otherwise it is `calloc(fields, 36)` and four temporary word arrays (`calloc(fields, 4)`) are filled from `FieldLineOffset`, `FieldLineSkip`, `FieldColor` and `FieldEye`; an allocation failure prints its own message and returns 0. Each 36-byte field entry is: word 0 line offset, word 1 line skip, words 2 to 6 the five vertical timing values (back porch, sync, sync pulse, front porch, active; from `FormatInfo_F_0_V_BackPorch`, `V_Sync`, `V_SyncPulse`, `V_FrontPorch` and `V_Active`), word 7 colour and word 8 eye. For a plain file the five values are read directly. When the file is dual-head or has a digital skew, the sync pulse is not read; the values come from the same names but are scaled by the `DCDTotalPixelsPerLine` value over the file's total pixels per line, truncated to an integer in the quotient before multiplying, and the sync value is stored in both word 3 and word 4. A zero divisor traps.
  Usability. The routine calls `odsyCanLoadOptionHW` with the screen and a flag that is 1 for a dual-head file and 0 otherwise (a zero result returns 0). The mode, taken from the fourth argument, then selects the test; a mode other than 0, 1 or 2 returns 0 here, after all the reading. Mode 1 (formats for the current screen): on a board whose halfword at +0x3e is zero (single head) the format must fit the screen (width at screen +8, height at +10) and not be dual-head; with the halfword set the format's width must be at most half the screen width, it must fit the height and be dual-head. Mode 2 (formats the current screen cannot hold): rejected when the stereo word is 4; on a single-head board it is rejected when it fits the screen and is not dual-head; with the halfword set the same width, height and dual-head conditions as mode 1 reject it when all hold. Mode 0 rejects only a stereo word of 4.
  Board and monitor tests (shared by the modes). With the option card present (halfword +0x3a of the board record equal to 3) the format is usable only when `DCDSupport` is nonzero, the pixel clock in `AchievedFrequency` is between 60.0 and 180.0 MHz (at most 220.0 when the module's global at 0x173aa10 is nonzero), it is not interlaced and has neither stereo bit 2 nor bit 4; when the global is zero, the format is not dual-head and has no digital skew, it also needs the blanking sum to be at most 500 times total pixels over DCD total pixels (as integers). A 1600 by 1024 format is refused when either of two monitor identity slots in the board record (valid word +0xc6 with bytes +0xd0 to +0xd3, valid word +0x148 with bytes +0x152 to +0x155) holds the bytes 0x4c, 0xf8, 0x41, 0x06. Without the card the format is usable when it is not dual-head (mode 1 and 0) or dual-head (mode 2) and has no digital skew. On a board whose word at +0x28 is zero and whose word at +0x18 is 2, a pixel clock strictly between 109.0 and 194.0 is refused, and 108.0 and 107.2512 are refused when the screen private record's word at +0x10 is 3. A refused format frees the field table, clears the pointer and returns 0.

- `odsyEnumDir..FB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x168bfb0, 520 bytes): **established.**
  Walks a directory under the graphics root and calls a function for each entry that matches a pattern (arguments: context word, directory path, optional regular expression, callback, two extra words; result the number of entries accepted, 0 on an error). A non-null pattern goes to `re_comp`; its error text is printed as "odsyEnumDir: regexp error: %s" and the result is 0. The path is built with `odsyMakeFileName`, opened with `opendir` (a failure prints "failed to open dir %s" and returns 0) and read with `readdir`. Entries named "." and ".." are skipped, a pattern is applied with `re_exec` (it must return 1), and with a callback the entry's name is appended to the directory path, `stat` fills a 0x400-byte block, and the callback runs with (context, full path, name, stat result, the two extra words); the entry counts when the callback returns nonzero. Without a callback every match counts. `closedir` runs before the count is returned.

- `odsyMakeFileName..EB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16b0d80, 260 bytes): **established.**
  Builds a path in the caller's buffer from the environment variable GL_GRROOT (empty when unset), an optional directory and an optional file name, and returns the address where the last piece starts. It copies the root, appends the directory (when the second argument is nonnull) and a slash when the text does not already end in one, then copies the name behind it (third argument) or terminates the buffer there.

- `odsyBuildFmtList..NB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15732d0, 280 bytes): **established.**
  Directory callback that adds one video format to a linked list (arguments: screen, full path, file name, stat block, list head pointer, mode; result 1 when added). It allocates a 600-byte node (a failure prints "odsyBuildFmtList: alloc failed!" and returns 0), fills it with `odsyReadVideoFormat..MB` (0 frees the node and returns 0), clears 255 bytes at node +4, copies the file name minus its last four characters (the ".vfo" suffix) there with a terminator, links the node at the head of the list (the node's first word takes the old head) and returns 1.

- `odsyBuildCmbList..OB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1587940, 332 bytes): **established.**
  Directory callback that packs the names of usable combination files into a buffer (arguments: screen, full path, file name, stat block, cursor pointer, mode; result 1 when the name was added). Modes 0 and 2 return 0 without reading. Otherwise `vfmtReadCmp1Combination` (the video-format library's combination reader) reads the file into a 0x1500-byte block on the stack; a nonzero result returns 0. For mode 1 the combination's width and height (the words at block +0x354 and +0x358) must fit in the screen (halfwords at +8 and +10), else 0. The file name is copied, byte by byte, to the cursor with a terminator, the cursor advances past the terminator and the routine returns 1.

- `odsyBuildFmtInfoArray..HB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1655030, 604 bytes): **established.**
  Turns the format list into an array of 312-byte format records (arguments: list head, output pointer, count; result the count, or 0 on failure). One `calloc(1, 312 * count)` holds the array and is stored through the output pointer. For each node it copies the node's 312-byte format block (node +0x104) into the entry, allocates a copy of the field table (`calloc(fields, 36)` for the entry's +0x12c fields, stored at entry +0x130, filled by copying the node's table at +0x234), clears the first 255 bytes of the entry and copies the node's name into them with a terminator. A failed allocation prints "odsyBuildFmtInfoArray: alloc failed!", frees the field copies already made, clears their pointers and returns 0.

- `odsyLstVideoFmt` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16f53f0, 224 bytes): **established.**
  Lists the installed video formats (arguments: screen, an unused second argument, output pointer for the array, mode). It runs `odsyEnumDir..FB` on the directory "/usr/gfx/ucode/ODSY/vof" with the pattern ".*\.vfo" and `odsyBuildFmtList..NB`, then, if the count is positive, `odsyBuildFmtInfoArray..HB`. A count that differs from the array's prints "odsyLstVideoFmt: odsyBuildFmtList (%d) != odsyBuildFmtInfoArray (%d)". The list nodes are freed by following their first word, and the routine returns the array builder's count (0 for an empty directory).

- `odsyLstVideoFmtCombs..BC` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x166d390, 120 bytes): **established.**
  Lists the combination files (arguments: screen, buffer, two output words, mode). It runs `odsyEnumDir..FB` on "/usr/gfx/ucode/STINGRAY/cmb" with the pattern ".*\.cmb" and `odsyBuildCmbList..OB`, using the buffer as the callback's cursor, and stores the count through the first output pointer and the number of bytes the callback used through the second.

- `odsyInitMonitorProtocol..CD` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x156ee40, 200 bytes): **established.**
  Starts the two-wire monitor protocol on one head (arguments: screen, head number). It clears a 48-byte request, sets the command halfword at +2 to 10, sends it with `ioctl(fd, 0x6f640021, request)` (the kernel's two-wire command request; command 10 is its protocol-initialise command), and stores 1 or 0 in the head's flag at +0x1ec of the 504-byte per-head entry (the array pointer at +8 of the video-control screen record, 504 bytes per head) when the ioctl succeeds or fails. It returns nothing.

- `odsyMonitorCommand..BD` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15e7f60, 252 bytes): **established.**
  Checks the monitor protocol of one head (arguments: screen, head number, skip flag, and two words for a status copy). When the head's flag at +0x1ec of its entry is not positive it returns 10. When the skip flag is nonzero it returns 0 without calling the kernel, after storing 0 through the last argument if the fifth argument is nonzero. Otherwise it sends a 48-byte request with command halfword 12 through `ioctl(fd, 0x6f640021, request)` and returns 0 on success; on failure it clears the head's flag and returns 10.

- `odsyValidateReservedMode` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x168bc90, 252 bytes): **established.**
  Makes the board show a reserved window id's display mode (arguments: screen, window id). It sends the id and the mode word of the id's resource-manager entry (36 bytes per entry, mode at +0xb4) with `ioctl(fd, 0x6f64001d, request)`, the kernel's display-mode request. A failure prints "odsyValidateReservedMode: ODSY_BRDMGR_SETDISPLAYMODE ioctl failed" and a second line with the id, the mode and `strerror(errno)`. Either way it ends with `rrmSetReservedDID` for that id with the entry's words at +0xb0 and +0xb4.

- `odsyGetDimensionsFromTTFile` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x157ced0, 484 bytes): **established.**
  Reads the frame buffer size of a named video format (arguments: board-private record, format name, pointer for the width, pointer for the height; result 0, or -1 when the file cannot be opened). It copies the name, appends ".vfo" unless the name starts with a slash, builds the path under the graphics root with `odsyMakeFileName` ("/usr/gfx/ucode/ODSY/vof") or, for an absolute name, uses the name as given after cutting it at the first newline, and opens it with `SDBreadOpenDatabase`; failure prints "odsyGetDimensionFromTTFile: SDBreadOpenDatabase failed to open '%s' with errno %d" and returns -1. `FrameBufferHeight`, `FrameBufferWidth` and `FormatInfo_DualHead` are read in that order; a miss prints "odsyGetScalar: unable to find %s", stops reading and still returns 0. When the board record's halfword at +0x3a is 3 (option card) and the file is dual-head, the width is doubled. The database is closed before the return.

- `odsyCmpIoctl` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1677290, 476 bytes): **established.**
  Sends one compositor register request (arguments: screen, 20-byte request: byte 0 is the two-wire address byte with the read or write bit in bit 0, byte 1 the register, word +4 the data). The board record's compositor word (+0x1cc) selects the route. When its bits 2:1 are set and bit 0 of the request's first byte is clear (a write, since a set bit 0 is a read in the two-wire address convention) the routine returns 0 with nothing sent. When the module's table of compositor links (80 bytes per entry, up to 32, index in bits 15:8 of the compositor word) has a positive descriptor at entry +8 and the address byte is 0xa5, 0xaa, 0xab, 0xd4 or 0xd5 (except 0xab with register 0x57), it sends a 20-byte request with `ioctl(descriptor, 0x636d7004, request)` over that link: word 0 is 0x14, word 1 is 1 for a write and 0 for a read, word 2 is 0 for 0xd4 and 0xd5, 1 for 0xaa and 0xab and 3 for 0xa5, word 3 the register and word 4 the data. A failure prints "USB Failure on Compositor link" and returns -1; on success a read (bit 0 set) stores the returned data in the request's word +4. Every other case goes to the kernel with `ioctl(fd, 0x6f640025, request)` and returns its result.

- `odsyReadTTfilename` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x162ed60, 852 bytes): **established.**
  Reads the per-screen timing-table file that `odsyWriteTTfilename` writes: a format name, then a depth, then an accumulation-buffer type, one per line (arguments: screen, SDRAM size in megabytes, path, name buffer of 512 bytes, depth pointer, accumulation pointer; result 0 when everything was read as written, 1 when the file was created or was empty, 2 when a value was replaced, 3 on an error, 10 when the file cannot be opened). `stat` on a missing file with errno 2 creates it with `creat(path, 0644)` and returns 1 after `odsyValidateBoardConfig` has chosen defaults and "No user specified screen size or depth..." and the chosen table, depth and memory are printed; any other `stat` failure does the same and returns 3. An existing file is opened "r+" (failure returns 10). The first line (newline cut) is the name; an empty file takes the defaults and returns 1. The second line is the depth: it must be 8 or 16 (the two-entry table at 0x1738240), otherwise `odsyValidateBoardConfig` picks one, a message says so unless the file was empty and the result becomes 2. The third line is the accumulation type: 0 and 1 pass, any other value prints "invalid accumulation buffer type specified, using software accumulation buffer" and becomes 0, and a nonzero type with less than 128 MB of SDRAM prints the 128 MB message and becomes 0 (result 2). A missing line prints the first message and the type becomes 0. The file is closed before the return.

- `odsyCheckCorona` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16683b0, 632 bytes): **established.**

It takes the monitor identity data from the board record: with the option card (halfword +0x3a equal to 3) the slot whose valid halfword is nonzero at +0xc6, else the one at +0x148, and without the card the slot at +0x44; the 128-byte block after the valid halfword is copied to the stack. If a block was found and bit 5 of its byte 0x18 (the monitor's active-off support) is set, it zeroes the module's default screen-saver interval and the screen-saver interval and installs `odsyPowerSaver` in the saved screen record's slot at +0xc. Otherwise it searches the monitor table (`numMonitors` entries of 0x4c bytes in `moninfoData`) for a case-insensitive match with the name `strcasecmp` gets from the video-control record, and in the matching entry for a record of type 14 among its 12-byte records (count at +0x44, pointer at +0x48): a value of 1 there gives the same power-saver set-up and any other value sets the default interval to 600,000 (10 minutes); a matching entry without a type 14 record changes nothing. An empty table sets the default interval to 600,000, and a table with no matching name changes nothing.

Read from the decompilation, with the branch skeleton of the listing checked against it; the unrolled 128-byte block copies were skimmed. The board record is the pointer at +0x5c of the screen's board-private record. Its words at +0x18 and +0x28 are tested together (called A and B here): "A equal to 2 and B zero" selects the 1280 by 720 default below, and +0x1cc is the compositor word. With no valid block at +0x44, and when the manufacturer word is neither 0x4ce9 nor 0x4cf8, the name is "1280x720_72" when A is 2 and B is 0 and "1280x1024_60" otherwise. For those two manufacturer words the product code (byte 10 plus 256 times byte 11) decides: 0x1100 gives "1280x720_72"; 0x0100 and 0x2000 give "1280x1024_60", with depth 8 when A is 2 and B is 0; 0x2100 gives "1600x1024_60"; 0x0200 and 0x420c give "1280x720_72" when A is 2, B is 0 and the version is not 1 or the revision is 0 (and "1280x1024_60" when that condition fails), and when the version is 1 and the revision nonzero the name descriptor text (copied up to a newline into a 33-byte buffer) is compared with "GDM-4011P": a different monitor gets "1280x1024_96" and that monitor gets "1280x1024_60", with depth 8 when A is 2 and B is 0. With neither block valid the name is "2@1280x1024_60" after the message "no valid EDID found, use default dual channel timing table" when the compositor word is zero, and "1280x1024_60" without a message when it is not. With a valid block: bytes 8 and 9 equal to 0x4c and 0xf8 with product bytes 10 and 11 equal to 0x00 and 0x21 give "2@1600x1024_60_ds"; otherwise "2@1280x1024_60", with depth 8 when bit 7 of byte 0x14 (digital input) is set in both copies.
  The name, depth and accumulation words are each written only through a nonnull pointer.

## Compositor routines

All compositor register writes go through `odsyCmpIoctl` as a request whose first byte is 0xaa (the two-wire address byte with bit 0 clear, a write), second byte the register number and word +4 the data. "Register n" below means that write. Unless stated, a failed write prints "Failure writing to compositor" and ends the routine.

- `odsyCompositorExternalWrite..KC` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1711bc0, 252 bytes): **established.**
  Writes one register of a device behind the compositor's own two-wire master (arguments: screen, device address, register, value; result 0, or -1 with "Error writing external compositor reg"). It writes compositor register 0x55 three times with the data (device address and 0xff) or 0x1300, then (register and 0xff) or 0x1200, then (value and 0xff) or 0x1a00. The three halves are the address, the sub-register and the data of the external device.

- `odsyProgramCompositors..GC` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15af3a0, 276 bytes): **established.**
  Numbers every open compositor (no arguments; returns 0). It walks the module's 32-entry table of compositor links (80 bytes per entry, descriptor at +8 of the entry) and for each entry with a positive descriptor sends a 20-byte request with `ioctl(descriptor, 0x636d7004, request)`: word 0 is 0x14, word 1 is 1 (a write), word 2 is 1, word 3 is 0x5a (the register) and word 4 is the one-based entry number shifted left 8. A failure prints "odsyProgramCompositors ioctl error: %s" with `strerror` and the walk continues.

- `odsyQueryCompositors..DC` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1636a70, 304 bytes): **established.**
  Finds the compositor devices the operating system offers (no arguments; result the number of open links, or -1 when the module's count at 0x173b418 is already nonzero). It opens the directory "/hw/compositor", skips names that start with a dot, converts each name with `atoi`, and for numbers 0 to 31 opens "/hw/compositor/%d" read-write; a descriptor above zero goes into entry n of the table (at +0x58 from the table start, 80 bytes per entry, so entry n sits where `odsyCmpIoctl` finds compositor number n+1) and the count is incremented. The directory is closed before the count is returned.

- `odsyPowerSaver..IE` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x163d150, 244 bytes): **established.**
  Screen-saver callback that blanks and restores the display (arguments: screen pointer, mode; result 0, 1 or 3). Mode 0 returns 0 and does nothing. Mode 1 clears the screen's saver flag (a word per screen at 0x173f330 + 4 times the screen number) with `odsyBlankScreen(0, screen)` when it was set, and returns 0. Mode 3 returns 1 when the flag is already set and 0 when the saved screen record's byte at +8 is 2; otherwise it calls `odsyBlankScreen(2, screen)`, sets the flag and returns 1. Any other mode returns 3.

- `odsySetCompositorTiling..PC` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16ad200, 1200 bytes): **established.**
  Programs the compositor's four tile windows from a combination record (arguments: screen, combination record; result 1 on success, 0 on a failed write). It first clears bit 5 of the control shadow at +0x84 of the board-private record and writes it to register 0. Then, for tile k from 0 to 3, it reads the four words at combination offsets 0x310 + 16k to 0x31c + 16k (a, b, c, d) and writes register 0x23 + k with ((c divided by 4, rounding toward zero) shifted left 16 and masked to 0x03ff0000) or ((a divided by 4) and 0x3ff), then register 0x27 + k with (b and 0xfff) or ((d shifted left 16) and 0x0fff0000). The order is 0x23, 0x27, 0x24, 0x28, 0x25, 0x29, 0x26, 0x2a. It ends by writing the shadow with bit 5 set to register 0.

- `odsyCompositorConfigurePLL..MC` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1655f40, 928 bytes): **established.**
  Sets up the compositor's genlock from a named sync format (arguments: screen, format name; result 1 after a missing or non-genlock format, 1 after a complete setup, 0 after a failed write). `vfmtFindCmpFormatFile` and `vfmtReadCmp1Format` read the format into a 0x540-byte block; a failed read prints "Failed to read sync format %s" and returns 1. When the block's word at +0x54c is zero the message "Format %s cannot be used for genlocking" is printed and the result is 1. Otherwise it writes register 0x41 from block word +0x130, 0x42 from +0x134, 0x43 from +0x128 and 0x44 from +0x12c, register 0x40 with 0x02800000 or (block +0x140 and 0x3ffff) or 0x80000 when block +0x138 is nonzero (0x300000 when it is zero), and register 0x50 with (block +0x148 and 0x1fff) or 0x4000. Then three external-device writes through `odsyCompositorExternalWrite` to device 0x4c: register 0 with 0x5e, register 7 with 0 and register 4 with 0 (their failures are not checked), register 0 with 1 and finally register 0 with 0x4020.

The name is the sixth argument, or the fourth when that is null; with neither the routine returns 1. Read as the whole listing; the stack housekeeping was skimmed.
  Format record. `vfmtFindCmpFormatFile` and `vfmtReadCmp1Format` read the format into a block (the offsets below are from its start); a failure prints "failed to find format %s!" and returns 1. The head's entry in the video-control record (504 bytes per head) gets its old field table freed, its format record cleared, the name copied (255 bytes at most) to +0x14, +0 set to 1, +4 and +8 to 0.0, +0xc to the height (block +0x1ec) and +0x10 to the width (+0x1f0) as floats. The 312-byte format record at +0x14 takes the height at record +0x100, the width at +0x104, total lines (block +0x1e4) at +0x108, total pixels per line (+0x1e8) at +0x10c, the rate (the double at +0x1d8, as a float) at +0x110, the product of the word at +0x524 and the rate at +0x114, the integer product of lines, pixels and rate (the pixel clock) at +0x118, block words +0x508, +0x50c, +0x510 and +0x504 at +0x11c to +0x128 and the field count (block +0x5e0) at +0x12c. Register 1 is written with 0x1c31. A divider search over n from 3 to 129 keeps the values with 0.008 <= 12/n <= 0.5 (so n from 24 up), takes m as floor(clock in MHz times n / 12), achieved = m times 12 / n, requires 100 < 2 times achieved < 500 and keeps the (m, n) with the smallest distance of achieved from the target (starting from 99.0).   Reading (first part). For each open compositor in the table of links (80 bytes per entry, `gp` - 0x1a78 up, count at 0x173b418) a read request goes through `ioctl(descriptor, 0x636d7004, request)`: word 0 is 0x14, word 1 is 0 (a read), word 2 is 3, word 3 the register and the data comes back in word 4. Register 3 holds a board offset; the record starts at ((offset times 8) plus 6) and holds length-prefixed strings whose length is the low 5 bits of the first byte. The first string (the manufacturer) is skipped. The second (the product name) is read byte by byte and right-aligned in a 14-byte field padded with spaces. The third (the serial number) is right-aligned in a 9-byte field, and its first six characters are also copied to a short field. The fourth (the part number) is right-aligned in an 18-byte field with each underscore replaced by a hyphen. The fifth (the dash level) is right-aligned in a 6-byte field. Afterwards ten bytes behind the dash level take 0x30 with two 0xff markers. A failed read prints "Failure reading NIC board offset: %s", "manufacturer", "product name", "serial number", "part number" or "dash level" (the same text with that item) with `strerror(oserror())`, sets the entry's error word to 1 and goes on to the next compositor.
  Distributing (second part). For screens 0 to 15 it calls `ddxGetBoardInfo`; a board whose word at +0x1f4 has a nonzero compositor number in bits 15:8, whose bytes at +0x20a and +0x20c are both zero, whose compositor entry has a positive descriptor and whose screen has a kernel descriptor (word +0x64 of the record at +0x74 of the screen) gets an 80-byte request: the compositor number and then the entry's 76-byte record. `ioctl(descriptor, 0x6f640027, request)` sends it; when it succeeds the same 76 bytes are copied into the board record at +0x200.

- `odsyQryMonitor` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x171f9b0, 1024 bytes): **established.**
  Writes a monitor name for one head into the video-control record (arguments: screen, head; no result). The destination is the 64-byte slot at 72 bytes per head from the string array that the video-control record's first word points to. The identity block comes from the board record: with the option card (halfword +0x3a equal to 3) the block at +0xc6 for head 0 and at +0x148 for the other head, without it the block at +0x44; a zero valid halfword gives "unknown". The 128-byte block is copied to the stack. For manufacturer words 0x4cf8 and 0x4ce9 the product code (byte 10 plus 256 times byte 11) selects a name: 0x1100 gives "SGI-4352", 0x0100 "SGI-256", 0x2000 "SGI-8192" and 0x2100 "SGI-8448". Any other product code of those manufacturers requires byte 0x12 equal to 1 and byte 0x13 above zero, and then copies the text of the monitor-name descriptor (up to a newline or 33 characters, starting at byte 0x5f) and gives "SGI-512" when it equals "GDM-4011P" and the text itself otherwise; without a version 1 block the name is "unknown". Any other manufacturer word is decoded into three letters (each five-bit field plus 0x40, from bits 14:10, 9:5 and 4:0) and written with "-%d" and the product code, for example the letters, a hyphen and the product number.

- `odsyListFormatsInComb..IB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15b5460, 872 bytes): **established.**
  Builds the format records of one combination file for a listing (arguments: an unused first word, the combination file name, a pointer that receives the record array, a pointer written with 0 on failure, a pointer that receives the record count; no result). The file is read with `vfmtFindCmpCombinationFile` and `vfmtReadCmp1Combination`; a failure prints "failed to load combination %s!" and stores 0 through the fourth argument. Otherwise it allocates 36 bytes per field of the first format plus 0x48 bytes plus one record (312 bytes) when the combination's mode word at +0x1484 is zero or three records (936 bytes) when it is not, and stores the record count (1 or 3) through the fifth argument. Record 0: the name from block +0x378 (255 bytes, terminated), height at +0x100 from block +0x564, width at +0x104 from +0x568, total lines at +0x108 from +0x55c, total pixels at +0x10c from +0x560, the rate as a float at +0x110 from the double at +0x550, a float at +0x114 from the word at +0x89c times the rate, the integer pixel clock at +0x118 from lines times pixels times rate, block words +0x480, +0x484, +0x488 and +0x47c at +0x11c to +0x128, the field count (block +0x558) at +0x12c and a pointer to a field table after the records at +0x130. Each 36-byte field entry takes the line offset (block +0xc8 + 4k), the line skip plus one (+0xd4 + 4k), five vertical timing values from the groups at +0x90 + 20k (+0x90, +0x94, +0x98, +0x9c and +0x8c) and the constants 7 and 1 in words 7 and 8. With a nonzero mode word, record 1 at +0x270 is filled the same way from the second format of the block (name at +0x1384, height +0x1498, width +0x1494, lines +0x14ac, pixels +0x14a4, rate +0x14d0, field count 1 or 2 by block word +0x14b8, pixel clock divided by the field count) and its first word is set to 1; the third record stays cleared.

- `odsyProbe` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1718af0, 260 bytes): **established.**
  Checks that the module can drive the board it is offered (arguments: dynamic-driver version, board descriptor; result 1 or 0). A version other than 0 or 3 prints "odsyProbe: Dynamic DDX versions don't match.", "(Expected %d, got %d)" and "ODSY DDX not loaded" and returns 0. The descriptor's byte at +0xc is the board number: 0xff makes the routine call `ddxFindFirstTypedBoard("odyssey")`, store the result there and return 1 when it is not 0xff; any other number is looked up with `ddxGetBoardName` (a failure returns 0) and the result is 1 when the name equals "odyssey".

## Board control routines

- `odsySetCntrl..ID` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16a56d0, 492 bytes): **established.**
  Sets one screen control (arguments: screen, head, control number, value block; no result). The kernel descriptor is word +0x64 of the record at +0x74 of the screen. Control 10 stores 0 at +0x20 of the value block and returns. Control 5 reads the float at +0x10 of the block: below 1.0 it stores 0 at +0x7c of the board-private record, otherwise 1, and sends the word with `ioctl(fd, 0x6f640023, &private+0x7c)` (the kernel's DAC gain request; failure prints "odsyEnableVTG: ODSY_BRDMGR_SET_DAC_GAIN Failed '%s'"). Control 11 acts only for the last head (the video-control record's word +4 minus one) on a board whose record word +0x1d0 is nonzero: it sends a 48-byte two-wire request (halfword 1 at +0, command 16 at +2, device byte 0x9b at +6, port 1, 4 bytes with the word 1 when the block's word at +0x28 is nonzero and 0 otherwise) through `ioctl(fd, 0x6f640021, request)`; a failure prints "odsy DM5 blanking: ioctl failed: %d" and ends. On success bit n of the mask at +0x134 of the board record (n the head) is cleared and set again when the block's word +0x28 is nonzero (the mask `odsyQryCntrl..JD` returns for control 11). Every other control number returns without action.

- `odsySetChannelInputRectangle..IC` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x156f690, 500 bytes): **established.**
  Moves the input window of the option card's second channel (arguments: screen, head, then the new x and y as floats; no result). It acts only when the head is the last one and the board record's word at +0x1d0 is nonzero. The head entry's width (+0x10) and height (+0xc) are read; x and y are clamped to at least 0 and so that the rectangle stays inside the screen (halfwords at +8 and +10 of the screen). The DM5 chip is then written through `ioctl(fd, 0x6f640021, request)` with a 48-byte two-wire request (command 16, device byte 0xa9, port 1, 16 bytes holding the high byte and the low byte of x, then of y, one in each word). A failure prints "SetChannelRect: ioctl failed. %d". On success the entry's +4 holds x, +8 holds y and +0xc and +0x10 are rewritten with the height and width.

- `odsyDisplayInit` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16d9fa0, 532 bytes): **established.**
  Programs the board's window-id display modes at screen start (argument: screen; no result). The per-screen table of window modes (480 bytes per screen, the pointer stored at +0x58 of the board-private record) is cleared. The record's word at +0x10 indexes a word table at 0x1738228: a value of 8 selects the 25-row mode table at 0x1738248 and 16 the 29-row table at 0x1738378 (the pointer is saved in the module's word at 0x173b41c). Each row holds a window id, a flag and a display-mode word; the three words are copied into the screen's table at 12 bytes per window id and sent with `ioctl(fd, 0x6f64001d, {id, mode})` (the kernel's display-mode request). A failure prints "odsyDisplayInit: ODSY_SETDISPLAYMODE ioctl failed" and "wid = %d, mode = 0x%x, errno = %d" and the loop goes on. Every row then calls `rrmSetReservedDID(screen, id, flag, mode)`. At the end bit 5 is set in the halfword at +0x528 of the resource-manager screen record. The tables list window ids 0 to 19 and 0x20 to 0x24, and the larger table 0 to 0x17 and 0x20 to 0x24; each of the ids 0 to 7 has flag 0, ids 8 to 13 flag 1 and the ids from 0x20 up flag 0x3c00 with the mode words 0xa1, 0xa9, 0xb1, 0xbb and 0xc3.

- `odsySetFBConfig..KD` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16081e0, 832 bytes): **established.**
  Changes the frame-buffer depth or the accumulation-buffer type and records it in the timing-table file (arguments: screen, what to change, request block; result 0, 1, 2, 10 or the result of the file write). It builds the path "/var/X11/Xvc/Odyssey%d_TimingTable" and reads the current name, depth and accumulation type with `odsyReadTTfilename` (a result of 3 prints "unable to create or read file '%s'" and returns 10). What to change 0 sets the depth from word 0 of the block: on a board whose words at +0x18 and +0x28 are 2 and 0 a depth of 16 with a format name starting "1280x1024_60" prints "16 byte framebuffer depth not supported" and "with 1280x1024_60* video format." and returns 1; a depth other than 8 or 16 (the two-entry table at 0x1738240) prints "Invalid framebuffer depth '%d'" and returns 2. What to change 1 takes the accumulation type from word 1 of the block: 0 and 1 are accepted, anything else prints "Invalid accumulation buffer type '%d'" and returns 2. After an accepted change the size of the current format comes from `odsyGetDimensionsFromTTFile` and, when that succeeds, a probe request goes through `ioctl(fd, 0x6f64001b, {width, height, depth, accumulation, 1})`; a nonzero answer prints "memory oversubscribed by the selected configuration" and returns 1. The file is written with `odsyWriteTTfilename` (failure prints "unable to write '%s'.") and its result is returned. Any other value of the first selector only writes the file.

- `odsyKernInit..O` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15ce820, 780 bytes): **established.**
  Starts a board's kernel side at screen set-up (arguments: screen, a word handed on to `irixKernInit`, a pointer to a word placed in the attach request, a pointer to the kernel descriptor; result 1, or 0 after any failure). Read as the whole listing. It calls `irixKernInit` for the screen (a zero result returns 0), then configures the frame buffer with `ioctl(descriptor, 0x6f64001b, request)` where the request holds the board-private record's words at +0x18 and +0x1c, the word of the table at 0x1738228 that the record's +0x10 selects, the record's +0x14 and a zero (no probe); a failure prints "odsyKernInit: ODSY_BRDMGR_CONFIG_FB failed" and returns 0. The five reply words are stored in the record at +0x20 to +0x34. The module keeps a pointer array of 32 per-board state regions (the array of 0x80 bytes is allocated on first use); the board number at record +0x5c must be below 32 ("Number of boards larger than allowed"). For a board without a region it prints "attaching for brdnum=%d", allocates 0x2e80 bytes aligned to 0x4000 ("cannot allocate buzz state region" on failure), clears them and sends the memory-attach request `ioctl(descriptor, 0x6f640000, request)` with -1 in word 1, the word the third argument points to in word 2, the region address at +0x10 and 0 at +0x18; a failure prints "odsyKernInit: ODSY_MM_ATTACH failed", frees the region and clears the table entry. On success the reply fields at +0x18 and +0x20 of that block are stored in the region at +0x2b7c and +0x2b78. Finally the configure reply words at +0x14 and +0x18 go to record +4 and +8.

- `odsyLoadCmap` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16f0ec0, 708 bytes): **established.**
  Loads a colour-map change into the board (arguments: screen, request block; no result). The block holds a count in its first halfword and a pointer at +0xc to 8-byte entries of an index and a packed colour (three 10-bit components in bits 29:20, 19:10 and 9:0). First every entry's colour word is stored into the board-private record's shadow table (the pointer at +0x54, four bytes per index). When the screen has a video-control colour record (`XSGIvcScreens[screen]`), every entry whose index is below 0x1900 is then corrected through the tables that the record's pointer chain (+0x24, then +0xc) leads to: each 10-bit component v is widened to 12 bits as ((v shifted left 2) or (v shifted right 8)) and compressed to a table index (the value itself below 0x200, 0x100 plus half of it below 0x400, otherwise 0x200 plus a quarter), and the 16-bit table entry is looked up at index times 2 in a table of 0x600 entries; the component in bits 29:20 uses the table at +0, bits 19:10 the one at +0xc00 and bits 9:0 the one at +0x1800, and the new colour word is (first result shifted left 14 and masked to 0x3ff00000) or (second shifted left 4 and masked to 0xffc00) or (third shifted right 6 and masked to 0x3ff), written back into the entry. Finally, when the word at +0x13c of the board-private record equals 1, the block is sent with `ioctl(fd, 0x6f64001a, block)` (the kernel's colour-load request); a failure prints "odsyLoadCmap: cmap load error".

## Cursor and gamma routines

In this section and the four after it, **established** means the decompilation was read in full and the listing was checked at every address and constant the entry cites; **inferred** marks an entry where the listing was checked less, and the entry says which parts.

- `odsyLoadGamma0` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1609d70, 624 bytes): **established.**
  Loads an all-zero gamma ramp and, when a video-control record exists for the screen, blanks the ordinary colour map as well (argument: the screen; no result). It reserves a 0xf810-byte block on the stack (a zero result at 0x1609dc0 prints "odsyLoadGamma0:  couldn't allocate memory" and returns) and builds a colour-load request in it: a count of 0x1f00 in the first halfword (0x1609dc8) and the entry array at +0x10, 8 bytes an entry (index, packed colour). The first 0x600 entries are written in the order the loop at 0x1609df0 enumerates the compressed ramp index (the sample value runs 0 to 0x1ff one at a time, then 0x200 to 0x3ff in steps of 2 and 0x400 to 0xfff in steps of 4, and the entry number is the sample for the first range, half of it plus 0x100 for the second and a quarter of it plus 0x200 for the third), each with index 0x1a00 plus that entry number (the addiu at 0x1609e00) and colour 0: the 0x600-entry gamma ramp at colour-map index 0x1a00 that `docs/buzz.md` places at `odyssey.c:467-483`. The other 0x1900 entries (the loops at 0x1609eb0) carry indices 0 to 0x18ff, each with the current colour from the board-private record's shadow table (the pointer at +0x54). The routine then writes every entry's colour back into that shadow table (0x1609f00 to 0x1609f28): the gamma indices are zeroed in the shadow and the ordinary ones are rewritten with their own values. If the screen has a video-control record (the slot at `vcScreenPrivateIndex`, tested at 0x1609f2c), the colour of every entry whose index is below 0x1900 is set to 0 in the request only (the store at 0x1609f68), after the shadow copy, so the shadow keeps the real colours. The request goes to the kernel with `ioctl(descriptor, 0x6f64001a, block)`, the descriptor being the word at +100 of the structure behind screen +0x74, and a nonzero result prints "odsyLoadGamma0: cmap load error". Unlike `odsyLoadCmap`, it does no table correction and never tests the board word at +0x13c. The reading that this blanks the display is **inferred** (a zero ramp and, with video control, all-zero colours; nothing in the routine names its caller's purpose).
- `odsyInstallCursor` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1605c90, 704 bytes): **established.**
  Programs one cursor image and position on the board (arguments: the realized cursor record, hotspot x, hotspot y, screen). It sends the kernel's cursor request `ioctl(descriptor, 0x6f64001c, block)` (kernel `gfP_OdsyBRDMGRcursor`, flag meanings in `docs/display.md`, "Kernel cursor request") five times, each with the flag in the block's first word and its own failure message. (1) Flag 4 with the control word at +0x44 of the board-private record and bit 1 cleared: the cursor is switched off while the image changes ("ODSY_SET_CONTROL ioctl failed"). (2) Flag 0x30, glyph pointer at block +0x18 equal to the buffer pointer at +8 of the cursor record and alpha pointer at +0x20 equal to that buffer plus 0x200, which is the 512-byte glyph part followed by the 256-byte alpha part that `odsyDisplayCursor` fills ("ODSY_CURSOR_SET_GLYPH ioctl failed"). (3) Flag 4 again: when the halfword at +0x3e of the structure behind record +0x5c is 1 and bit 4 of the control word is set and the video-control record's object at +8 exists, bit 5 of the control word is set if the float at +0x1fc of that object is not above the global pointer position `coreX`, and cleared otherwise; without that condition bit 5 is cleared; either way bit 1 is cleared (the test at 0x1605d98 and the compare at 0x1605eec). I read bit 4 as dual-head enabled and bit 5 as pointer on the second head (**inferred**; the control word's bit meanings are unknown in `docs/display.md`). (4) Flag 4 with bit 0 set. The stored word then gets bit 0 cleared and bit 1 set, so bit 0 is the transient switch-on request and bit 1 the module's record that the cursor is shown, as in `odsyCursorOn`. (5) Flag 3 with word 1 holding the hotspot (x in the high half, y in the low half) and word 2 the pointer position from `coreX` and `coreY` in the same packing.
- `odsyDisplayCursor` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x168afc0, 2368 bytes): **established** for the control flow and request sequence, **inferred** for the pixel meaning of the glyph and alpha bits.
  Makes a cursor the current hardware cursor of a screen (arguments: screen, cursor; result 1). The cursor's per-screen record is the word at index screen-number + 5 of the cursor structure (+0x14 onward) and holds a realized flag (halfword +0xc), a pointer at +4 to a record that carries a second colour and a 768-byte buffer pointer at +8. If the cursor is the one the board record already shows (the word at +0x48), only the control sequence is repeated: flag-4 requests clearing bit 1, then setting bit 2 and, under the same dual-head test as `odsyInstallCursor`, bit 5 from the pointer position, then setting bit 0, and a flag-3 position request with hotspot 0 and the pointer position (the sends at 0x168b0e0, 0x168b144, 0x168b194 and 0x168b214; the failure messages name ODSY_SET_CONTROL and ODSY_SET_POS). Otherwise a cursor with no record for the screen returns 1 at once; else bit 2 of the control word is cleared, and if the realized flag is 0 it is set and the buffer is filled from the cursor's source and mask bitmaps (clamped to 32 by 32). Each row takes a 32-bit source word and mask word masked by a left-aligned width mask and writes 16 bytes of glyph (4 bits a pixel) and 8 bytes of alpha (2 bits a pixel, value 3 where the mask is set); the glyph value is 1 and the pixel is expanded through the 16-word table at rodata offset 0xb70, which maps each four-bit group of the source-and-mask word to four nibbles of value 0 or 1 in mirrored order. When the record at +4 is absent or its byte +6 is zero this table path is used; otherwise the loop at 0x168b304 sets glyph value 1 for the first 16 pixels of the row wherever the mask is set and, for the last 16, only where source and mask are both set, with alpha 3 for every mask pixel; why the halves differ is **unknown**. The installed function is then called through the pointer at +0x8c of the structure behind screen +0x74 with the cursor record and the cursor's hotspot halfwords (+0xc and +0xe of the cursor bits), and `odsySetCursorColor..CB` reloads the colour table. Nothing in this routine stores the +0x48 word.
- `odsySetCursorColor..CB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15e5700, 880 bytes): **established.**
  Builds and sends the cursor's 15-word colour table (arguments: the cursor, the screen; no result). Word 0 comes from the cursor's background colour (halfwords at +0xa, +0xc, +0xe), word 1 from its foreground colour (+4, +6, +8) and word 2 from the second colour that the cursor's per-screen record points to (three halfwords at +0, +2, +4; with no second colour all three components use table index 0). The other 12 words are zero. Each 16-bit X component is shifted right by 4 and compressed to a table index (itself below 0x200, half plus 0x100 below 0x400, a quarter plus 0x200 above), then looked up at index times 2 in the 16-bit table at the end of the video-control record's pointer chain (`XSGIvcScreens[screen]`, +0x24, then +0xc), the one `odsyLoadCmap` uses for red; all three components use this table, not the three per-channel tables of `odsyLoadCmap`. The word is (red entry masked to 0xffc0, shifted left 14) or (green entry masked to 0xffc0, shifted left 4) or (blue entry shifted right 6): red in bits 29:20, green 19:10, blue 9:0. The 60-byte table goes to the kernel as flag 8 with its address at block +0x10 through `ioctl(descriptor, 0x6f64001c, block)`; a failure prints "odsySetCursorColor: ODSY_SET_CMAP ioctl failed, errno = %d". That table word n is the colour of glyph pixel value n (15 entries for values 0 to 14, with 15 having no entry) is **inferred**: it fits the glyph values written by `odsyDisplayCursor` (0 for the background pixels under alpha 3, 1 for the foreground) and the PROM note in `docs/display.md` that value 15 has no entry. That note's reading of the 15 words as the colours of values 1 to 15 is not consistent with this routine.

## Pixel-buffer and hyperpipe routines

- `odsyAllocatePbuffer` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x166d8f0, 1388 bytes): **established.**
  Allocates the board memory behind a GLX pixel buffer and attaches a drawable to it (argument: the pixel-buffer record; result 1, or 0 after any failure, which also releases the group). The record supplies the screen at +0x10, the buffer id at +4, width at +0xc and height at +0xe. It builds a 72-byte memory-allocation request (layout in `docs/opengl.md`): -1 at +0 and +4 (no group yet), object type 0, and a description with byte 0 = 3, byte 1 = 2 shifted left by a size code, sizes at description +16 and +20 equal to the width and height and +24 equal to 1. The size code comes from a table in the board-private record: the entry at +0x50 selected by the GLX window private's word at +0x48 minus the screen record's word at +0x24 when the buffer has no window with a visual, otherwise the 0x44-byte entry at +0x4c selected by the visual (word +0xc of the entry). Byte 15 is 1 when the GLX private's word at +0x54 is 0x18. The request goes out as `ioctl(descriptor, 0x6f640001, block)` at 0x166db30 (kernel `gfP_OdsyMMalloc`); a failure prints "Unable to allocate pbuffer depth %d width %d height %d" (the depth printed is always 16), and a returned group of -1 prints "ODSY_MM_ALLOC didn't fail but we got back an invalid handle". When the reply flag word (+52) has bit 1 set, its value at +56 is stored in the 0x2e80-byte region that `odsyKernInit` made, in the table at region +0x2c00 at the index in the reply halfword at +64. If the GLX private's byte at +0x50 is nonzero and byte 15 is 1, the request is repeated for object types 3 and 4 with byte 1 set to 8, which adds two further objects to the same group (the loop at 0x166daf0; a failure releases the group with `ioctl 0x6f640002`). It then queries slots 0 to 6 of the group (`ioctl 0x6f640004`, "Failed ODSY_MM_QUERY (group) ioctl") and, when reply flag bit 4 is set, stores the reply's value in the region table at +0x2b80 at the index in the reply halfword at +0x20. The reply halfwords at +0x10 and +0x12 locate a 16-byte record in that table (chunk number the upper 6 bits, record the lower 10, 0x166dcc4 to 0x166dcf0): its halfword at +4 is set to the entry of the table at region +0x2b7c plus 1 and its halfword at +6 to 1, and after a `sync` (0x166dd14) the group's slots 0 to 6 are made resident (`ioctl 0x6f640003`, "Failed MakeResident"). The last request, `ioctl 0x6f64002a` with the buffer id and the group, associates the buffer with a drawable ("ODSY_PBUF_ASSOC failed: '%s'" with `strerror`). The kernel entry points and their numbers are in `docs/ioctls.md`.
- `odsyQueryHyperpipeConfig` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x165f7f0, 444 bytes): **established.**
  Fetches the current display-pipe configuration of a screen from the kernel and caches each pipe's name (arguments: screen number, configuration id, a pointer for the pipe count; result a pointer to the module's table of pipe records, or 0 with the count 0). If the compositors have not been opened (`odsyCompositorFDsOpen` zero) and `odsyQueryCompositors..DC` finds some, `odsyProgramCompositors..GC` runs first (0x165f964 to 0x165f980). It sends the 524-byte configuration block with the id at +8 as `ioctl(descriptor, 0x6f640011, block)` (kernel `gfP_OdsyHPgetConfig`), the descriptor being the word at +100 of the structure behind the screen's record in `screenInfo`. A nonzero result stores 0 in the count. On success the count (the first word of the reply) is stored through the third argument, then for every pipe (16-byte entries from block +0xc) the entry's words at +0x10, +0x0c and +0x14 are copied into the module's pipe table (stride 0x5c) and the board-information request `ioctl(descriptor, 0x66, {entry word at +0x18, buffer, 0x28})` fills a 40-byte buffer whose name is copied with `strcpy` into the 0x54-byte name records (the loop at 0x165f8e0 to 0x165f954); a result of -1 calls `perror`, stores 0 and returns 0.
- `odsyQueryHyperpipeNetwork` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1665e00, 712 bytes): **established.**
  Reads the hyperpipe topology and refreshes the module's pipe name table and per-pipe board records (arguments: screen number, a pointer for the pipe count that may be null; result a pointer to the table of name records, 0 on failure). The compositor start-up check is the same as in `odsyQueryHyperpipeConfig`. It sends `ioctl(descriptor, 0x6f640010, block)` (kernel `gfP_OdsyHPgetTopology`, 388-byte result; a failure calls `perror` and stores 0 in the count) and then, for each pipe of the reply (12-byte entries), asks the board-information request 0x66 for the name (40-byte buffer, copied into the 0x54-byte name record) and stores two words of the entry into the module's per-pipe tables. When the caller passes no count pointer it also asks for the full 588-byte record with request 0x66 ("Get board info (long) failed") and stores the record's word at +500 (the compositor word) into the board-information record that `ddxGetBoardInfo` returns for that pipe. After the loop it calls `odsySetupCompositorInfo` and sets the module's network-read flag (the word at 0x173a9f8 is raised to 1 if it was 0, the store at 0x166604c).
- `odsyHyperpipeConfig` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1671c00, 616 bytes): **established.**
  Programs a display-pipe configuration (arguments: screen, configuration id, a pointer that receives the new configuration id, a pointer to the pipe count and the array of pipe name records to use; result 0 after the request, or 0 early for an unknown pipe). It stores -1 in the output word first and starts the compositors as in `odsyQueryHyperpipeConfig` when they are not open. It builds the 524-byte block with the count at +0, the id at +8 and for each requested pipe a 16-byte entry from +0xc holding three words copied from the record's +0x54, +0x58 and +0x50. If the network has not been read (the word at 0x173a9f8 is 0) `odsyQueryHyperpipeNetwork` runs. The record's name is matched with `strcmp` against the module's name table (0x54-byte records; an unmatched name returns 0), and the pipe's identity word from the module's table at 0x1739df8 (indexed by the matched record's position) is stored in the entry at +0x18 and sent with the board-information request 0x66, whose reply buffer is not read (a result of -1 calls `perror` and returns 0). The block then goes to the kernel as `ioctl(descriptor, 0x6f640012, block)` (kernel `gfP_OdsyHPconfig`, which also reprograms the compositor tiling); on success the reply word at +8 is stored in the output, otherwise -1 is stored.

## Platform parameter routines

- `odsySetPltParam` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1685800, 1204 bytes): **established.**
  Applies one platform parameter that a client (`setmon`, the compositor tools) sends to the X module (arguments: screen, unused, parameter id, data size, data pointer; result 0, or 2 for a bad id or size). The id indexes a 0x5d-entry jump table at 0x172a6e0 (an id of 0x5d or more, or any id whose entry is the default, prints "odsySetPltParam: Unknown paramId = 0x%x" and returns 2); the entries I decoded are these, and every other id below 0x5d takes the default. Ids 0 and 1 need a size of 0x20 and call `odsySetFBConfig..KD` with the data. Id 2 needs size 4 and sends the video timing generator control request `ioctl(descriptor, 0x6f640020, {2, value})` ("odsyEnableVTG: ODSY_BRDMGR_VTG_CONTROL Failed"). Id 3 needs at least 4 bytes: it stores the first word at +0x88 of the board-private record and, when that word is nonzero, copies the string that follows into the record at +0x8c and calls `odsyReadPFDArgs..LD` on it. Id 6 needs size 0xc and sends the data through `odsyCmpIoctl` as a compositor register write ("odsy compositor write: ioctl failed."), and id 7 needs size 0xc and only stores its first byte, second byte and halfword at +8 into the record's +0x80, +0x81 and +0x82 (the compositor read request that `odsyQryPltParam` completes). Id 8 needs size 0x30 and passes the data unchanged as a two-wire command to `ioctl 0x6f640021` (kernel `gfP_OdsyBRDMGRi2ccmd`); a negative result prints the command and returns 2, and a nonzero status word at +0xc in the reply prints "status = %d" and returns 2. Ids 9 and 0x5b build a 48-byte two-wire command in the stack from the data and send it with `ioctl 0x6f640021`: id 9 needs size 0x38 and sends command 4 with the word at +0 and the halfword at +4 copied into the command's word at +8 and halfword at +0, the data pointer set to +9, the count taken from the byte at +0x31 and the halfword at +0x34 copied as well; id 0x5b needs size 0xa8 and sends command 0x10 with a byte count of four times the first word, the byte at +4 as the command's byte at +6 and the data pointer set to +8 ("odsy I2C sendbytes platform param: ioctl failed"). Id 0xc needs size 4 and stores its word in the module's word at 0x173aa10; id 0x5c does nothing and returns 0. The names of ids 6 to 9 and 0x5b to 0x5c follow `docs/vpro-tool-functions.md` where its callers are read; the commands' meaning on the wire is **inferred** from the compositor routines in this file.
- `odsyQryPltParam` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15f3e10, 888 bytes): **established.**
  Answers one platform parameter query (arguments: screen, unused, parameter id, data size, data pointer; result 0, or 2 for a failed request, a bad size or an unknown id). For ids 0 and 1 it needs a size of 0x20 and fills the block with the frame-buffer depth from the module's table `bufferDepths` indexed by the board-private record's word at +0x10, the record's word at +0x14, and six floats made from the unsigned words at record +0x20 to +0x34, each multiplied by 2^-20 (the constant at rodata 0x172e164 is 0x35800000); I read the six as 12.20 fixed-point per-screen values (**inferred**; this routine does not name them). Id 7 needs size 0xc: it copies the record's three values at +0x80, +0x81 and +0x82 into the block and runs `odsyCmpIoctl` on it, the read half of the compositor register access ("odsy compositor read: ioctl failed."). Id 10 needs size 0x38 and sends command 3 as a two-wire read (`ioctl 0x6f640021`, "odsy I2C recvbytes platform param: ioctl failed"). Id 0x1f needs size 0xa4 and sends command 0xf with a one-word reply buffer at +4; the reply word is stored at +0 of the data, or 0 on a failure ("odsy I2C vbob platform param: ioctl failed"). Any other id prints "odsyQueryPlatformParam: Unknown paramId = 0x%x" and returns 2; a wrong size prints "odsyQryPltParam: Illegal dataSize = 0x%x".

## Image transfer routines

All five routines below belong to the X module's `PutImage` and `GetImage` paths. The rectangle argument is four 16-bit values (x0, y0, x1, y1), the user buffer is a host address and the stride is in bytes (0 means the row length). Every routine starts by clearing the word at +0x84 of the board's per-board state region (the 0x2e80-byte block that `odsyKernInit` makes) and reads the window's format entry from the 0x44-byte table that the board-private record's word at +0x4c points to, indexed by the window's frame-buffer-layer private word at +0x3c minus the screen record's word at +0x50.

- `odsyReadImage` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1571cb0, 1120 bytes): **established.**
  Reads a rectangle of the window into host memory (arguments: rectangle, host buffer, stride, window; no result). It first writes the FIFO settle sequence that `docs/opengl.md` gives (0x10443 with operand 0xfa, two 0x10046, 0x10019, then 0x10443 with 0x96 and four 0x10046, then 0x10443 with 0xfa, two 0x10046) and the words 0x10658 and 0x120000, clears the halfword at +0x68 of the board-private record, and builds one 80-byte transfer request in the stack for `ioctl(descriptor, 0x6f640006, block)` (kernel `gfP_OdsyMMxfr`, request layout in `docs/kernel.md`, the call at 0x15720b8; failure prints "odsyReadImage: ODSY_MM_XFR pixel transfer failed: '%s'" with `strerror`). The request has -1 in word 0, the group id -2 (the board's own table) at +4 and the entry index 1 when the window's class (frame-buffer-layer private +0x14) is 7 and 0 otherwise, the host address at +16, the length (row bytes plus stride times height minus 1) at +24, a first header word from the board record's word at +4 (+8 for class 7) masked to 29 bits, the rectangle origin at +32, its width and height at +40, the row length and stride difference at +48 and +52, a format word at +44 built from the format entry's words at +0x30, +0x38 and +0x3c (or +0x40 when the window's double-buffer private byte is 1), `0x80000000` and 0x1000, and a word at +56 from the entry's +0x2c and +0x34 with top byte 0x33. The flag word at +60 is 2: a read (bit 0 clear) that makes the kernel wait for the transfer before it returns. The header word 7's top byte is rewritten by the kernel, so the 0x33 is not what the board sees. Where the entry's fields name hardware pixel-mode bits is **inferred** from `docs/kernel.md`, the shapes only: that table gives the header layout, this routine's masks fill it.
- `odsyReadDeepImage..GE` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16bf3a0, 1152 bytes): **established.**
  The read for windows that use the deep formats (arguments as `odsyReadImage`). The settle sequence, the 0x10658 and 0x120000 words and the 80-byte `ioctl 0x6f640006` request are the same, with these differences read from the two listings: the entry index is always 0 (the group id is -2 as before); the format word at +44 takes the pixel-mode fields from the entry's +0x38 and +0x3c (or +0x40) only and carries a fixed 0x1033 where `odsyReadImage` derives low bits from +0x30; the word at +56 has top byte 0x03; and when the window record's word at +0x78 is 0 it calls `FindWindowWithOptional` and ignores the result. The transfer call is at 0x16bf780 and the failure message reads "odsyReadDeepImage: ODSY_MM_XFR pixel transfer failed: '%s'".
- `odsyDrawImage` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15aece0, 400 bytes): **established.**
  Entry for putting an image into a window (arguments: rectangle, buffer, stride, raster-op, plane mask, window). A window whose double-buffer private has byte +1 equal to -1 (drawing to both buffers) and whose format entry word at +0xc is 3 is drawn twice: byte +1 is set to 0 and `odsyDrawImageSingleBuffer..J` runs, then byte +1 is set to 1 and it runs again, and the byte is restored to -1. For every other window the single-buffer routine runs once. The function does nothing else.
- `odsyDrawImageSingleBuffer..J` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15ee5d0, 312 bytes): **established.**
  Chooses the transfer method for one buffer by the byte count (arguments as `odsyDrawImage`). A rectangle with zero width or height returns at once. The count is the width shifted left by the format entry's word at +0x1c (the log2 of bytes a pixel) times the height. Above `env_dma_threshold` it calls `odsyDrawImageDMA..H`; above `env_pio_threshold` it calls `odsyDrawImagePIO..G`; at or below it, a window of class 0 or 7 (frame-buffer-layer private +0x14) goes to `odsyDrawSmallCI8Image..I` and every other class to `odsyDrawImagePIO..G` (the compares at 0x15ee684 and 0x15ee68c). The two thresholds are words at 0x17345e0 and 0x17345e4 whose initial values in the module's data are 9216 and 64 (bytes); `odsyInit` replaces them with `atoi` of the environment variables `ODSY_DMA_THRESHOLD` and `ODSY_PIO_THRESHOLD` when they are set and nonempty.
- `odsyDrawImageDMA..H` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16a84e0, 8412 bytes): **established** for the control flow, the table of window classes, the state words and the transfer request (the whole listing, 0x16a84e0 to 0x16aa5b4, was read next to the decompilation; the ten repeated class templates were read once each and compared). What each state word selects on the board is **unknown**.
  Draws an image into a window by host-to-board DMA (arguments: rectangle of four signed halfwords x1, y1, x2, y2; host buffer; row stride, 0 meaning the packed row length; raster-op; plane mask; window; no result). The window's frame-buffer-layer private gives the window class (+0x14, the value the switch below uses) and its depth byte (window +2) gives the mask (1 shifted left by the depth, minus 1). A byte in the screen-private array of the window's table (index through the module's `-0x6eb8` slot) selects the buffer: -2 or 0 gives buffer 0 with draw-buffer value 3, -3 or 1 gives buffer 1 with value 0xc, -1 gives buffer 1 with value 0xf when the entry's word at +0xc is 2 and buffer 0 with value 3 otherwise, and any other byte gives buffer 0 with value 3 (0x16a8608 to 0x16a8638, 0x16a968c); the buffer index picks one of the entry's words at +0x3c and +0x40 for the transfer. Class 7 changes which board-record word supplies the first header word (+8 instead of +4).
  The direct path is taken only when stencil testing is off (`enableStencilTest`, the module word at gp-0x1a74), the raster-op is 3 (copy), the plane mask covers every bit of the depth mask, the class is neither 6 nor 7, the draw-buffer value is not 0xf, and the window's entry in the screen's table at +0x58 (stride 0x14) is not 0xe to 0x13 (0x16a9518 to 0x16a9570). It sends the settle sequence, the word 0x10019, the long settle form (operand 150 and four 0x10046), and the words 0x10658 and 0x120000, sets the halfword +0x68 of the board-private record to 0 and goes straight to the transfer with flag 3 and top byte 0x32 in the +56 word. Otherwise the routine first refreshes the clip: when the cached bounds in the board-private record (halfwords +0x6c, +0x70, +0x6e, +0x72) differ from the current clip box (+0x60, +0x64, +0x62, +0x66) it copies them over and recomputes the eight words at region +0x2b40 to +0x2b5c (the box origin, x above y in 12-bit fields; the extent minus one in the same layout; then three 0 and 0xffffff pairs), sending them behind the settle sequence and header 0x1204b.
