  Wraps window copying. It temporarily replaces the screen's copy-window slot (+0xe0) with the resource manager's saved original (+0x78 of its screen record), calls that original for the window with the region argument, and restores the slot's previous contents.
- `rrmCreateWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15c6470, 120 bytes): **established.**
  Wraps window creation: it clears the window's resource-manager private slot, installs the saved original create procedure (+0x70 of the resource manager's screen record) in the screen's slot at +0xb4, calls it, and reinstalls itself.
- `rrmWakeupHandler..PE` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15ba4a0, 260 bytes): **established.**
  Runs the resource manager's work from the wakeup path, at most every 50 ms (it returns at once when the milliseconds since the time kept at 0x173f370 are below 50, the `sltiu` at 0x15ba4d4): it calls entry +0x18 of each screen's record and ORs the results; when the OR is nonzero it stores the current time at 0x173f370 (0x15ba550), and when it is zero (every screen answered done) it unregisters `rrmBlockHandler..OE` and itself with `RemoveBlockAndWakeupHandlers` and clears the flag at 0x173f378.
- `rrmMapWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x156eaa0, 480 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the no-record path, the ancestor walk, the identifier choice and the request-byte actions were traced).
  Wraps window mapping (argument: window; result what the saved original map procedure returns). A window without a record, or one whose record is not registered with the kernel (flag bit 0 clear), skips the identifier work: when it has no parent (the root) it first calls `rrmInitRootWindow..AG` (screen, window) to publish the board number, and in either case it still maps the window through the saved original procedure. Otherwise it calls `incrRRMpriv` for the window's parent, then walks up the ancestors adding 1 to each one's record count at +0x10, calls the screen record's procedure at +0x20 with (window, 0, 0), and reads the request byte at +5 of the window's record (before that call). It looks for an identifier with `FindFreeID` and, when none is free, uses `StealID` if the request byte is nonzero and `FindBestID` otherwise. It then installs the saved original map procedure (screen record +0x68) at screen +0xc4, calls it, reinstalls itself, and acts on the request byte: bit 1 calls `FindFreeCID` and `rrmValidateCID` when the screen record's word +4 is nonzero and then calls procedure +0x1c with the window; bit 3 calls procedure +0x24 with (window, record, screen record).
- `rrmUnmapWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1586c80, 808 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: both unlink blocks were followed store by store, as were the ancestor loop and the saved-procedure swap).
  Wraps window unmapping. For a registered window it runs the record procedure +0x20, then releases the window's clip identifier by unlinking it from the identifier entry's window list (updating the entry's use count and the flag bits of +0xa8, or, when the window's identifier equals the screen record's word 0 (the identifier count, the value one past the last real entry), only setting the identifier to -1 and skipping the unlink), does the same for its clip-region slot (a 0x1c-byte entry of the table at +0x94 selected by the word at +0x18 of the record, with its own list links at +0x3c and +0x40), decrements the mapped-child counts of all ancestors, and finally calls the saved original unmap procedure (+0x6c) through the screen's slot at +0xc8 before reinstalling itself.
- `rrmProcessRequestQueue` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15e7d60, 512 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the unlink, both window states and the return test were traced).
  Takes the first window off the screen's pending list (head at +0x8c, tail at +0x90, record links +0x34 and +0x38; it clears the queued flag 0x20 and the links) and performs what was requested for it. A viewable window (bit 11 of +0x7c) gets an identifier from `FindFreeID` or, when none is free, `StealID`; request bit 3 then calls the screen procedure at +0x24, and request bit 1 finds and validates a clip identifier (`FindFreeCID`, `rrmValidateCID`, when the screen's word +4 is set) and calls procedure +0x1c. A window that is not viewable but has the record's flag bit 2 set only runs the two procedures. It returns true when the list is still nonempty afterwards.
- `rrmBlockHandler..OE` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16e43a0, 664 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the two timeout branches and the division by 1000 as a multiplication by 0x83126e98 were traced).
  Keeps the server's select timeout short while the resource manager still has work. It reads `GetTimeInMillis`; when under 50 ms (0x32) have passed since 0x173f370 it lowers the timeout (seconds and microseconds, read through the pointer in the second argument) to the time that is left, or installs a fresh timeout record at +0x140 of the data block when none exists; otherwise it runs entry +0x18 of every screen's record, stores the time, and if any screen still has work it limits the timeout to 50 ms.

## Resource-manager window binding, validation and default colour-map routines

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, read from a Ghidra listing and decompilation with `$gp` resolved and the GOT slots and small-data strings named. The window record (slot `rrmWindowPrivateIndex` of the window's private array at window +0x84, 0x4c bytes) has these fields in the routines below: flag halfword +0 (bit 0 bound to a render node, bit 2 bound to an OpenGL render node, bit 3 on an identifier list, bit 5 on the screen's pending list, bit 6 on a clip list), window class +2, request byte +4, halfword +8 (1 for an OpenGL binding, 0 for an IRIS GL one), render-node id +0xc, count of mapped descendants +0x10, identifier +0x14 (-1 when none), clip number +0x18, two mode words +0x20 and +0x24, a child array pointer +0x28, identifier-list links +0x2c and +0x30, pending-list links +0x34 and +0x38 and clip-list links +0x3c and +0x40. The screen record (slot `rrmScreenPrivateIndex`) has the default identifier at +0, the default clip number at +4, flag word +0xc, a clip table at +0x94 (0x1c bytes a clip; head, tail, flags, count in the first four words, the shared region from +0x10) next to the identifier table of 0x24-byte entries at +0x98 described above, and the pending list at +0x8c and +0x90. All of these meanings are **inferred** from how the code uses the fields; the offsets and the control flow are read.

- `rrmBindRNToClip` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1721f20, 780 bytes): **inferred** (the already-bound refusal and the failure branch of the parent count were matched in the listing; the rest is from the decompilation).
  Binds a window to an IRIS-GL-style render node (arguments: window, render-node id, two mode words; result 1 on success and 0 on failure). It makes the window's 0x4c-byte record with `Xalloc` and `bzero` when none exists and calls the screen record's procedure at +0x50 for a new record (zero in class +2 when there is none); a record already bound (bit 0) returns 0 at once, which is the difference from `rrmOGLBindRNToClip`. It stores the id at +0xc and the two mode words at +0x20 and +0x24, zeroes +8 (so the binding is not OpenGL), and sets the flags to exactly 1. `incrRRMpriv` on the parent must succeed, otherwise the new record is freed, the slot cleared and 0 returned. For a class-1 window on a screen whose flag word has bit 0 set the identifier becomes -1 and, when the window is viewable (bit 11 of window +0x7c), every ancestor's descendant count is raised and the identifier is set to the screen's default identifier (word 0). For every other window it clears bit 0 of the frame-buffer private word at +0x20, calls `nfbSetDidFlags` and, when viewable, raises the ancestors' counts and takes an identifier with `FindFreeID`, falling back to `FindBestID` (never `StealID`).
- `rrmOGLBindRNToClip` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16e7360, 896 bytes): **inferred** (the test of an existing record at 0x16e73b4 to 0x16e73c0 and the GLX private test at 0x16e7440 were matched in the listing, the rest is from the decompilation).
  The OpenGL form of the bind, with the same arguments. A record already bound is not refused: when its halfword +8 is 0 (an IRIS GL window) it prints "Xsgi: attempt to bind OpenGL renderer to IRIS GL window: WID=0x%x" (rodata 0x172ea20) with the window id and returns 0, and when it is already an OpenGL binding it only replaces the id at +0xc and returns 1. A new binding sets flags 5 (bound and OpenGL) and halfword +8 to 1. If the window's GLX private (slot `glxWindowPrivateIndex`) exists with a nonzero word at +4, the window takes identifier -1 and the routine returns 1 without counting ancestors or taking an identifier, so a window the GLX layer manages never gets one here. Otherwise it follows the IRIS GL path, with a failed `incrRRMpriv` freeing the record.
- `rrmUnbindRNFromClip` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x158ed30, 1488 bytes): **inferred** (from the decompilation only; four child slots are cleared by one recursive call each).
  Undoes a bind (arguments: window, a value passed on to the recursive calls). It returns 0 for a window with no record. It calls the screen procedure at +0x20 with (window, 0, 0), takes the window off the pending list when flag bit 5 is set (list head +0x8c, tail +0x90; link words +0x34 and +0x38), and for a viewable window leaves its identifier (the default identifier just becomes -1; any other entry is unlinked with the sequence described above, with the entry's count at +0xac lowered and its flags recomputed) and its clip entry (when the clip number is not the default and flag bit 6 is set; the same unlink through links +0x3c and +0x40 on the 0x1c-byte clip entry). Descendant counts of all ancestors are lowered, `cmapDID` supplies the window's display identifier for the frame-buffer private word at +4, and the child array at +0x28, when present, is cleared slot by slot: each of the four child windows is detached and unbound with a recursive call before the array is freed. Unless the identifier is the default, bit 0 of the frame-buffer word +0x20 is set, `nfbSetDidFlags` runs and a viewable window gets the screen procedure +0x48 with its old identifier. Finally the record is freed (slot cleared) when no descendant is mapped (+0x10 is 0), otherwise only bit 0 is cleared.
- `rrmOGLUnbindRNFromClip` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x170e660, 1300 bytes): **inferred** (from the decompilation only, set beside the decompilation of the unbind routine line by line).
  The unbind for an OpenGL window, with only the window as argument. It runs the same pending-list, identifier and clip unlinking as `rrmUnbindRNFromClip` but has no child array to tear down: when the child pointer at +0x28 is nonzero it prints "rrmOGLUnbindRNFromClip: pPriv->prrmBoundWins non zero! 0x%08X" (rodata 0x172ea68) and carries on. A window whose GLX private has a nonzero word at +4 is freed and cleared right after the pending-list step and the routine returns 1 without touching identifiers or clips, mirroring the early exit in the OpenGL bind. The tail is the same: `cmapDID`, bit 0 of +0x20 and the +0x48 procedure when the identifier is not the default, then free the record when +0x10 is 0 and otherwise clear bit 0.
- `rrmDoubleBufferWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1612830, 896 bytes): **inferred** (from the decompilation only; the 38 halfword zero stores were counted there).
  Makes a window double-buffered through the resource manager (argument: window; result an X error code, 0 on success, 11 on allocation failure and 0x11 for a window already bound to an IRIS GL render node). It creates the window's record, zero-filled halfword by halfword (38 stores) instead of with `bzero`, calling the screen procedure +0x50 for a new one. An already bound OpenGL record (halfword +8 nonzero) skips the set-up and goes straight to the final call. Otherwise it marks the record OpenGL (halfword +8 = 1, flags 5), takes the render-node id from word +0x68 of the screen's device record (window +0x10, +0x74), calls `incrRRMpriv` (on failure it prints "rrmDoubleBufferWindow: incrRRMpriv failed" from rodata 0x172f0f8, frees the record and returns 11), raises ancestors' counts when viewable, and takes the two mode words from the window's current identifier entry (+0xa0 with bit 2 cleared and bit 1 set, and +0xa4, or the entry's alternate word +0xb4 when the screen word +0x30 and the entry word +0xb8 are both nonzero). It clears bit 0 of the frame-buffer word +0x20, calls `nfbSetDidFlags`, marks the identifier -1 and, for a viewable window, takes an identifier with `FindFreeID` (falling back to `StealID`) and calls the screen procedure +0x28. Every successful path ends in the screen's procedure at +0xc0 with (window, 0).
- `rrmValidateCID` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15e3ac0, 1124 bytes): **inferred** (from the decompilation only; the screen slots +0x5c, +0x158 and +0x4c were read there).
  Gives a window the clip entry its current state needs. It returns at once when the window already uses the default clip number (screen +4). It asks the screen predicate at +0x5c whether the window needs a private clip; when it does not, the window leaves its clip list (flag bit 6, the +0x3c and +0x40 links and the entry's count and flags as in `rrmUnbindRNFromClip`) and takes the default clip number. When it does and screen flag bit 1 is set, it first walks the other clip entries from 1 up to the default number: for a head window whose class (+2) differs from this window's and whose request byte (+4) has bit 1 set, it calls the screen procedure +0x20 with (window, 2, 0), takes that window off its clip entry and relinks it at the tail of entry 0 (count +1, flags |2, |6 once the count passes one). Then it copies the window's region (window +0x2c) into the shared region of its own clip entry (entry base + 0x10) through the screen's region-copy slot at +0x158 and tail-calls the screen procedure +0x4c with (window, region, clip number).
- `rrmInvalidate` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x168b900, 904 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the request words, the display-mode copy and the clip step were traced).
  Sends the kernel a list of pending changes for a window's render node (arguments: window, a mask of change bits, a flag word; the mask is ANDed with the record's request byte +4, and a zero mask means all pending bits). It clears the sent bits from +4, fills a five-word request (render-node id +0xc, window id, the mask, two result words) and calls `ioctl` on the descriptor at +100 of the device record with 0x3f2 (1010, `RRM_InvalidateRN`); the five words are, in memory order, the render-node id (+0xc), the window id, the mask, and two result words that are zero on the way in. When mask bit 3 (display mode) is among the bits and the call did not fail, the fourth word's bit 2 (value 4) is merged into record flag +0x20 bit 2, and the mode words +0x20 and +0x24 are copied into the window's identifier entry (+0xa0, +0xa4) when its identifier is not -1: as they are when the fifth word's bit 0 is clear, with bit 2 of the first flipped when it is set and, in that case with a nonzero flag argument, `SetDIDMode` called for the window. If bit 2 of +0x20 changed, the routine stores it in the window's double-buffer private at +2, assigns a fresh serial number from `globalSerialNumber` (wrapping to 1 above 0x10000000) into window +0x14 and calls `FlushClientCaches` with the window's id. An `ioctl` result of -1 skips the display-mode work but still runs the clip step. When mask bit 1 (clip) is set, the screen has a default clip and the window has no GLX private word at +4, a window whose clip number (+0x18) is 0 or equals the screen record's word +4 only gets +0x18 cleared; any other gets the screen procedure +0x4c with (window, clip entry, 0) followed by the screen's slot +0x188 on the entry's region.
- `rrmInvalidateWindowTree..PF` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15c0f90, 1532 bytes): **inferred** (the compiler unrolled the loop that ORs per-bit words five times, and that part of the decompilation was checked only at its branches).
  Walks a window and all its mapped descendants and asks the screen procedure +0x20 for (window, 0, 1) on every one the screen's change set touches. The set comes from the screen device record (window +0x10, +0x74): a mask at +0x48, a count at +0x28 and a word array at +0x34, read once. With a second array at +0x38 present the routine ORs the array words picked by the set bits of the mask into one bitmap and invalidates a window when bit (frame-buffer word +0x18 mod 32) is set in it; without +0x38 it takes the lowest set bit of the mask as a threshold and invalidates every window whose +0x18 is at least that. Only windows with bit 10 of +0x7c set, a record with bit 0, and a nonzero +0x44 are tested, and it goes into a window's children only when the record's +0x10 descendant count is nonzero, then moves to the next sibling (+0x1c) or climbs through the parent (+0x18) until it is back at the start.
- `rrmCreateDefColormap` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16660d0, 752 bytes): **inferred** (the colour tables are identified by their rodata base addresses).
  Creates a screen's default colour map and fills in its white and black pixels (argument: the screen). It finds the visual record whose id equals the screen's root visual (the 0x24-byte array at screen +0x7c, unrolled in the decompilation to a five-way compare), makes the map with `CreateColormap` (allocation mode taken from bit 0 of the visual's flag halfword) and returns 0 on failure. For a class-3 visual with more than 4 planes, when the board record's word at +0x4c equals the word the board's colour-map table gives for the root visual, it sets `sgiOverrideColorCompare` and allocates every colour in the ranges listed from `odsyScrnPriv` + 0x46e4 (start and end pairs ended by a negative start, the first start being `initGL`) from three 256-entry byte tables in rodata (red at 0x40e0, green at 0x41e0, blue at 0x42e0 from the table base, each byte shifted into the top of a 16-bit value), keeping the pixel of table entry 0 in screen +0x2c and that of entry 7 in +0x28; it returns 0 when any allocation fails. Any other visual gets white (0xffff three times) in +0x28 and black (0) in +0x2c through `AllocColor`. It ends by calling the screen's install-colour-map procedure at +0x13c.

## Display-mode, colour-map, cross-hair and audio-signal routines

- `odsyValidateMode` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x163ffd0, 804 bytes): **inferred** (from the decompilation only; the two message strings were read from the module's rodata).
  Builds a window's two display-mode words and loads them into the board's mode table (arguments: window, the window's resource-manager record, the resource-manager screen record). It returns at once for a window whose identifier (record +0x14) is negative. It finds the window's visual (through window +0x78, falling back to `FindWindowWithOptional`) and its layer number from the device record's visual table (0x14 bytes a visual, word +4), looks up the window's colour map with `LookupIDByType` (type 6; none for an input-only window), and takes the colour map's table number from the map's first word at +0x44 (the visual's own table when it is negative or the map is missing); the table's field at +0x18 shifted right by 8 goes into the second mode word. For identifiers below 32 that value is shifted left by 4 into bits 4 to 8 of the word (other bits kept from +0x24, with the reserved-entry bits 0 to 3 and 25 to 31 taken from the identifier entry's word +0xa4 when its flag bit 3 is set); from 32 up it is shifted left by 3 into bits 3 to 7, bits 0 and 1 coming from the entry's word when it is reserved. Bit 30 follows the first mode word (cleared when its bit 1 is set, then set again when its bit 2 is set), and an identifier from 32 up sets bit 1 unless the layer number is 10. It then sends five words in this memory order, render-node id (record +0xc), window id, identifier, the final second mode word and the first mode word, to the kernel with `ioctl` 0x3f9 (1017, `RRM_LoadDisplayMode`) on the descriptor at +100 of the device record; a failure prints "odsyValidateMode: Can't load display mode" and "rnid=0x%x, clipid=0x%x, errno=%d" (rodata 0x172bc88, 0x172bcb8; the second number printed is the window id). Whatever the outcome it records the identifier and both words in the board record's 12-byte-per-identifier table at +0x58.
- `odsyInitRootWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x157d0c0, 392 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the atom, the record set-up, the resource and property calls and both exit paths were traced).
  Creates the screen's cross-hair cursor resource when the root window is set up (argument: the screen). It first calls `odsyCheckCorona`, interns the atom `_SGI_CROSSHAIR_CURSOR` (21 characters, rodata 0x172e900) and allocates a zero-filled 0xa8-byte cursor record (failure prints "Couldn't malloc cross-hair cursor property!" and exits with 1). The record's first word points at its own bits at +0x54, the reference count at +0x10 is 1 and the halfwords at +4 and +10 are 0xffff. It gets a fake client id, registers the record as a cursor resource (type 5) with `AddResource`, stores the record at +0x48 of the screen's board record and attaches the id as a 32-bit property (type 8, `XA_CURSOR`) of the root window with `ChangeWindowProperty`; a failed property change prints "Couldn't create cross-hair cursor property!" and exits with 1, and a failed `AddResource` only frees the record.
- `odsyBell` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16bfde0, 1072 bytes): **inferred** (from the decompilation; the two size limits were read in the listing).
  Plays the keyboard bell (arguments: volume 0 to 100, pitch in Hz, duration in ms; any zero argument returns). It keeps the last bell's time (module variable +0x12c) and the length of the sound (+0x130): the time moves on only when the previous sound has finished. It asks `odsyGetSampleRate..AE` for the audio rate (+0x128) and rebuilds the sample buffer only when the three arguments differ from the cached ones (+0x11c, +0x120, +0x124) or the buffer was never built. The wave is a square wave of 8-bit samples: half a period is rate / (2 x pitch) samples, the amplitude is (volume / 16) + 1 with the second half negated, and the number of periods is pitch x duration / 1000 (the multiply by 0x83126e98, 2,199,023,256, and shift by 41 divides by 1000), cut short when the sample total would pass 0x2000 (8192; the test is `slti` 0x2001 at 0x16bff48 and 0x16c0204); the sample count is kept at 0x173f324, and the length kept at +0x130 is periods x 1300 (0x514) / pitch. To play it (when the previous sound is over and the rate is known) it makes an audio configuration of one channel and one byte per sample (`ALnewconfig`, `ALsetwidth`, `ALsetchannels`), opens the port named "xbell" in mode "w", writes the samples with `ALwritesamps`, polls `ALgetfilled` with `sginap(1)` until it drains and closes the port; a failed open prints "odsyBell: open audio port failed". Its buffer starts at 0x173d220.
- `odsyKeyClick` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16e4030, 872 bytes): **inferred** (from the decompilation; the three limits were read in the listing).
  Plays the key-click sound (argument: volume; zero returns). It is the bell's synthesis with a fixed short shape: it refreshes its time stamp (+0x138, rate at +0x13c, last volume at +0x134) when more than 30 ms (0x1e; `slti` 0x1f at 0x16e4080) have passed, builds a square wave of half-period rate / 3000 samples (the multiply by 0xaec33e20, 2,932,031,008, and shift by 43) and the same amplitude rule, repeats at most 15 (0xf) periods and stops growing at 256 (0x100; `slti` 0x101 at 0x16e4144) samples (count at 0x173f328, buffer at 0x173f220, separate from the bell's), and plays it through the same open, write, drain and close sequence on the port "xbell" (failure message "odsyKeyClick: open audio port failed"). It rebuilds the samples only when the volume or the rate changed.
- `odsyCreateColormap` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x163f560, 1952 bytes): **inferred** (from the decompilation only; the rodata colour tables are identified by base address).
  Fills a new colour map's initial entries (argument: the map; result 1 on success, 0 when `nfbCreateHWColormap` fails). The entries are 20 bytes each with three 16-bit components, in one array for gray and static colour (+0x38) or in three arrays for true colour (+0x38, +0x3c, +0x40), and the visual's class decides the contents; the class is the halfword at +4 of the visual. The even classes go through the static fills: class 0 (static gray) writes a gray ramp, entry k = (k scaled to the visual's bits per colour) x 0xffff / (2^bits - 1) in all three components; class 2 (static colour) on a 256-entry visual, when the byte at +0x11 of the object at +0x44 of the active-screen record is a space, copies the 256-byte red, green and blue tables of the module's rodata (offsets 0x40e0, 0x41e0, 0x42e0, each byte put in the high half of a 16-bit component), and otherwise decodes the index with the visual's three masks and shifts and rescales each field to 16 bits; class 4 (true colour) fills each array from a ramp over its field. The odd classes (gray scale, pseudo colour, direct colour) get no fill: the routine looks up the window layer for the visual in the device record and, for a layer of 32 or more whose board mode word lacks bit 1, reserves entry 0 when the map's flag halfword bit 1 is clear (component flags set to 0xffff, +0x10 of the entry cleared, the map's free count at +0x14 lowered) and returns 0 when bit 1 is set; every other case returns 1 untouched.

## Symbol-database open, close and read routines and the format-file readers

The symbol database is an XDR-encoded file: a header (identification string of 4 bytes, two version numbers at +4 and +8, a 100-byte name at +0xc, the symbol count at +0x70, the stream position of the header at +0x74, the position of the symbol table at +0x78, ten spare words from +0x7c; 0xa4 bytes in memory) followed by data and, at the end, a table of 0x2c-byte entries sorted by name. A write handle is 0x14 bytes (XDR stream pointer, `FILE`, header, table, a zero word); a read handle is 0xb0 bytes with the header inline from +8, the symbol count at +0x78 and the table pointer at +0xac. Memory comes from the interface table `_SDBmemface` (calloc at +4, free at +0xc). Everything below is read from the listing; the three statics named by address are `FUN_0154e9ec`, `FUN_0154efd4` and `FUN_0154f08c`.

- `SDBopenNewDatabase` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154edf8, 356 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end together with the static writer at 0x154e9ec that it calls).
  Opens a database for writing (arguments: path, two values handed to `SDBsetHeader`). The path "-" means standard output (`__iob` + 0x10, which is never closed); any other path is opened with `fopen` in mode "w", and a failure sets `errno` to 2. It callocs the 0x14-byte handle and an 0x18-byte XDR stream (`errno` 12 on failure), attaches the stream to the file with `xdrstdio_create` in encode mode and calls the static routine at 0x154e9ec (listing read), which callocs the 0xa4-byte header, copies the 4-byte identification string, sets the version halfwords to 0 and 1, calls `SDBsetHeader`, copies the name into +0xc (100 bytes), clears the ten spare words and the count, records the stream position in +0x74, zeroes +0x78 and writes the header through `xdr_bytes` and the version, count and offset encoders. If that writer fails `errno` becomes 9 and the handle is closed with `SDBcloseNewDatabase`.
- `SDBcloseNewDatabase` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154ec64, 404 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the table sort, the position bookkeeping, the table and header writes and the frees were traced).
  Finishes and frees a write handle (argument: the handle; none is accepted as a no-op). With a stream, file, header and table present it sorts the table by name with `qsort` (entry size 0x2c, `compareSTEName`), stores the current stream position in header +0x78, writes the whole table with `xdr_array` (at most 0xffff entries, `xdr_SDBsymbolTableEntry`), seeks back to the position kept in header +0x74 and rewrites the header with `xdr_SDBfileHeader`, so the count and table offset are filled in. It then frees the header, destroys the XDR stream (method +0x1c), closes the file unless it is standard output and frees the table and the handle.
- `SDBreadOpenDatabase` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154f3d0, 424 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end together with the static table reader at 0x154efd4).
  Opens a database for reading (argument: path). "-" is standard input and anything else is opened with mode "r"; failure sets `errno` 2. It callocs the 0xb0-byte handle and the XDR stream (`errno` 12), attaches it in decode mode and reads the header with `xdr_SDBfileHeader`; the static routine at 0x154efd4 then callocs count x 0x2c bytes, clears them with `SDBinitializeSymbolTableEntries`, seeks to the table offset (header +0x78) and decodes the table with `xdr_array`, failing when the number decoded differs from the header's count. The first four bytes of the header must equal `SDBidentificationString` (`strncmp`); any failure sets `errno` 20 and releases the handle with `SDBcloseReadDatabase`.
- `SDBprocessOneUserMethod` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154f5d0, 420 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end together with the version test at 0x154f578 and the symbol lookup at 0x154f08c).
  Loads one named value into caller memory, falling back to a default (arguments: read handle, a request record of 8 words: name pointer, data type, element count, destination, two halfword pairs, a default routine and its argument). It accepts the stored value only when the file's second version number (halfwords at handle +0x10) lies inside the two halfword pairs at request +0x10 and +0x14 (the static routine at 0x154f578 tests each halfword of the version separately against the matching halfword of the low pair and the high pair), the symbol exists (the static routine at 0x154f08c: `bsearch` of the name, cut to 32 bytes, in the symbol table with `compareSTEName`, then a seek of the stream to the entry's offset; a miss sets `errno` 6) and its type equals the request's. A stored count larger than the request's count sets `errno` 22 and fails; otherwise `xdr_typedData` reads the elements. If the value could not be read, or fewer elements than requested arrived, and a default routine is present, it calls that routine for each missing element (element size from `mapTypeToXDRPROC`) with the routine's argument and answers 1.
- `xdr_SDBfileHeader` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154fba8, 320 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: every field step with its offset and size was traced, including the count variable of the spare-word array).
  The XDR encoder and decoder of the header (arguments: stream, header). It codes the 4-byte identification string with `xdr_bytes` (current length from `strlen`), the two version numbers at +4 and +8 with `xdr_SDBversionNumber`, the 100-byte name at +0xc with `xdr_bytes`, the symbol count at +0x70 with `xdr_SDBelementIndex`, the two file offsets at +0x74 and +0x78 with `xdr_SDBfileOffset` and the spare words at +0x7c with `xdr_array` of `xdr_u_long` (at most 10 elements of 4 bytes; the element count lives in a local variable that is 0 on entry, so an encode writes a count of 0 and no words, and a decode accepts a count of up to 10). It answers 1 only when all steps succeed.
- `vfmtReadCmp1Format` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154ae40, 536 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the lookup, open, stat and five reader calls with their three recorded lines were traced).
  Reads a compositor video format from its database file (arguments: format name, format node). `vfmtFindCmpFormatFile` makes the path (0 means found); `SDBreadOpenDatabase` opens it; `vfmtGetLastKonaFileStat` supplies the stat record of the file just found, whose word at offset 0x4c goes to node +0x100; then five static readers fill the node from the database in turn (`FUN_015493e0` with flag 0 reads the scalar header symbols into the node, followed by the readers at 0x1549f70, 0x154a1f0, 0x154a480 and 0x154a8b0, each of which must succeed). A missing file records the source line in `DAT_01738dc0` (0x1ab for the path lookup, 0x1b1 for the open, 0x1b5 for the stat) and prints "failed to open %s" or "failed to stat %s" through the module's message routine, which prefixes "%s(%d): " with the file name "cmp1Fmt.c". The result is 0 on success and an error code otherwise; the database is closed on every path.
- `vfmtReadCmp1OdyFormat` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154b060, 308 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the lookup, open, header read and close calls and the two recorded lines were traced).
  The same read for a VPro format (arguments: name, node), but short: `vfmtFindOdyFormatFile` and `SDBreadOpenDatabase` as above, then only the scalar header reader (the static routine at 0x15493e0) with flag 1; there is no stat step and none of the four later readers. A failed lookup records line 0x1e0 and "failed to stat %s", a failed open line 0x1e6 and "failed to open %s" (the module's message routine at 0x15492b0, line number at 0x1738dc0); the result is 0 on success and 1 otherwise, and the database is closed with `SDBcloseReadDatabase` when it was opened.
- `vfmtReadCmp1SG2Format` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154b1a0, 316 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the lookup, file open, parse and close calls and the two recorded lines were traced).
  Reads an SG2 format, which is a plain file and not a symbol database: `vfmtFindSG2FormatFile` finds it, `fopen` opens it (mode string "ro", rodata 0x173f768), the parser at 0x1549c80 reads the node with flag 1, and the file is closed with `fclose`. Errors are recorded at lines 0x1fd and 0x201.
- `vfmtFindFormatFile` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154b390, 592 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the two environment-variable paths, the slash check and the result values were traced).
  Builds the path of a `.vfo` file (arguments: output buffer, name, subdirectory; result 0 found, 1 not found, 3 refused). The root is the environment variable `GL_GRROOT` (empty when unset). The name gets ".vfo" appended when it lacks it. A name without a slash becomes root + subdirectory + "/" + name; a name with a slash is accepted only when the part before its last slash equals root + subdirectory (otherwise 3) and is used as given. The path is checked with `stat`. When it is missing the routine tries `$VFOPATH/name`; with `VFOPATH` unset it returns 1, and when that is missing as well it leaves the bare name in the buffer and returns 1.
- `vfmtFindKonaCombinationFile` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154b770, 308 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: both stat tests, the root-variable fallback and the copies back to the output buffer were traced).
  Finds a combination file for the KONA family (arguments: output buffer, name; result 1 found, 0 not found, the reverse of the format finders). A name that exists as given is returned unchanged; otherwise the path is "$GL_GRROOT/usr/gfx/ucode/KONA/dg4/cmb/name" (format at rodata 0x1723930; an empty root when the variable is unset), found when `stat` accepts it, and a miss leaves the bare name and returns 0.
- `vfmtFindCmpCombinationFile` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154b8b0, 616 bytes): **established.**
  Finds a compositor combination file (arguments: output buffer, name; result 0 found, 1 not found). It tries the name as given, then three directories under `GL_GRROOT`, VOYAGER/cmb, STINGRAY/cmb and diags/compositor/data (rodata 0x1723958, 0x1723978, 0x17239a0), then repeats the whole search once with ".cmb" appended to the name when it does not already contain it. The three directory attempts pass a buffer size of 4 to `snprintf` (the listing shows `li a1,4` before each call, at 0x154b95c, 0x154b9a4 and 0x154b9ec), so only three characters of each path are written and the `stat` after it tests that stub; as written, only the name as given can be found, and the final fallback (size 0x400, the STINGRAY pattern for a name without a slash, the name copied for one with a slash) returns 1.
- `vfmtReadCmp1Combination` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154bce0, 6180 bytes): **established.**
  Reads one compositor combination database into the caller's 0x14f8-byte record (arguments: combination name, record; result 0 on success and a nonzero error otherwise). A null record records the invalid-handle error and returns 2. It finds the file with `vfmtFindCmpCombinationFile`, opens it with `SDBreadOpenDatabase`, and reads the version, format names, synchronisation and tiling scalars, the optional demultiplexer fields, and four `Pipe00` through `Pipe03` rectangle groups with `SDBgetSymbolValueScalar` or `SDBgetSymbolValueArray`. Missing scalars, arrays, names or format files take the recorded error path and close the database. Non-empty Odyssey, compositor and sync names are then parsed into the record's three 0x558-byte format slots with `vfmtReadCmp1OdyFormat`, `vfmtReadCmp1SG2Format` and `vfmtReadCmp1Format`; an empty name leaves that slot unused. The record fields are at +0x1380 for the major version, +0xff, +0 and +0x1fe for the three names, +0x300 through +0x370 for synchronisation and tiling, +0x350 and +0x354 for pipe count and size, +0x35c through +0x370 for synchronisation values, +0x1484 through +0x14f4 for the optional demultiplexer block, and +0x310 through +0x34c for the four rectangle groups. The listing reads `DmxActiveLinesF0` twice, so the second value replaces the first at +0x14b8; it likewise writes both low and high `OdylPSD` and `OdylHmask` names into their shared destination slots.
- `vfmtWriteCmp1Combination` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154d510, 5148 bytes): **established.**
  Writes the same combination record format (arguments: output name, 0x14f8-byte record; result 0 on success, 1 for file-open failure and 2 for an invalid record or failed symbol write). A null record records the invalid-combination error. It sets database version 1.1 and the description "Compositor video combination", finds the output path, opens it with `SDBopenNewDatabase`, and emits the three format names, the size and synchronisation arrays, the scalar synchronisation and tiling values, the optional demultiplexer fields and the four pipe rectangle groups at the offsets read by `vfmtReadCmp1Combination`. Version fields use the 16-bit type, names use the string type, the frame-rate field uses the float type, and the remaining scalar fields use the integer type. It writes `DmxVBlankLinesF0` from +0x14bc, `DmxVBlankLinesF1` from +0x14c0 and `DmxActiveLinesF1` from +0x14b8, then closes the database; any failed add returns 2.
- `mapTypeToXDRPROC` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154f7ac, 524 bytes): **established.**
  Maps a symbol type to an XDR procedure and element size, storing the procedure through the second argument and the size through the third. Types 1 through 20 select the table entries: void, signed and unsigned integer widths, character and Boolean forms, arrays, enumerations, wrapped strings, float, double and pointer. Type 20 takes the caller's procedure from the fourth argument and asserts that it is present. Type 7 uses `xdr_u_short` with size 2; type 12 uses `xdr_array` with size 1; types 13, 15 and 19 use `xdr_wrapstring` with sizes 1, 4 and 4; type 17 uses `xdr_double` with size 8; type 18 uses `xdr_pointer` with size 4. Type 0 asserts that the unused type was not requested, a type above 20 asserts that it is defined, type 14 asserts that its procedure is non-null, and a failed table lookup asserts before returning. The callers use the procedure for scalar or array symbol encoding and the size to advance fallback values.

## Invert, clear and set loops with a mask

Each of these loops was executed over random words with a small interpreter of the listing: the invert loop wrote the complement, the masked clear destination AND NOT mask, the masked set destination OR mask and the masked invert destination XOR mask, each with exactly one store per destination word and none outside the run, for runs of 1 to 129 words. These four are the long forms of the loops above: the constant and complement operations get a software-pipelined main loop with loads issued ahead of the stores. All take (source, destination pointer, destination end pointer) and the mask loops add the mask as a fourth argument; the source is never read. A run whose destination is at or past the end does nothing. The same two-path shape appears in all four: a head loop for the leading words with a store prefetch 0xfc bytes ahead, then, when at least one full block remains, a prefetch 0x100 bytes ahead, a main loop that loads the next block while storing the previous, and a plain block loop used instead when fewer than one block lies below the end.

- `nfb10RopLoop..KH` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1611b30, 1436 bytes): **established.**
  Invert: each destination word becomes its complement (`nor` with zero at 0x1611b78). The head handles the low three bits of the word count as single words, so up to seven; a block is eight words (32 bytes), the pipelined loop is unrolled four blocks to a pass (32 stores, 128 bytes, 0x1611c2c to 0x1611dd8) and stops when the cursor reaches the end less 64 bytes (0x40), after which the loaded words are written out in a tail of 16 stores. When the next block would pass the end the routine uses a plain loop of eight words a pass instead (the branch to 0x1612058).
- `nfb0MaskRopLoop..AI` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1653e10, 1460 bytes): **established.**
  Masked clear: the bits in the mask are cleared and the others kept, destination = destination AND NOT mask; the routine complements the mask once before the loops and uses a single `and` per word. Head of up to seven words, eight-word blocks, the same four-block unrolled pipelined loop (32 stores a pass, 128 bytes) and end-less-0x40 test as the invert loop; the fallback plain loop (0x1654350) stores eight words a pass and the tail 16. A run of 200 words did 184 of its stores in the pipelined loop and 16 in the tail.
- `nfb15MaskRopLoop..PI` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1582e00, 1460 bytes): **established.**
  Masked set: destination = destination OR mask, with the mask used as given (no complement step) and one `or` per word. Same head, block and pipelined-loop structure as the masked clear (32 stores a pass in the pipelined loop at 0x1582f08 to 0x15830b4, 8 in the fallback loop at 0x1583340, 16 in the tail), the two being the same code with `or` for `and` and no complement of the mask; its pipelined loop starts 0x108 bytes into the function against 0x110 for the clear, whose set-up has the `nor` that complements the mask (0x1653e48).
- `nfb10MaskRopLoop..KI` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16718d0, 812 bytes): **established.**
  Masked invert: destination = (destination AND NOT mask) OR (mask AND NOT destination), that is destination XOR mask, with the complement of the mask held in a register. It is the short-block form of the family: a head of up to three words (the low two bits of the word count), four-word blocks, a pipelined loop unrolled three blocks to a pass (12 stores, 48 bytes, 0x1671990 to 0x1671ad0) that stops at the end less 16 bytes (0x10), and a four-word tail (4 stores); a plain loop of four words a pass (0x1671b3c) covers the case where the next block reaches the end.

## Window-private set-up, graphics-context operation tables and small drawing entry points

- `odsyBlackCmap` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1693be0, 308 bytes): **established.**
  Loads an all-black colour map (argument: the screen). It takes a 0xd010-byte block by moving the stack pointer down. When the result is zero it prints "odsyBlackCmap: couldn't allocate memory" (format string at 0x172b7d0) and returns. Otherwise it writes the entry count 0x1a00 into the block's first halfword and builds 0x1a00 (6656) eight-byte entries from +0x10, each the pair (index k, 0), with k counting from 0 and the component word always 0; the unrolled fill is at 0x1693c3c to 0x1693cd8. A pointer to the entries is stored at +8. The block goes to `odsyLoadCmap` with the screen.
- `odsyCopyRect` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16cd970, 388 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the layer lookup, the double-buffer byte test and the three calls to the single-buffer copy were traced).
  Entry point for a window-to-window copy (arguments: the four rectangle arguments and the window). It finds the window's layer entry (the 0x44-byte records at +0x4c of the board record, indexed by the window's frame-buffer id minus the device record's first id, word +0xc) and clears word +0x84 of the board's command-state record. When the window's double-buffer byte (+1 of its `dbcWindowPrivateIndex` slot) is 0xff, meaning both buffers are drawn, and the layer's word is 3, it sets the byte to 0 and runs `odsyCopyRectSingleBuffer..F` for the front buffer, sets it to 1 and runs it again for the back buffer, then restores 0xff. Every other case runs the single-buffer copy once.
- `odsyDrawSolidRects` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16806b0, 400 bytes): **established.**
  The same both-buffers wrapper in front of `odsyDrawSolidRectsSingleBuffer..HE` (six arguments, the window last): it clears word +0x84 of the board's command-state record, tests the window's double-buffer byte for 0xff and the layer word for 3, and then draws with the byte set to 0 and again with it set to 1 before restoring 0xff; otherwise it draws once. The two wrappers differ only in the routine they call and the argument count, which the listing shows (the second call passes all six registers back, 0x1680784 and 0x1680800).
- `odsyValidateWindowPriv` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16120d0, 484 bytes): **established.**
  Sets a window's frame-buffer private record after a validate (argument: window). It installs the table `odsyWinOps` (0x1734198) at +0 and stores the window's hardware pixel mode, word +8 of the 0x14-byte visual record for the window's layer, at +0x14. A jump table of 12 modes then limits the three words at +8, +0x10 and +0xc to the mode's depth: modes 0, 7, 8 and 9 AND them with 0xff; modes 1, 10 and 11 with 0xfff; mode 2 stores 0xfff in +8 and ANDs the other two with 0xfff; mode 3 stores 0xffff in +8 and ANDs the others with 0xffff; mode 4 stores 0xffffff; modes 5 and 6 store 0x3fffffff. A mode of 12 or more prints "Unknown hwmode 0x%x" (rodata 0x172b6f0).
- `odsyValidateReducedGC` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15aea60, 628 bytes): **inferred** (from the decompilation; the jump table was read from the module's data).
  The context-side twin of the window routine (arguments: context, window). The words at +0x28, +0x2c, +0x34, +0x40, +0x44 and +0x4c of the context's frame-buffer private (slot `nfbGCPrivateIndex`) are the colour and mask values that must fit the window's pixel mode, and each is ANDed with the mode's mask: 0xff for modes 0, 7, 8 and 9; 0xfff for 1, 2, 10 and 11; 0x7fff for mode 3 (where the window routine uses 0xffff); 0xffffff for 4; 0x3fffffff for 5 and 6. Unlike the window routine it never stores a constant. A mode of 12 or more prints "Unrecognized hwmode: 0x%x" (rodata 0x172ae10).
- `odsyDrawMonoPixels` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 24920 bytes): **established.**
  The full routine starts at 0x1568230. It clears the board command-state word at +0x84 (`sw` at 0x15682d8). It derives the signed box width and height from the four halfwords at the first argument (the reads are `lh` at 0x15682dc, 0x15682e0, 0x15682ec and 0x15682f4), and returns through 0x1568c74 when either dimension is non-positive, the image pointer is null, or the raster operation is 5 (the tests are at 0x1568330, 0x1568338, 0x1568340 and 0x1568348). When the cached draw mode differs, it clears the pending flag at +0x1cb8 and emits the synchronization words 0x10443, 0xfa, 0x10046, 0x10046 and 0x10019; the corresponding FIFO stores are at 0x156838c, 0x1568398, 0x15683a4, 0x15683ac and 0x15683b8. It then selects a format-specific packing path through the table reached from the mode value at the board record's +0x2668, reading source bytes, halfwords or words and packing them into the FIFO stream; the table dispatch begins at 0x1568778, with byte loads such as `lbu` at 0x15688e0, halfword loads at 0x156e148 and 0x156e14c, and word loads at 0x156e0d8. Each path advances the source by the caller's row stride and loops over the derived width and height, so the image is sent row by row rather than copied as an opaque block. At completion it restores the saved registers, clears the pending flag when the path sent its terminating command, and returns through the common epilogue at 0x1568c94. **Established** from the complete 6.5.30m N32 `odyssey.so` listing and the exact instruction addresses above; this describes the shipped driver's FIFO stream, not hardware behavior.
- `odsyDrawOpaqueMonoImage` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16c0210, 512 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the descriptor refresh and the call to `odsyDrawMonoPixels` with its stack arguments were traced).
  Draws a one-bit image with both foreground and background (the window is the ninth argument, on the stack). It clears word +0x84 of the board's command-state record, compares the clip box held in the board record (halfwords +0x60 to +0x72) with the copy last sent, and, when it changed, rebuilds the window descriptor words at +0x2b40 to +0x2b5c of the state record (the first is the box's x0 shifted left 12 with y0 in the low 12 bits, the second the same for x1 minus 1 and y1 minus 1, then 0xffffff, 0, 0xffffff, 0, 0xffffff, 0 for the three unused boxes), when the state record's word +0x1cb8 is set it clears it and first sends the four words 0x10443, 0xfa, 0x10046 and 0x10046 to the command FIFO, then sends command 0x1204b with the eight descriptor words. It then calls `odsyDrawMonoPixels` with the stack flag set to 1.
- `odsyDrawMonoImage` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x170eb80, 536 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end and its stores compared with those of the opaque form).
  The transparent form of the same routine (the window is the eighth argument, in a register): the same clip-descriptor refresh, the same optional four-word prefix and command 0x1204b as `odsyDrawOpaqueMonoImage`, and the same tail call to `odsyDrawMonoPixels`, but with the flag passed in the stack slot at 0xc(sp) set to 0 (0x170ed6c), where the opaque routine passes 1 (0x16c03ec).
- `odsyReadPFDArgs..LD` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16e7820, 936 bytes): **inferred** (from the decompilation; the ten symbol names were read from rodata).
  Reads the ten pixel-format limit values from a symbol-database file (arguments: screen, file name; result 0, or 10 when the file cannot be opened). A name that starts with "/" is the path (truncated to 1023 bytes); any other name is copied (at most 511 bytes), ".vfo" is appended unless it already contains it, and `odsyMakeFileName..EB` builds the path under "/usr/gfx/ucode/ODSY/vof". `SDBreadOpenDatabase` opens it (failure prints "odsyReadPFDArgs: failed to open %s"). Ten scalars are then read in this order into the board record at +0x10c, +0x110, +0x114, +0x118, +0x11c, +0x120, +0x124, +0x128, +0x12c and +0x130: ODY_GEN_lPFD, ODY_GEN_hPFD, ODY_GEN_lPFD_HI, ODY_GEN_hPFD_HI, ODY_GEN_lPFD_LO, ODY_GEN_hPFD_LO, ODY_GEN_lPSD, ODY_GEN_hPSD, ODY_GEN_lHMASK and ODY_GEN_hHMASK (rodata 0x172c508 to 0x172c630). The first missing symbol prints "odsyGetScalar: unable to find %s" and stops the reading, but the result stays 0; the database is closed on every path.
- `odsyNewGCOps..E` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x166d410, 1244 bytes): **inferred** (from the decompilation; the default table was read from rodata).
  Builds and caches a drawing-operation table for a graphics context (arguments: context, cache index). It allocates 0x54 bytes (21 words) and copies the default table from rodata 0x172adb0: fill-spans `nfbFillSpans`, set-spans, put-image, copy-area, copy-plane, `odsyPolyPnt`, `miWideLine`, `miPolySegment`, `miPolyRectangle`, `odsyPolyArc`, `odsyFillPolygon`, `nfbPolyFillRect`, `miPolyFillArc`, the four text entries, `odsyImageGlyphBlt`, `miPolyGlyphBlt`, `miPushPixels` and a zero. The context state word at +0x10 gives the fill style in bits 24 and 25 and the line style in bits 30 and 31. For a zero-width line (halfword +6): a solid line with solid fill takes `odsyLineSS` (polyline), `odsySegmentSS` (segments) and `odsyZeroPolyArc`; a solid line with other fill takes `miZeroLine`, `miPolySegment` and `miZeroPolyArc`; a dashed line with solid fill takes `odsyLineSD` and `odsySegmentSD`, or `nfbLineSD` and `miPolySegment` when the byte at +4 of the context's private record is 0; a dashed line with another fill takes `miZeroDashLine` and `miPolySegment`, with `miZeroPolyArc` for arcs in all dashed cases. A wide dashed line takes `odsyWideDash`. Solid fill sets fill-spans to `odsySolidFillSpans`, fill-polygon to `odsyFillPolygonSolid`, poly-fill-rect to `odsyPolyFillRectSolid` and poly-glyph to `odsyPolyGlyphBlt`. When the font (context +0x2c) has bit 14 of its word +0xc set and the sum of its halfwords at +0x44 and +0x46 is at most 16, its width (+0x16 minus +0x20) of 6, 8 or 9 selects the `odsyImageGlyphBltTE6`, `TE8` or `TE9` image routine and, for solid fill, the matching `odsyPolyGlyphBltTE` routine. Stippled and opaque-stippled fills with a stipple pixmap (context +0x24) of at most 32 by 32 take `odsyPolyFillRectStippled`, or `odsyPolyFillRectStippled32x32` and `odsyFillPolygonStippled` when it is exactly 32 wide and its height is a power of two. The table is stored in `odsyGCOpsCache` at the index.

## Rectangle copy, window copy and circle routines of the 2D layer

- `odsyCopyRectBody` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16b2bd0, 1500 bytes): **established.**
  Sends one rectangle copy to the board (arguments: source box of four halfwords x0, y0, x1, y1; destination point of two halfwords; window; a block of eight words describing the copy). Its command words were extracted from the listing in address order and agree with the decompilation. It first clears word +0x84 of the board's command-state record, then sends three groups, each preceded by clearing the state record's word +0x1cb8: 0x10443, 0xfa, 0x10046, 0x10046, 0x10019 (5 words); 0x10443, 0x96 and four 0x10046 (6 words); 0x10443, 0xfa, 0x10046, 0x10046, then the state write 0x10658 with 0x120000 (6 words); it then zeroes the halfword at +0x68 of the FIFO-pointer record. The copy itself is command 0x2031 followed by eight words: the block's word 0, the destination x and y packed with y in the high halfword, block word 2, the size ((y1 minus y0) in the high halfword, (x1 minus x0) in the low), block words 4 and 5, the source packed the same way (y0 high, x0 low) and block word 7. When bit 27 (0x8) of the byte at the top of block word 7 is clear, that block of nine words is all that is sent and the routine returns. When it is set, the 0x2031 block is preceded by a textured set-up: an optional four-word sync (0x10443, 0xfa, 0x10046, 0x10046) when +0x1cb8 is set, three 0x10046, 0x11453 with the five words at +0x1b34 to +0x1b44, the four-word sync again, 0x14400 with operand 10, the rectangle's four corners as floats (flipped in x when bit 31 of block word 7's top byte is set and in y when bit 30 is set; stored at +0x1b5c to +0x1b78), 0xcf80a92f with ten words from +0x1b4c to +0x1b70, and the quad header 0x8080c800 with two zero words; after the block it sends 0x14001, the four-word sync when +0x1b7c is nonzero, and leaves +0x1cb8 set to 1 (0x16b30f0); the short path returns without setting it.
- `odsyCopyWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16f4e40, 1452 bytes): **inferred** (read in the decompilation; the region slot numbers are matched against the screen record's use in other routines).
  Moves a window's contents after the window moved (arguments: window, the old origin as two halfwords packed in one register, the region of newly exposed area to copy). The offset is old origin minus new origin (window +8 and +0xa). With a single frame-buffer layer (private word +0x1c zero) it translates the region by the negated offset with the screen's slot +0x178 and intersects it with the window's clip (window +0x38) through slot +0x164; with several layers it translates each non-empty region of the array (count at the device record's +0x28, 12 bytes each) and intersects the window's clip with the region of the window's own layer (private word +0x18). The surviving rectangles are copied into a stack array moved by the offset (four-way unrolled halfword adds) and `odsyDoBitBltSS..BB` does the blit with the window; the stack array is freed with slot +0x160 when it was not allocated. For a multi-layer window it then walks the window tree through the first-child (+0x24), next-sibling (+0x1c) and parent (+0x18) links, collecting each descendant's clip per layer into two accumulator regions (a bit mask of the layers seen, the layer number being the private word +0x18) and copies those with a second `odsyDoBitBltSS..BB` call per layer, up to two layers.
- `odsyFastZeroCircle..EE` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15fdc10, 1484 bytes): **inferred** (read in the decompilation; the integer stepping variables were followed through both parities but not re-derived from the listing).
  Draws the outline of a circle of zero line width as one-pixel rectangles (arguments: screen, an unused second argument, diameter, x of the box, y of the box). It selects block 0x14400 with operand 0 and then sends each rectangle as four vertices, each the word 0x8080c800 followed by an x and a y word, ending with 0x14001 and, when the board state's word +0x1b7c is set, the sync 0x10443, 0xfa, 0x10046, 0x10046; the state's word +0x1cb8 is set to 1 and the halfword at +0x68 of the FIFO-pointer record to 1 on return. An even diameter starts from the four axis rectangles at the centre and steps two decision variables (initially 17 - 2 x diameter and 4 x diameter - 12; the second falls by 8 or 16 a step, by 16 when the first is not negative) outward, sending eight rectangles per step by symmetry; an odd diameter uses a separate stepping loop with decision variables starting at 10 - 2 x diameter and 4 x diameter - 8, repeating until the row reaches the centre row and column, and finishes with the central rectangle pair.

## Clip and visual validation routines

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, read from a Ghidra decompilation and listing with `$gp` resolved and the global-offset-table slots named, with every branch quoted below checked in the listing. The screen's region procedures are named from their slot offsets, which follow the order of the public X11 screen record (initialise +0x154, copy +0x158, uninitialise +0x160, intersect +0x164, union +0x168, subtract +0x16c, translate +0x178, empty +0x188, append +0x190, validate +0x194); the argument shapes at every call agree with that order, but the slot names are **inferred** from it, not from a table in the module. A region is the 12-byte X region record: extents at +0 (four halfwords x0, y0, x1, y1) and a data pointer at +8 whose word +4 is the rectangle count and whose boxes start at +8.

- `odsyValidateClip` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x166bb50, 2160 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the thirteen request words, the one-box and window-box cases, the layout-dependent clears and the final byte update were each traced to an address).
  Sends the kernel a window's visible clip (argument: window; no result). The window's clip region is at window +0x2c, with its data pointer at +0x34; no data pointer means the clip is the single extents box. The routine builds a request of 13 words and sends it with `ioctl(descriptor, 0x3f3, request)` (1011, `RRM_ValidateClip`, descriptor at +100 of the device record behind screen +0x74). In memory order the words are: the render-node id (window resource-manager record +0xc), the window id (window +4), the window's origin and size as four words (halfwords +8 and +0xa signed, +0xc and +0xe unsigned), the entry count, a pointer to the entry array, the record's identifier (+0x14), two flag words, the constant 1 and the record's first mode word (+0x20). Each array entry is four words (x, y, width, height); the clip's boxes come first, converted from (x0, y0, x1, y1), and the window's own box is always the last entry, so the count is the box count plus one. The cases (listing 0x166bbe4 to 0x166bd70 and 0x166bf24): when bit 1 of the record's first halfword is set the array holds only the window box (count 1), and window bit 11 of +0x7c decides the rest: set (0x166bc54, branch to 0x166c0e0) makes the header box the whole screen (origin 0, 0, size from screen +8 and +0xa) with the first flag word 0, clear leaves the header at the window's own origin and size with the first flag word 1; when the clip is one box that equals the window box and lies inside the screen, the count is 1 and both flag words are 0; an empty clip (data present, no boxes) gives count 1, header width and height 0 and first flag 1; a clip of one to four boxes sends them all with first flag 1 and second flag 0; a clip of more than four boxes sends only the window box with both flags 1.
  On a head layout other than 0 (device record +0xdc) whose window frame-buffer private (`nfbWindowPrivateIndex`) has a zero word at +0x34 and which has boxes, it first clears the window's area: it translates the region in y by the device record's halfword +0xe2, intersects it with the first or second head's rectangle (layout 1: y from +0xe2 to +0xe0 plus +0xe2; layout 2: y from 0 to +0xe0, full screen width), calls `odsyClearPlanes` (screen, region, 1 when the window's pixel mode word +0x14 is 7 and 0 otherwise, value 0) once per head, translating by -0xe2 between the layout-1 clears and by +0xe2 between the layout-2 clears, and finally by -0xe2 again; a layout 0 screen never reaches this clear (0x166bbc8 jumps over it for a zero layout; a window with no clip data pointer counts as one box and does clear) although the code at 0x166c398 would make a single call for it. A failed ioctl prints "odsyValidateClip: RRM_VALIDATECLIP failed, rnid=0x%x, clipid=0x%x: %s" (rodata 0x172bbf0) with the render-node id, the window id and `strerror(errno)`.
  It ends by testing bit 1 of the record byte +4 (0x166bd30): when it was clear the routine sets it and clears bit 1 of byte +5 (0x166c094 to 0x166c0c4; byte +4 is the record's request byte that `rrmInvalidate` masks), and when it was already set both bytes are left alone. How the kernel uses the two flag words is **unknown** here.
- `odsyValidateVisual` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1652360, 4160 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the three region passes, the head-split clears and the inline plane-clear word stream were each traced store by store).
  Repaints the plane identifiers after the visual regions of a screen changed (argument: screen; no result). The device record (screen +0x74) holds the count of visual regions at +0x54, their current areas as 12-byte regions from +0x5c, a second region array from +0x60 (the area each region owned before), a word array at +0x30 that gives each region's group (0 or 1), two group regions from +0x2c and two group flags from +0x3c; the board record (slot `odsyScreenPrivateIndex`, +0x40) keeps two regions that hold the group areas last painted. Three scratch regions are made with the region-initialise slot. Pass one validates each visual region and, for a non-empty one, subtracts the old area from it and appends the remainder to its group's scratch region; both group scratch regions are then validated. Pass two runs for each visual region: its old area loses the group's changed area, gains the new region when that is non-empty, and the new region is cleared with `odsyClearPlanes` (screen, region, -1, region number) so the small-field plane in that region takes the region's number (value shifted left 3 into the 5-bit field, per the plane-clear entry); on head layout 1 the region is first intersected with the first head's rectangle (y from +0xe2 to +0xe0 plus +0xe2), cleared, moved up by +0xe2 and cleared again (0x1652648 to 0x16526e8), on layout 2 with the second head's (y from 0 to +0xe0), cleared, moved down by +0xe2 and cleared again (0x16524f4 to 0x1652588); on layout 0 there is one clear (0x16528b0). The old area is then intersected with the group's region from +0x2c and the new region emptied. Pass three runs for both groups whose flag is set: the area last painted minus the group's new area is cleared with `odsyClearPlanes` (screen, region, group number 0 or 1, value 0), with the same head-split handling, and for group 1 additionally cleared with plane argument -1 and value 0x20 (a 3-bit field of zero); on layout 2 that second clear is an inline copy of the plane-clear command stream (0x1652968 to 0x1653214; the 0x10443 settle sequence of 0xfa and 0x96 operands is repeated wherever the board record's pending flag at +0x1cb8 is set, then the flag is cleared; window descriptor 0x1204b, destination 0x10404 = 0x80000, write mask 3 through 0x10424, the 0x22008001 and 0x23008003 words that are skipped when the cached words at +0x2664 and +0x2668 already hold 1 and 3, the 0xce80cc08 and 0xc580c406 blocks of zeros, descriptor 0x11023 with 0x7000 and 1, mask 0x10422 = 3, the four 0x11017 forms, 0x14400 operand 7, and four vertices of 0x8080c800 per rectangle, 0x14001 last). The flag is then reset and the group's painted area is replaced with a copy of its new area. The three scratch regions are released. What the group numbers mean in the display (they split the regions into two sets) is **unknown** here.

## Zero-width arc points, tiled fills and pixel read-back routines

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, read from a Ghidra decompilation and listing with `$gp` resolved; the two message strings and the call targets quoted below were checked in the listing. Entries here are **inferred**: the decompilation was read in full, the listing was consulted only where the entry names an address.

- `odsyZeroArcPts..FE` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x162e1b0, 1936 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: both send loops, the even-width axis points and the closing points were traced store by store).
  Sends the pixels of one zero-width arc to the board as single vertices (arguments: screen, an unused second argument, the arc: halfword width at +4 and height at +6). It zeroes word +0x84 of the board's command-state record (the record reached through `buzz_regionsp` and the board record's pipe index), sends 0x14400 with operand 0 and calls the shared X server's `miZeroArcSetup(arc, &info, 1)` (GOT slot 0x1733480), which fills a stack record (the field roles below are read from how the loops use each word, not from a declaration): the current x and y, the decision variable and its increments, the two origins (the arc's first corner and the opposite corner, used as the mirror), the end point and the start and end quadrant masks. Every point goes out as the word 0x8080c800 followed by its x and a y word, mirrored into up to four quadrants (x + origin and y + origin; opposite x minus x; opposite y minus y; combinations of both), with the quadrant bits 1, 2, 4 and 8 of a mask selecting which mirror is sent. Before the loop, when the arc's width is even (bit 0 of the halfword +4 clear) the two axis points selected by mask bits 2 and 8 are sent once, at the opposite x with the first row's y and at the opposite x with the opposite y. A nonzero result of the setup call (a full ellipse) selects a loop that sends all four mirrors at every step until the point reaches the end coordinates; a zero result selects a loop that checks, after every step, whether the point reached the start or end coordinate of the angular range and swaps in the alternate mask, and sends only the quadrants the mask allows. In both loops a step moves either one pixel along the major axis or diagonally, by the sign of the decision variable, and when the first increment goes negative the loop swaps the increments for the other octant. After the loop the end point is sent, using the alternate mask (stack word +0x50) when x equals the stack word +0x48 or y equals +0x4c and the main mask (+0x44) otherwise (0x162e444 to 0x162e458); mask bits 1 and 4 always send, bits 2 and 8 only when the arc's height is odd (halfword +6 bit 0; 0x162e4cc to 0x162e4d8), then 0x14001, then the sync 0x10443, 0xfa, 0x10046, 0x10046 when the board record's +0x1b7c is nonzero; the record's +0x1cb8 is set to 1 (0 before the sync is sent) and the halfword +0x68 of the FIFO-pointer record to 1.
- `odsyTileRects` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1681ca0, 3044 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the early returns, the replication sizing from the first box, the doubling copies and the double-buffer byte save and restore were traced to addresses; the three table procedures it calls are not read here).
  Fills a list of boxes with a repeating tile (arguments: array of boxes as four halfwords x0, y0, x1, y1, the box count, the tile's pixels, the tile's row pitch in bytes, the tile's width and height in pixels, a pointer to the tile origin as two halfwords, the raster-operation code 0 to 15, the plane mask and the window). The window's frame-buffer private (slot `nfbWindowPrivateIndex`) points to a table of procedures; the routine uses entry 0 (copy a source box to a destination point, called with operation 3 and the plane mask), entry 1 (fill boxes with a constant, arguments boxes, count, value, operation, plane mask, window) and entry 2 (draw one box of pixels, arguments box, data, pitch, operation, plane mask, window). Operation 5 (no change) returns at once. Operations 0 (clear), 15 (set) and 10 (invert) are not tiled: each goes to entry 1 with value 0, -1 and -1 and operation 3, 3 and 10. For the others the pixel size is the shift in the high nibble of byte +3 of the window.
  When the tile has fewer pixels than a threshold (`env_replicate_tile_threshold`, or `env_replicate_tile_for_cp_threshold` when `enableStencilTest` is 0 and the operation is 3 or 12) it is first widened by repeating it across (in whole tiles, up to the smaller of the first box's width and the threshold divided by the tile height; the other boxes of the list do not enter the sizing) and then repeated down (whole tiles again, up to the smaller of the first box's height and the threshold divided by the new width) into a buffer from `Xalloc`, using `bcopy` per row, and that buffer becomes the tile (width, height and pitch updated, released with `Xfree` at the end); with no enlargement possible it keeps the original.
  The first-box offset into the tile is the (x minus origin x) and (y minus origin y) remainders modulo the tile size, computed through floating-point division and truncation (so a negative offset gives a negative remainder that is fixed up by adding the size). For operations 3 and 12 with the stencil test off, each box is drawn only for its first tile-sized block (up to four pieces through entry 2, split at the tile seams), and the rest is filled by doubling: with the window's double-buffer private (slot `dbcWindowPrivateIndex`) byte 0 forced to 1 or 0 (1 when byte 1 is 1 or 0xfd) for the duration, entry 0 copies the drawn block to the right at doubling widths and then downward at doubling heights, with a final shorter copy for the remainder; the byte is restored after each box. In every other case each box is drawn tile by tile through entry 2 (a row of tiles for the first row of the box, then the same row's pixel data for the rest of the row, then the next row of tiles). Division by a zero tile size traps.
- `odsyReadConvertPixels` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15b30b0, 3760 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: each depth's field masks and table index were traced, including the group bits that differ between the 8-bit and 12-bit paths).
  Reads a box of the window's pixels and converts them to 32-bit pixels with 0xff in the alpha byte (arguments: window, the layer index, the box as four halfwords x0, y0, x1, y1, the destination row length in words, the destination). The window depth is the byte at window +2; 0 returns, otherwise a scratch buffer of rows padded by `PixmapWidthPaddingInfo` (the entry for the depth gives the padding and the two shifts) is made with `Xalloc`; failing that it prints "odsyReadConvertPixels: Image buffer allocation failed" and returns.
  The window's layer entry is its screen-record index (the id in the word behind window +0x78, or from `FindWindowWithOptional` when that pointer is null, minus the device record's first id at +0x50); when the layer's pixel mode (word 0 of the 0x14-byte entries at device record +0x58) is 0xc or 0x16 the read is `odsyReadDeepImage..GE`, otherwise entry 5 (offset +0x14) of the window's frame-buffer procedure table, called with (box, buffer, row bytes, window). The conversion by depth: 8 bits looks each pixel up in the board's colour-map table (board record +0x54, 256 entries a group, the group number taken from the layer's mode word (word +8 of the 0x14-byte entry at device record +0x58): bits 8:4 when the layer's word in the array at device record +0x30 is 0 (0x15b3c2c) and bits 7:3 when it is nonzero (0x15b3348)) and repacks the three 10-bit fields of the entry (bits 29:22, 19:12 and 9:2) into the three low bytes, leaving the destination pixel unchanged for index 0 in the second group when mode bit 1 is clear; 12 bits (0xc) on a visual of class 4 expands each 4-bit field into a byte by repeating the nibble, otherwise looks up the low 12 bits plus 256 times bits 8:4 of the mode word in the same table, whatever the group array says (0x15b3554 to 0x15b3594); 15 bits (0xf) widens the three 5-bit fields to bytes by repeating their top three bits and replicates bit 15 over the alpha byte; 24 bits (0x18) copies the rows with `bcopy` on a class-4 visual and otherwise maps each of the three colour bytes through a 256-entry table selected by bits 8:4 of the mode word (entries at +0x400 a group; 0x15b3908 to 0x15b3918), keeping the top byte; 30 bits (0x1e) copies with `bcopy` after a deep read and otherwise reduces the three 10-bit fields to their top 8 bits and fans the top two bits out over the alpha byte. Any other depth prints "odsyReadConvertPixels: Unsupported depth '%d'" and writes nothing.
  The scratch buffer is released with `Xfree`.

## Generic glyph drawing and graphics-context validation

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, read from a Ghidra decompilation and listing with `$gp` resolved. The three entries are **inferred**: the decompilation was read for the whole routine and the listing only at the addresses and strings the entries name. The glyph routines work on the shared X server's character records: five signed halfwords (left bearing, right bearing, advance, ascent, descent) followed by attributes, with the glyph's bitmap pointer at +0xc; the bitmap rows are padded to an even number of bytes.

- `odsyPolyGlyphBlt` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1610c60, 3784 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the record-building loop, both bitmap merge cases, the descriptor refresh test and the batch loop were traced to addresses).
  Draws a string of glyphs with the context's foreground in one blit (arguments: drawable, context, x, y, number of glyphs, array of pointers to glyph records). The context's frame-buffer private (slot `nfbGCPrivateIndex`) supplies the clip region (record +8, whose data pointer at +8 gives a count at +4 and boxes from +8; no data pointer means the region's single box), words +0x28 and +0x34 and the byte +0x30, and the routine first clears word +0x84 of the board's command-state record and sends the 0x1204b window descriptor again when the board record's current window box (halfwords +0x60 to +0x66) differs from the box last sent (+0x6c to +0x72; 0x1610d34 to 0x1610ea4; this test is on the window box, not on the context's clip boxes, which are sent later per batch; the descriptor's first word of a box is x0 shifted left 12 with y0 in the low 12 bits, the second the same for x1 minus 1 and y1 minus 1, and the unused slots hold 0xffffff and 0). It builds a table of 32-byte records on the stack (one per glyph: pen x, the glyph's left x, right x, top y, bottom y, bitmap pointer, row bytes), advancing the pen by each glyph's advance and tracking the minimum and maximum of all four edges; the stack is made large enough by moving the stack pointer, and a null result prints "odsyPolyGlyphBlt(): can't allocate memory". The bounding box gives the row length in bytes (width plus 7, shifted right 3) and a second stack block, cleared with `bzero`, for a one-bit-per-pixel bitmap of the whole string. Each glyph's rows are ORed into that bitmap at its bit offset (a byte-aligned copy when the offset is a multiple of 8, otherwise a two-byte shift merge with the carry into the next byte). The bitmap is sent to `odsyDrawMonoPixels` (box of the bounding rectangle as four halfwords, the bitmap, a zero, the row length, the context word +0x28, zero, the byte +0x30 as the raster operation and the word +0x34, with the drawable as an eighth argument on the stack), once for each batch of up to four clip boxes; for each batch the descriptor words 0x2b40 to 0x2b7c of the board record are rewritten with the batch's packed boxes (unused slots 0xffffff, 0), the descriptor 0x1204b is resent (preceded by the sync 0x10443, 0xfa, 0x10046, 0x10046 when +0x1cb8 is set) and the sent box becomes the cached one. A single box that equals the cache is not resent. The meaning of the words at +0x28, +0x34 and +0x30 of the context's private (foreground, plane mask and operation, by their use as the third, last and seventh arguments of the mono-pixel routine) is inferred from that call.
- `odsyImageGlyphBlt` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15e0f00, 4080 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end, with its record, bitmap and batch code compared instruction by instruction against the plain glyph routine).
  Draws a string of glyphs over a filled background rectangle (arguments as for `odsyPolyGlyphBlt`). It saves the context's word +0x18 (the foreground pixel) and byte +5 (the raster operation), sets the foreground to the frame-buffer private's word +0x2c (the background colour, taken from listing 0x15e0f84 to 0x15e0fa0) and the operation to 3 (copy), calls the first slot of the context's procedure table (pointer at context +0x44, the X server's validate-context slot, 0x15e1204 to 0x15e1218) with (context, change mask 5, drawable) for the new values, fills the rectangle of the text with `odsyPolyFillRectSolid` (drawable, context, one rectangle), restores the saved values and calls the same entry again. The rectangle is x from the string's left edge to its right edge, y from the baseline minus the font ascent (halfword +0x44 of the context's font at +0x2c) to the baseline plus the font descent (+0x46).
