  For a constant-width font (bit 12 of the font's word at +0xc) the extent comes from the advance (+0x24) times the glyph count, otherwise from `QueryGlyphExtents` (context font, glyph array, count, result record whose overall advance sits at +0x14). After the fill it builds the same one-bit bitmap of the glyphs as `odsyPolyGlyphBlt` (same 32-byte records; its own failure text "odsyImageGlyphBlt(): can't allocate memory" at 0x172b800, printed for either a failed record table or a failed bitmap block) and sends it with `odsyDrawMonoPixels`, with the raster operation fixed at 3 instead of the context's byte and the drawable on the stack as for `odsyPolyGlyphBlt`, once per batch of up to four clip boxes (call at 0x15e1c24). The window descriptor 0x1204b is refreshed first when the board record's window box (halfwords +0x60 to +0x66) differs from the box last sent (+0x6c to +0x72), as in `odsyPolyGlyphBlt` (the store of its first word is at 0x15e1064).
- `odsyValidateWindowGC` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16303a0, 4996 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the pixmap copy, the fill-style table choice, the dash pattern and the cache index bits were each traced to addresses).
  Re-validates a window's graphics context after a change (arguments: context, the change mask, drawable). It first calls `odsyValidateReducedGC` when the mask has any of 0x10e.
  When mask bit 0x800 (the tile or stipple pixmap changed) is set and the context has a stipple or tile pixmap at +0x24 narrower than 17 pixels, it makes a copy through the pixmap's screen procedure at +0xec, copies the rows (`memmove`), masks each row to the pixmap's width, then repeatedly ORs each row with itself shifted right by the width, doubling the width until it is wider than 16 (the copy takes the pixmap's place at +0x24, with the old one's reference count at +0x18 dropped and freed with `Xfree` at zero; 0x1630be4 to 0x1631198); a pixmap already wider than 16 is left as it is. When the mask has the fill-style bit 0x100 clear, the pixmap path then runs a four-way test of the fill style (0x163104c to 0x1631088) whose fall-through calls `FatalError` with "odsyValidateWindowGC: illegal fillStyle"; the style is a two-bit field, so that fall-through cannot be reached.
  The context's fill style, bits 25:24 of the word at +0x10, selects the private operation table stored in word +0x24 of the frame-buffer private: solid selects `odsySolidGCPrivOps`; tiled `nfbTiledPrivOps`; stippled `odsyStippledGCPrivOps` when the stipple pixmap is at most 32 pixels wide and `nfbStippledPrivOps` otherwise; opaque-stippled `odsySolidGCPrivOps` when the foreground (+0x18) equals the background (+0x1c), else `odsyStippledGCPrivOps` for a stipple up to 32 wide and `nfbOpStippledPrivOps` otherwise.
  The table choice runs when any of 0x10c changed (or 0x800). When the mask has 0x300000 (dashes) the routine adds up the dash lengths (bytes at the pointer in +0xc, count in halfword +0xa); a total above 32 clears the byte at +4 of the module's context private (slot `odsyGCPrivateIndex`) and skips the pattern (the routine goes on to the index below), otherwise it ORs a run of ones of the on-dash length at the running offset for every on-off pair, repeats the pattern by doubling until the period passes 16, rotates it right by the dash offset (halfword +8), stores it in word +0 of that private and stores the period minus 0x11 in byte +4. Unless the mask is wholly outside 0x304130 (and no flag was set), it computes a cache index from the context: bit 0 when the line width (halfword +6) is 0, bit 1 when the line style (bits 31:30 of +0x10) is solid, bit 5 when the private byte +4 is nonzero, bit 2 when the fill style is solid, bits 3 and 4 when the font (+0x2c) has bit 14 of its flag word set, its glyph cell is 6, 8 or 9 wide (the difference of halfwords +0x16 and +0x20: 6 gives 0x18, 8 gives 0x10, 9 gives 8) and its ascent plus descent is under 17, and bit 6 or 7 when the fill style is stippled or opaque-stippled with a stipple up to 32 by 32 (bit 7 when 32 wide with a power-of-two height, bit 6 otherwise). The index, shifted left by 2, picks a pointer from the module's operation-table cache (`odsyGCOpsCache`); a null entry makes the routine call `odsyNewGCOps..E` with (context, the index) and use its result (0x1631030 to 0x1631040; this routine does not store into the cache, the callee builds and caches the table). The context's operations pointer (+0x48) takes the table. In every path that returns, the frame-buffer private's halfwords +0x58 and +0x5a take the drawable origin (halfwords +8 and +0xa) plus the context's clip origin (+0x28 and +0x2a).

## Audio-library entry points, backing-store wrapper restores and small mi helpers

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, every listing below read in full from a Ghidra listing with `$gp` resolved (13 to 30 instructions each, so all entries are **established** unless marked). The audio-library objects use the two magic words of the statically linked library: 0xbb81 at +0 of an open port and 0xbb82 at +0 of a configuration. A failing check sets `errno` through `setoserror` and calls the library's error hook (a function pointer, skipped when null) with the code, a message from its table and the routine's name.

- `ALsetsampfmt` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1550590, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Old-style alias of the sample-format setter: it loads the target from the global-offset-table slot at gp - 0x7e40 and branches-and-links (0x15505ac) to `alSetSampFmt` at 0x1552860 with the arguments untouched, then returns its result.
- `alSetSampFmt` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1552860, 372 bytes): **established.**
  Stores a sample format in a configuration (arguments: configuration, format). The configuration needs the magic 0xbb82, otherwise `errno` 2 and the invalid-configuration message. The format must be 1, 0x20, 0x40 or one of the seven words 0x1000002f to 0x10000035; anything else gives `errno` 0x13 (19) with its message and -1. A good value goes to +0xc and the result is 0.
- `ALgetsampfmt` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15505d0, 52 bytes): **established.**
  The reading twin of the setter alias: the slot at gp - 0x7e3c holds `alGetSampFmt` (0x15529e0), and the `bgezall` at 0x15505ec reaches it, so the format word of a configuration comes back in the result register unchanged.
- `ALsetchannels` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1550610, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Calls `alSetChannels` (0x15520b0, slot at gp - 0x7e58) through the `bgezall` at 0x155062c; the frame is only the saved `gp` and return address, so the caller's two arguments arrive as they were.
- `alSetChannels` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15520b0, 312 bytes): **established.**
  Sets the channel count of a configuration (magic 0xbb82 required, else `errno` 2). Only 1, 2, 4, 8 and 16 are accepted; any other count gives `errno` 0x11 (17) with the error hook called and -1. An accepted count is stored at +0x10 and the result is 0.
- `ALgetchannels` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1550650, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Forwards to `alGetChannels` at 0x15521f0 (slot at gp - 0x7e54, branch-and-link at 0x155066c), which returns the count at +0x10 of a checked configuration.
- `ALgetfloatmax` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15506d0, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Where its siblings use a relative branch-and-link, this alias calls through the loaded address: `jalr t9` at 0x15506ec reaches `alGetFloatMax` (0x1562420, slot at gp - 0x7d28) with the configuration argument unchanged, and the double it returns is passed back (no register is touched after the call).
- `ALcloseport` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1550710, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Alias for `alClosePort` at 0x1551ac0, reached with the `bgezall` at 0x155072c through the slot at gp - 0x7e60.
- `alClosePort` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1551ac0, 232 bytes): **established.**
  Closes an open audio port (magic 0xbb81, else `errno` 1 and the invalid-port message with -1). It unmaps the port's ring buffer (address +0xc, length +4) with `munmap`, closes the descriptor at +0x24, writes the closed-port magic 0xbb92 over +0, clears the fields at +0x1c, +0x14, +0x10, +0xc, +8 and +4, sets the descriptor field to -1 and frees the port with `free`, returning 0.
- `ALgetframenumber` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15507d0, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Alias for `alGetFrameNumber` at 0x15516e0 (slot at gp - 0x7e70, `bgezall` at 0x15507ec); the port and the pointer for the frame number reach the target unchanged.
- `alGetFrameNumber` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15516e0, 372 bytes): **established.**
  Reports the frame the port has reached (arguments: port, pointer to a 64-bit result). A null port or a wrong magic gives `errno` 1. It issues `ioctl(descriptor at +0x24, 0xb, buffer)` and on failure sets `errno` 0 with the hook and returns -1. The buffer gives a 64-bit counter and two ring positions; their difference (the second minus the first, plus the ring size at +8 when negative) is divided by the word at +0x38 when bit 28 of the flag word at +0x3c is set, shifted right by the sample-size shift at +0x80, and subtracted from the counter when the port's direction word at +0x28 is 0 or added when it is not. The result is written through the pointer and the return is 0.
- `ALgetframetime` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1550810, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Alias for `alGetFrameTime` at 0x1552330, reached with the `bgezall` at 0x155082c; its three arguments (port and the two result pointers) arrive as they were.
- `alGetFrameTime` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1552330, 284 bytes): **established.**
  Reads the time stamp of a frame on an open port (arguments: port, pointer to a 64-bit frame number, pointer to a 64-bit time; magic 0xbb81 required, else `errno` 1). It runs `ioctl(descriptor at +0x24, 0x13, buffer)` and copies the buffer's first and third doublewords through the two pointers; a negative ioctl result sets `errno` 0, calls the hook and returns -1, otherwise 0.
- `miBSCheapChangeGC..LCB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15631c0, 96 bytes): **established.**
  Backing-store wrapper for a context's change-GC entry. It finds the wrapper's private word through the index in the global at 0x173f728 (0x15631dc to 0x15631ec), stores it as the context's function table (+0x44), calls the table's entry at +4 (0x15631f4 to 0x156320c) with the context and change mask as received, and then puts the module's cheap wrapper table (the data word at 0x1738da0, gp - 0x1c20) back in +0x44 (0x1563210).
- `miBSCheapChangeClip..OCB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1593100, 96 bytes): **established.**
  The same unwrap-call-rewrap as the change-GC wrapper, using the original table's entry at +0x10 (0x1593134), the clip-change slot, and reinstalling the cheap table at +0x44 afterwards (0x1593150).
- `miBSCheapCopyClip..PCB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15cd620, 96 bytes): **established.**
  Restores the original function table for the duration of a clip copy: entry +0x18 of the saved table is called (0x15cd654) with the arguments untouched, then the cheap table goes back to +0x44 (0x15cd670).
- `miBSCheapDestroyClip..ADB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15fe880, 96 bytes): **established.**
  Calls entry +0x14 of the saved function table (0x15fe8b4), the destroy-clip slot, between taking the table into +0x44 and writing the cheap table back (0x15fe8d0).
- `miBSCheapCopyGC..MCB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x167ca60, 96 bytes): **established.**
  Differs from its siblings in where the context is: the third argument (a2) is the destination context, so the private word is read from its device-private array (0x167ca80) and its table entry +8 (0x167ca94) is called with the other arguments as they came; the cheap table returns to +0x44 of that context (0x167cab0).
- `miBSChangeGC..DFB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x169a510, 120 bytes): **established.**
  The full wrapper restore for change-GC. The private record (found through the index at 0x173f728) holds the saved function table at +0x14 and the saved operation table at +0x10; both go back into the context (+0x44, +0x48, 0x169a544 and 0x169a54c), the saved table's +4 entry runs (0x169a558), and afterwards the wrapper's own tables at 0x1738d28 and 0x1738d48 are reinstalled (0x169a570, 0x169a578).
- `miBSChangeClip..GFB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1668330, 120 bytes): **established.**
  Same shape as the change-GC restore with the saved table's +0x10 entry (0x166836c), the clip-change slot, between the two table swaps (0x1668360, 0x1668390).
- `miBSCopyClip..HFB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x162eae0, 120 bytes): **established.**
  The wrapper restore around the +0x18 entry (0x162eb20): saved function table and operation table into +0x44 and +0x48 first (0x162eb14, 0x162eb1c), the wrapper's pair (0x1738d28, 0x1738d48) back after the call (0x162eb40, 0x162eb48).
- `miBSCopyGC..EFB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1605f50, 120 bytes): **established.**
  Context is the third argument. It restores both saved tables into that context (0x1605f80, 0x1605f88), calls the saved table's +8 entry (0x1605f8c) and puts the wrapper's tables back (0x1605fb0, 0x1605fb8).
- `miBSDestroyClip..IFB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1593160, 120 bytes): **established.**
  Restores the saved function and operation tables from the private record (+0x14, +0x10) into the context, runs the saved table's destroy-clip entry at +0x14 (0x15931a0) and installs the wrapper tables at 0x1738d28 and 0x1738d48 again (0x15931c0, 0x15931c8).
- `nfbSolidFillRects` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x163c5f0, 116 bytes): **established.**
  Fills a list of rectangles with the context's colour (arguments: context, window, rectangles, count). It takes the frame-buffer procedure table through the window's private (slot `nfbWindowPrivateIndex`, 0x163c608 to 0x163c62c) and calls its entry at +4 (0x163c634 to 0x163c650) with the rectangles and count in the first two slots, the context private's words +0x40 (colour), +0x48 (raster operation byte) and +0x4c (plane mask),, and the window.
- `miDasin..EO` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1671e70, 120 bytes): **established.**
  Arc sine in degrees for the arc code. An argument of 0 returns 0, 1.0 returns 90.0 and -1.0 returns -90.0 without calling the C library (the constants at 0x172db10, 0x172de28, 0x1730d18 and 0x1730d28 hold 1.0, -1.0, 90.0 and -90.0); every other value calls `asin` (0x1671ec8) and multiplies the result by 57.29577951308232 at 0x1730d30.
- `miMarkWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1690a30, 108 bytes): **established.**
  Starts a window's validation data (public mi code, `mi/mivaltree.c` in the X distribution). A window whose word +0x44 is already set returns at once; otherwise it allocates 0x18 bytes with `XNFalloc` (0x1690a64), clears the words at +4 and +8, stores the window's x and y halfwords (+8, +0xa) at +0 and +2 of the record as the old corner and links the record into +0x44.
- `nfbListInstalledHWColormaps` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16b31b0, 112 bytes): **established.**
  Lists the colour maps held in hardware (arguments: screen, output array of ids; result: count). The screen's device record (+0x74) holds a slot count at +0x70 and 0x1c-byte slots from +0x74; each slot whose first word (the colour map) is nonzero contributes that map's id (map word +8) to the output and the count is returned (0x16b31e0 to 0x16b3210).
- `ValidateSwapNClip..IF` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16dd4d0, 96 bytes): **established.**
  Runs the swap-buffer validation and then the clip validation for the same arguments: it saves the four argument registers on the stack, calls `ValidateSwapbuf..GF` (0x168c370, 0x16dd500), reloads them and tail-calls `ValidateClip..CF` (0x16df630, 0x16dd528).
- `Unbind..FF` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15f4190, 108 bytes): **established.**
  Entry point for taking a window off a render node. A nonzero second argument only prints "Unbind is calling rrmOGLUnbindRNFromClip; shouldn't happen" (rodata 0x172eb58, 0x15f41b4) and returns; a zero one calls the screen's resource-manager record procedure at +0x44 (record through slot `rrmScreenPrivateIndex`, 0x15f41cc to 0x15f41ec) with the window and the third argument as its second.

## Audio-library converters and configuration setters, backing-store set-up and window-record helpers

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, read from a Ghidra decompilation with `$gp` resolved; strings and constants quoted below were checked in the listing or the file. All entries are **inferred** (decompilation only), and the converter loops are unrolled by two or four, so each entry gives the arithmetic of one sample. The audio converters take 24-bit samples held in 32-bit words (the library's internal format) and a converter record whose scale factors sit at fixed offsets.

- `xdr_typedData` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154fa54, 340 bytes): **inferred.**
  Encodes or decodes one typed value of the symbol database over an XDR stream (arguments: stream, type code, pointer to a count, pointer to the data, the database). A type code of 0xf or 0x13 with a zero count and a data pointer that is still null reads a string with `xdr_wrapstring` into a stack buffer, allocates `strlen + 1` bytes with the database's allocator function pointer (`_SDBmemface`), copies the string in, stores the new pointer and sets the count to 1. Every other type asks `mapTypeToXDRPROC` for the element routine and element size; with a zero count the routine runs once on the data and the count becomes 1, otherwise `xdr_array` transfers `count` elements of that size.
- `ALsetqueuesize` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154ffa0, 288 bytes): **inferred.**
  Sets the queue size of a configuration (magic 0xbb82 at +0). A size from 1 to 0x3fffb goes to +4 and bit 30 of the word at +0x24 is cleared (mask 0xbfffffff); anything outside gives `errno` 10 with the error hook and -1; a bad configuration gives `errno` 2.
- `alGetConfig` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1552740, 276 bytes): **inferred.**
  Returns a fresh copy of an open port's configuration (port magic 0xbb81, else `errno` 1). It allocates 0x28 bytes with `malloc`, copies 0x28 bytes from +0x30 of the port with `bcopy`, writes the configuration magic 0xbb82 over the first word and returns the copy; a failed `malloc` gives `errno` 6 and a null result.
- `alSetWidth` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1561cb0, 312 bytes): **inferred.**
  Sets the sample width of a configuration. The accepted values are 1, 2, 4, 0x20 and 0x40; others set `errno` 8 and return -1, a good value is stored at +8 and the result is 0.
- `alSetFloatMax` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1562320, 248 bytes): **inferred.**
  Stores the largest floating-point sample value of a configuration (arguments: a double in the first floating-point register and the configuration; magic 0xbb82 required, else `errno` 2). A value of 0.0 is refused with `errno` 0x16 (22); any other is stored as a double at +0x18.
- `_alGetResourceValue` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1562a00, 240 bytes): **inferred.**
  Turns a resource code into a device number. Code 9 reads the environment variable "AL_DEFAULT_IN" (0x17243f8) and code 10 "AL_DEFAULT_OUT" (0x1724408); when the variable is set it is looked up with `alGetResourceByName` (type 0xb, mask 0x20000002) and a result below 1 becomes 0. Any other code, or an unset variable, returns the code itself.
- `_AL2WriteMonoToStereo_8` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1553800, 264 bytes): **inferred.**
  Widens a mono run of signed 8-bit samples into stereo words (arguments: source bytes, destination words, sample count): every sample is shifted left by 16 and written twice, left then right, so the output has two words per input byte; an odd count is handled first, then pairs.
- `_AL2WriteMonoToStereo_16` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1553910, 264 bytes): **inferred.**
  The 16-bit twin: each signed halfword is shifted left by 8 (not 16) before it is written to both channel words of its frame, which puts 16-bit samples at the same 24-bit scale as the 8-bit routine.
- `_AL2WriteMonoToStereo_f` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1553ae0, 312 bytes): **inferred.**
  Converts mono floats to stereo words. Each float is multiplied by the converter record's scale at +0x90 (fourth argument), compared with the limits 8388607.0 and -8388608.0 (0x173f850, 0x173f854), clamped to them, truncated to an integer and stored in both channel words.
- `_AL2WriteMonoToStereo_d` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1553c20, 312 bytes): **inferred.**
  Same conversion for doubles: the scale is the double at +0xa8 of the converter record and the clamp limits are the doubles 8388607.0 and -8388608.0 at 0x173f750 and 0x173f758.
- `_AL2ReadMonoFromStereo_8` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1553d60, 376 bytes): **inferred.**
  Mixes stereo words down to signed bytes: the two channel words of a frame are added and shifted right by 17, which is their average scaled from 24 bits to 8 (the loop is unrolled by four).
- `_AL2ReadMonoFromStereo_16` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1553ee0, 376 bytes): **inferred.**
  The 16-bit mixdown: the channel words of each frame are added and shifted right by 9 and the low halfword is stored.
- `_AL2ReadMonoFromStereo_24` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1554060, 376 bytes): **inferred.**
  The 24-bit mixdown, with a shift of only 1: the sum of the two channel words halved, stored as a full word, with the same unrolled structure (single odd frame first, then runs of four).
- `_AL2Read4ChanFromStereo_d` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1558f40, 360 bytes): **inferred.**
  Expands stereo words to four-channel doubles (arguments: destination doubles, source words, frame count, converter record). Channel 0 is the left word times the scale at +0x98 of the record, channel 1 the right word times it, and channels 2 and 3 are 0.0, giving four doubles per frame.
- `_AL2WriteMonoToStereo_16v` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15600c0, 416 bytes): **inferred.**
  The strided form of the 16-bit mono widening (arguments: pointer to the source cursor, pointer to the stride in samples, destination, frame count). A null cursor fills the destination with zeros (`bzero` of count x 8 bytes); otherwise each sample is read at the running stride, shifted left by 8 and written to both channels, and the cursor is advanced by stride x count samples at the end.
- `_AL2WriteMonoToStereo_24v` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1560260, 372 bytes): **inferred.**
  Strided widening of 24-bit words: the same cursor, stride and null-cursor zero fill (`bzero`, count x 8) as the 16-bit routine, but the samples are copied to the two channel words unshifted.
- `cfbAllocatePrivates` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1568050, 272 bytes): **inferred.**
  Allocates the 8-bit colour layer's private slots. When the frame-buffer layer's window and context indexes (`nfbWindowPrivateIndex`, `nfbGCPrivateIndex`) are both still -1 it gets new ones from `mfbAllocatePrivates`, otherwise it takes the existing pair; both layers then share one pair of indexes. It asks for 0x10 bytes of window private and 0x14 of context private, points the copy-plane hook `cfbPuntCopyPlane` at `miCopyPlane` and returns 1, or 0 on any failure.
- `cfb16AllocatePrivates` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1596030, 300 bytes): **inferred.**
  The 16-bit layer's version of the same set-up, with the same 0x10 and 0x14 byte requests and the same copy-plane hook, followed by one more allocation: a screen private index stored in `cfb16ScreenPrivateIndex`, whose result decides success.
- `miInitializeBackingStore` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1576120, 328 bytes): **inferred.**
  Wraps a screen's backing-store hooks (argument: screen). On the first call of a server generation it allocates a screen private index and a context private index (the globals at 0x173f720 and 0x173f728); it then allocates a context private, makes a 0x1c-byte record of seven saved screen procedures (offsets +0x98, +0xa4, +0xa8, +0xc0, +0x130, +0xb8 and a pointer to `nfbBSFuncRec`) and replaces those slots, plus +0xf4 to +0x108, with the module's own backing-store routines (`miBSCloseScreen`, `miBSGetImage`, `miBSGetSpans`, `miBSChangeWindowAttributes`, `miBSCreateGC`, `miBSDestroyWindow` and the save, restore, exposure-copy, translate, clear and draw-guarantee set).
- `mfbPutImage` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x157e500, 396 bytes): **inferred.**
  Puts a one-bit image through the context's own copy routines (arguments include the context, the image, its width and height, the left padding and a format flag). It needs bit 0 of the context's word +0x14 set and a nonzero width; it makes a scratch pixmap header over the image data with `GetScratchPixmapHeader` (row length rounded up to 32 bits), clears bit 7 of the context's private word for the call, runs the copy-area entry at +0xc (format flag nonzero) or the copy-plane entry at +0x10 (zero, with a trailing 1) of the context's operation table, sets the bit again and frees the header with `FreeScratchPixmapHeader`.
- `nfbRealizeWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x157e690, 372 bytes): **inferred.**
  Updates the frame-buffer layer's bookkeeping when a window is mapped. A window with bit 10 of word +0x7c set raises the count in the screen's table (from device record +0x40, indexed by the window's layer at +0x18). A window with a parent calls `nfbSetDidFlags` and, when the layers of window and parent differ, raises the child counter at +0x1c of every ancestor's private; the result is 1. A root window instead sets the bit of its layer in word +0x24 of its private, builds the screen's box (0, 0, width, height from halfwords +8 and +0xa), calls the screen's region slot +0x174 (by the public screen record's order, the region-reset procedure) for the layer's 12-byte region and runs `nfbInitRootWindow`.
- `Fullscreen..KF` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x158a1c0, 364 bytes): **inferred.**
  Sets or clears the full-screen mark on a window's resource-manager record (arguments: window, an address from an OpenGL caller, an unused third, a flag). A nonzero second argument prints "Fullscreen shouldn't be called by OGL:window ..." (rodata 0x172ecb8) with the window id, flags and addresses. A record without bit 0 (not bound) prints "Xsgi: attempt to do fullscreen on unmanaged window: 0x%x" (0x172ed18); otherwise bit 1 of the record's flag halfword is set or cleared by the flag, or, when the record has a child array at +0x28, in each of up to four child windows' records.
- `Dontfreeze..JF` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15cb1e0, 364 bytes): **inferred.**
  The same walk as the full-screen routine for the freeze-avoidance mark: its message reads "Dontfreeze: Shouldn't be called by OGL window ..." (rodata 0x172ec18) and the bit it sets or clears is bit 2 (value 4) of the record's flag halfword, in the record itself or in each of up to four child records.
- `cfb16SegmentSS1Rect` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15a0550, 332 bytes): **inferred.**
  Draws single-pixel segments of one clip rectangle with the 16-bit layer (arguments: drawable, context, segment count, segment array). The raster-operation byte at the start of the context's colour-layer private selects a pair: copy (3) takes `cfb16SegmentSS1RectCopy` with `cfb16ClippedLineCopy`, xor (6) the xor pair and everything else the general pair. The fast routine draws as many segments as it can; when it returns a count other than -1 the next segment (8 bytes of four halfwords) goes through the clipped-line routine with the private's colour word at +8 and a flag from bits 29:28 of the context word +0x10 being 0, and the loop continues with the remaining count.
- `miBSPutImage..FDB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15aee70, 292 bytes): **inferred.**
  Backing-store wrapper for put-image on a window with a backing pixmap (arguments: drawable, context and the image arguments). It saves the context's tables, takes the saved ones from the wrapper's private record (+0x10, +0x14), calls the saved entry at +8, and then runs the same entry of the context's operation table (at +0x48, entry +8) on the backing pixmap with the drawing origin moved by the window's halfwords +4 and +6 of the backing record; afterwards it stores the changed operation pointer back into the record and reinstalls the wrapper tables.
- `miSendGraphicsExpose` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15c9860, 352 bytes): **inferred.**
  Sends graphics-expose or no-expose events to a client (arguments: client, region, drawable id, major opcode, minor opcode). With no region or an empty one it sends one event of type 0xe (NoExpose) carrying the drawable, minor and major opcodes. Otherwise it builds one 32-byte event of type 0xd (GraphicsExpose) per rectangle, with the box's position and size, the count of events still to follow counting down (halfword +0x12) and the opcodes, in a stack array, and hands them all to `TryClientEvents` in one call.

## Two-dimensional drawing routines read from whole listings

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`. These routines send their words through the board's command FIFO pointer, which the module keeps in word +0 of the board record (the record behind `odsyScreenPrivateIndex`); every store goes through that pointer, so no word of them is a store to a fixed address. The Ghidra decompilation was made with `MarkVolatileWindows.java` and its FIFO stores were counted against the listing (an assembly pass over every `sw` through a pointer loaded from the board record found the same number). In these routines "the sync" means the four words 0x10443, 0xfa, 0x10046, 0x10046, which the routine sends only when the command-state record's word +0x1cb8 is set (clearing it) in the guarded places and unconditionally (also clearing it) in the reset runs, and "the long sync" the six words 0x10443, 0x96 and four of 0x10046.

- `odsyDrawSmallCI8Image..I` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16380f0, 6860 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end; the 382 stores through the FIFO pointer in the decompilation match the 382 in the listing, and the mode-switch table at 0x172ab98 was read from the file).
  Draws a box of 8-bit pixel values one pixel at a time through the command FIFO (arguments: the box as four halfwords x0, y0, x1, y1; the source bytes; the source row pitch; the raster-operation code 0 to 15; the plane mask; the window). It clears word +0x84 of the command-state record, finds the window's layer record (0x44-byte entries at +0x4c of the board record, indexed by the nfb window private's word +0x3c minus the device record's first id at +0x50) and keeps its words +8, +0xc and +0x10 for the format word. The double-buffer private's signed byte +1 picks a buffer mask: 3 for 0xfe or 0, 0xc for 0xfd or 1, 0xf for 0xff and 3 otherwise (0x16381a0 to 0x16381f0); a layer whose mode word (nfb window private +0x14) is 7 always uses 0xc.
  Window descriptor. When the board record's window box (halfwords +0x60 to +0x66) differs from the box last sent (+0x6c to +0x72), it copies the box over the cache and sends 0x1204b with the eight words at +0x2b40 to +0x2b5c of the command-state record: x0 shifted left 12 with y0 in the low 12 bits, x1 minus 1 and y1 minus 1 packed the same way, then 0xffffff and 0 three times (preceded by the sync when +0x1cb8 is set; 0x16381f4 to 0x1638238 and the block after it).
  Buffer mask. When the mask is not the word cached at +0x2b68, it stores it there and sends a reset run: the sync, 0x10019, the long sync, the sync, 0x10424 with the mask, the long sync, two syncs, 0x10019, the long sync, the sync, the four-word form 0x11017 with the board record's words +0x1b24, +0x1b28, +0x1b2c and +0x1b30, the long sync, the sync, two more 0x11017 forms (the first with the third word replaced by 0, the second with the third word ORed with 0x800000), the sync, 0x10019, the long sync, the sync, a plain 0x11017 form, the long sync and the sync; it then sets the halfword +0x68 of the FIFO-pointer record to 1 and +0x6a to 0.
  Pixel-mode switch. Word +0x84 of the command-state record gets bit 15 from `enableStencilTest`, and the window's mode number (0 to 11; any larger value prints "Unrecognized mode: 0x%x" with `ErrorF` and sends nothing of this block) selects one of nine blocks through the table at 0x172ab98 (0x163876c): modes 0, 1 and 7 the block at 0x1638bb0 (word +0x84 gets 0x80000), modes 8, 9, 10 and 11 the blocks 0x16391d8, 0x1639064, 0x1638ef0 and 0x1638d78 (0x100000), and modes 2 to 6 the blocks 0x1639974, 0x16397f4, 0x1639674, 0x16394ec and 0x163934c (0x100000). Every block sends 0x10404 with word +0x84 (after the sync when pending), then the program words: for modes 0, 1 and 7 to 11 the words 0x22008001 and 0x23008003 (skipped when the cached words at +0x2664 and +0x2668 already hold 1 and 3), then 0xce80cc08, three zeros, 0xc580c406 and a zero; for modes 2 to 6 the words 0x22008000 and 0x23008002 (cached as 0 and 2), then 0xc580cc08 (0xc580cc09 for modes 5 and 6), three zeros and no second block. It then sends the plane mask as 0x11023 with the cached words +0xe8, +0xec and the constant words +0xf0 and +0xf4, when the packed mask differs from the cache. The packing of the mask word by mode is: modes 0, 1, 7, 10 and 11 the mask shifted left 12 with 1 in the second word; modes 8 and 9 the mask shifted left 16 with 1; mode 2 bits 3:0 to 23:20 and 7:4 to 11:8, second word bits 11:8 to 23:20 with 0xf00; mode 3 bits 4:0 to 23:19 and 9:5 to 11:7, second word bits 14:10 to 23:19 with 0x800; mode 4 the mask's bits 7:0 to 23:16 and 15:8 to 11:4, second word bits 23:16 with 0xff0; mode 5 bits 9:0 to 23:14 and 19:10 to 11:2, second word bits 29:20 to 23:14 with 0xc00; mode 6 bits 9:0 to 23:14, 19:10 to 11:2 and 9:8 to 13:12, second word bits 29:20 to 23:14 and, again, bits 29:28 to 13:12 with 0xfff. The field meanings behind these layouts are inferred from the shift counts alone.
  Format word. For mode 7 the first format word is the board record's word +8 and the second 0; otherwise it is word +4 and the low 3 bits of word +0x10 shifted left 13. The second is ORed with 0x20000000 when the raster operation is 0, 3, 12 or 15, with the layer word +0xc masked to 3 bits shifted left 10, with 1 or 0 shifted left 19 (1 for modes 0, 1 and 7), with 0x400000 when the layer word +0xc is 3, and with the layer words +0x10 (3 bits, shifted left 4) and +8 (3 bits, shifted left 7). The operation is stored at +0xc8 and sent as 0x10422. When the halfword +0x6a is set, or either format word differs from the cache at +0x1b24 and +0x1b2c, it stores them there and sends the sync, 0x10019, the long sync, the sync, a 0x11017 form with words +0x1b24 to +0x1b30, the long sync and the sync, then clears +0x6a and +0x68.
  Pixels. It sends 0xce80cc08, three zeros, 0x14400 and a zero, then for every pixel the five words 0xc580c406, the source byte, 0x8080c800, x and y (loop at 0x1638ab0 to 0x1638aec; the source pointer advances by the pitch at the end of each row), and finally 0x14001. When the board record's word +0x1b7c is zero it only sets +0x1cb8 and the FIFO record's halfword +0x68 to 1; otherwise it first sends the sync (0x1638b2c to 0x1638b98).
- `odsyDrawPoints` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x169a590, 6884 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end; the 376 stores through the FIFO pointer in the decompilation match the 376 in the listing, and the mode-switch table at 0x172ac60 was read from the file).
  Draws single pixels in one colour (arguments: an array of points as pairs of halfwords, the point count, the pixel value, the raster-operation code 0 to 15, the plane mask, two arguments it does not use, and the window as the eighth). It starts like `odsyDrawSmallCI8Image..I`: it clears word +0x84 of the command-state record, finds the layer record and its words +8, +0xc and +0x10, refreshes the 0x1204b window descriptor when the board record's window box differs from the box last sent, and resends the buffer mask (here 0xc when the double-buffer private's byte +1 is 1 and for a layer whose mode word is 7, otherwise 3) with the same reset run. The text of that reset run in the two decompilations differs only in the order of three stores.
  The pixel-mode switch (table at 0x172ac60, mode numbers as in that entry; a mode above 11 prints "Unrecognized mode: 0x%x") sends 0x10404 with word +0x84, the program words and the colour. Modes 0, 1 and 7 (block 0x169afb4) use the program 0xce80cc08, three zeros and 0xc580c406 followed by the pixel value unchanged, with the mask shifted left 12 in the 0x11023 descriptor; modes 10 and 11 (0x169b2f4, 0x169b178) the same with the 0x100000 flag; modes 8 and 9 (0x169b5f4, 0x169b470) send the pixel value shifted left 4 and the mask shifted left 16. The other modes use the program 0x22008000, 0x23008002 and 0xc580cc08 (0xc580cc09 for modes 5 and 6) followed by three colour words made from the pixel value: mode 2 (0x169be18) (v and 0xf) shifted left 4, v and 0xf0, (v shifted right 4) and 0xf0; mode 3 (0x169bc80) (v and 0x1f) shifted left 3, (v shifted right 2) and 0xf8, (v shifted right 7) and 0xf8; mode 4 (0x169baec) the three bytes of v; mode 5 (0x169b94c) (v and 0x3ff) shifted left 6, (v shifted right 4) and 0xffc0, (v shifted right 14) and 0xffc0; mode 6 (0x169b778) the same three, each also getting the top two bits of its 10-bit field (pixel bits 9:8, 19:18 and 29:28) at bits 5:4. The plane mask goes out in the 0x11023 descriptor packed exactly as in `odsyDrawSmallCI8Image..I`. The format word, the cache at +0x1b24 and +0x1b2c, 0x10422 with the raster operation and the sync follow the same code.
  It then sends 0x14400 and a zero and, for every point, the three words 0x8080c800, x plus the window's x origin (halfword +8) and y plus its y origin (+10), then 0x14001 and the same closing sync test on +0x1b7c as the image routine (0x169aeb0 to 0x169af84).

## Integer line and point placement (software expectation)

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, listing read around the cited addresses; the stores through the FIFO pointer were listed with `scripts/fifo_words.py --all-stores`. Entries are **inferred**: the instructions are established, the board behaviour they presuppose is not shown by any frame.

- `odsyDrawPoints` (described above) draws each point as one integer vertex word `0x8080c800` followed by x plus the window's x origin and y plus its y origin, with no fraction added. The server therefore expects pixel (x, y) to be the pixel lit, so that integer vertex words name pixel positions with the pixel's upper-left corner at the integer.
- `odsyLineSS` (0x15d5570, 8484 bytes) sends a polyline as primitive type 3 (line strip, `0x14400` then 3) of such integer vertices (stores at 0x15d6200 to 0x15d6260 for the first point, 0x15d6274 to 0x15d62b4 and 0x15d637c for the rest). After the strip and before `0x14001` it sends one more vertex (0x15d5e10 to 0x15d5e40: the word `0x8080c800`, then a point's x plus the origin plus 1, and its y plus the origin) when two tests pass: bits 29:28 of the word at +16 of the graphics-context argument are nonzero (0x15d5dd0 to 0x15d5ddc; read here as the cap style, **inferred**) and the last two points differ in x or y (0x15d5de4 to 0x15d5e08). Which of the two points the extra vertex copies is not settled by the listing (the pointer arithmetic at 0x15d5e0c names the point at offset -4 from a register that is also compared against offset 0). **inferred** reading: the board's line rule leaves the last pixel of a strip unlit, so the server adds a one-pixel segment to light it when the cap style asks for the end point; the zero-length case is left out because it would add nothing.
- Consequence for a model, **inferred**: for integer vertices a horizontal line at integer y lights row y and a vertical line at integer x lights column x, which is not the exact-edge rule the vendor simulation frames show for float vertices (an exact minor-axis edge goes to the lower pixel there; no shipped frame draws integer vertex words). The server cannot have been built against a one-pixel shift of every 2D line, so the integer vertex path must differ from the float path at pixel edges. **unknown:** whether the board adds a bias to integer words or applies another tie rule.

## Region test, audio mixdown and sample converters read from whole listings

- `miPointInRegion` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1683780, 356 bytes): **established.**
  Tests whether a point lies in a region and, when it does, copies the rectangle that holds it to a box (arguments: region, x, y, box; result 1 when the point is inside, 0 otherwise, with the box written only on 1). The region record holds four halfwords for its extents, x1 at +0, y1 at +2, x2 at +4 and y2 at +6, and the word at +8 is a pointer to a rectangle list. That list's count is the word at +4 of the pointed-to block, and its rectangles follow at +8, each eight bytes of four halfwords (x1, y1, x2, y2). The box is four halfwords at +0, +2, +4 and +6. The routine makes no calls and reads no global.
  The point test uses the extents alone when the list pointer is null: x must satisfy x1 <= x < x2 and y must satisfy y1 <= y < y2 (the branches at 0x1683808, 0x1683814, 0x168381c and 0x1683824), and the extents are written to the box (0x16838bc to 0x16838e0) when it passes. With a list, a count of zero returns 0 at 0x1683794, the same extents test runs first (0x16837b0 to 0x16837d4), and a count of one copies the extents to the box (the test at 0x16837dc).
  With two or more rectangles the list is scanned from its first entry (the loop at 0x1683848). A rectangle with y at or past its y2 (0x1683864) or x at or past its x2 (0x1683880) is skipped. A point above a rectangle's y1 returns 0 at once (0x1683870), and so does a point left of its x1 (0x1683878), because the list is in bands. The first rectangle that holds the point is copied to the box: x1, y1, x2 and y2 (the stores at 0x1683888 to 0x16838ac), and the routine returns 1. A scan that passes the last rectangle returns 0 (0x16838b0).
- `_AL2Write4ChanTo2Chan_24` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1558630, 436 bytes): **established.**
  Mixes four channels to two (arguments: source frames, destination, frame count). A source frame is four 32-bit words, 16 bytes, and an output frame is two 32-bit words, 8 bytes. The left output is word 0 plus word 2 of the source frame and the right output is word 1 plus word 3. Each sum is clamped to the 24-bit signed range: a value above 0x7fffff is stored as 0x7fffff (the constant built at 0x1558658 and 0x1558668) and a value below -0x800000 as -0x800000 (the constant built at 0x1558638 as 0xff800000). Any other sum is stored as it is. The routine returns at once when the count is zero or less (the test at 0x1558634, the return at 0x15586c0).
  An odd count converts one frame first (the test at 0x1558664, the frame at 0x155866c to 0x1558698, the stores around 0x15586a0 and 0x15586a4). The remaining frames are converted two per pass (the loop from 0x15586dc, 32 bytes of source and 16 bytes of output per pass), up to the end pointer at the source start plus 16 times the count (0x155865c), and the loop exits at 0x15586d4. The routine makes no calls and no branch-likely instruction, and it saves s0, s1, s2 and ra on the stack.
- `_AL2WriteNToN_f` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1555e20, 460 bytes): **established.**
  Converts single-precision samples to 24-bit words, one word per sample, with no mixing (arguments: source samples, destination, count, record; the record in the fourth argument, a3, supplies the gain and the shift). The gain is the float at +144 of that record. The shift is the word at +128 minus one: t1 = count << (word at +128 - 1), and the routine converts 2 * t1 samples, 4 bytes in and 4 bytes out each, from the end pointer at source plus 8 * t1 (the sllv at 0x1555e38 and the sll at 0x1555e58).
  Each scaled sample is compared against two floats in the module's constant pool, at 0x173f850 (8388607.0) and 0x173f854 (-8388608.0), reached through the routine's own t9 (the constants are at t9 + 0x1e4ba0 + 20112 and + 20116). A sample above 8388607.0 is stored as 0x7fffff (a6, built at 0x1555e28 and 0x1555e3c), a sample below -8388608.0 is stored as -0x800000 (a7, built at 0x1555e24), and any other sample is truncated toward zero with trunc.w.s and stored. A NaN fails both compares and goes to the truncation, which the body does not guard.
  When t1 is odd the first two samples are converted singly (0x1555e60 to 0x1555fc0); the rest runs in passes of four samples (0x1555eb0 to 0x1555f6c). With even t1 the passes start at once. The pass branch at 0x1555e90 is branch-likely: the load in its delay slot at 0x1555e94 runs only when the branch is taken, so with a zero pass count the routine returns at 0x1555e98. The routine makes no calls.
- `_AL2WriteNToN_d` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1556290, 460 bytes): **established.**
  The double-precision form of `_AL2WriteNToN_f`, with the same count, shift, store and clamp structure. The source samples are doubles of eight bytes, so the end pointer is the source plus 16 * t1 and a pass of four samples advances the source by 32 bytes (the first load at 0x15562d8 and the 32-byte advance within the pass). The gain is the double at +168 of the record (ldc1 at 0x15562b4), and the constant base is t9 + 0x1e4730 = 0x173a9c0, set at 0x155629c and 0x15562a4. The clamp constants are the doubles at 0x173f750 (8388607.0, bytes 0x415fffffc0000000) and 0x173f758 (-8388608.0, bytes 0xc160000000000000), compared with c.lt.d and truncated with trunc.w.d. The stores are the same 32-bit words as the float form, the same odd-count prologue and the same branch-likely at the pass entry, and the routine makes no calls.

## Audio mixdown from 8-bit, 16-bit, float and double frames

- `_AL2Write4ChanTo2Chan_8` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1558270, 468 bytes): **established.**
  Mixes four channels to two for 8-bit samples (arguments: source frames, destination, frame count). A source frame is four signed bytes and an output frame is two 32-bit words, 8 bytes. The left output is channel 0 plus channel 2 and the right output is channel 1 plus channel 3. Each sum is shifted left 16 bits, so a sum lands in bits 23:16 of the word. A shifted sum above 0x7fffff is stored as 0x7fffff (the constant built at 0x155829c and 0x15582ac) and a shifted sum below 0xff800000 as 0xff800000 (built at 0x1558278). Those limits fall on sums of 128 or more and of -129 or less; any other sum is stored as it is.
  The routine returns at once when the count is zero or less (the test at 0x1558274, the return at 0x1558310). An odd count converts the first frame at entry (0x15582b0 to 0x15582f4, the branch at 0x15582a8), and the rest runs in passes of two frames: 8 source bytes and 16 output bytes per pass, from 0x155832c. The loop ends when the source pointer reaches the end pointer (the test at 0x1558324). The branches are all plain conditional branches, and the routine makes no calls.
- `_AL2Write4ChanTo2Chan_16` (0x1558450, 468 bytes): **established.**
  The 16-bit form of `_AL2Write4ChanTo2Chan_8`, with the same arguments and clamp. A source frame is four signed halfwords, 8 bytes, and an output frame is still two words. Each sum is shifted left 8 bits instead of 16, so the clamps fall on sums of 32768 or more and of -32769 or less. The odd-count frame is converted first (0x1558490 to 0x15584d4), and the pass body from 0x1558510 converts two frames, 16 source bytes and 16 output bytes per pass (two output frames of two words each). The pass entry at 0x15584d8 is branch-likely (`bnezl`): the halfword load in its delay slot runs only when the branch is taken, which is when at least one pass remains. The loop exit is the test at 0x1558504 and the return at 0x15584f4. The routine makes no calls.
- `_AL2Write4ChanTo2Chan_f` (0x15587f0, 532 bytes): **established.**
  Mixes four channels to two for single-precision samples (arguments: source frames, destination, frame count, record). A source frame is four floats, 16 bytes, and an output frame is two words. The left sum is the float at offset 0 plus the one at offset 8, and the right sum is the one at 4 plus the one at 12. The sum is formed first (add.s) and then multiplied by the gain, the float at +144 of the record (loaded at 0x1558800), with mul.s. The scaled value is compared with the float constants at 0x173f850 (8388607.0) and 0x173f854 (-8388608.0), reached through the routine's own t9 (t9 plus 0x1e21d0 plus 20112 and 20116): a value above 8388607.0 is stored as 0x7fffff (a6), a value below -8388608.0 as 0xff800000 (a7), and any other value as its truncation toward zero (trunc.w.s, stored with swc1). A NaN fails both compares and goes to the truncation, which the body does not guard.
  The routine returns at once when the count is zero or less (the branch at 0x1558804 to the return at 0x1558860). An odd count converts the first frame at entry (0x1558828 to 0x1558854, with its right half at 0x15589bc to 0x15589fc, which also steps the source by 16). The pairs run from 0x1558880: two frames per pass, 32 source bytes and 16 output bytes, the source pointer advanced by 32 at 0x155896c. The exit compares the source pointer with the end pointer (source plus 16 times the count, computed at 0x1558818 and 0x1558828) at 0x1558878. The routine makes no calls and no branch-likely instruction.
- `_AL2Write4ChanTo2Chan_d` (0x1558a10, 532 bytes): **established.**
  The double-precision form of `_AL2Write4ChanTo2Chan_f`, with the same structure, clamps and truncation. A source frame is four doubles, 32 bytes: the left sum is the double at offset 0 plus the one at 16, and the right sum is the one at 8 plus the one at 24. The gain is the double at +168 of the record (ldc1 at 0x1558a20). The constants are the doubles at 0x173f750 (8388607.0, 0x415fffffc0000000) and 0x173f758 (-8388608.0, 0xc160000000000000), reached through t9 plus 0x1e1fb0 plus 19856 and 19864, and compared with c.lt.d. Values are truncated with trunc.w.d. The odd frame is converted first and the pairs run from 0x1558aa0, 64 source bytes and 16 output bytes per pass, with the end pointer at source plus 32 times the count (0x1558a38 and 0x1558a48). The loop exit is at 0x1558a98. The routine makes no calls and no branch-likely instruction.
- `_AL2Read4ChanFromStereo_f` (0x1558d80, 448 bytes): **established.**
  Spreads a stereo frame over four float channels, the reverse of the mixdown (arguments: destination in a0, source frames in a1, frame count in a2, record in a3; the destination is the first argument here, unlike the mixdown routines). A source frame is two signed 32-bit words, 8 bytes, and an output frame is four floats, 16 bytes. The output is left times the gain, right times the gain, then two zero words. The gain is the float at +136 of the record (loaded at 0x1558d8c, multiplied with mul.s). Each integer is converted with cvt.s.w and not clamped. The routine returns at once when the count is zero or less (the test at 0x1558d88). A count below three runs a one-frame loop from 0x1558efc, which ends at 0x1558f30. Three or more frames run an unrolled loop of three frames per pass (its branch back is at 0x1558e8c, the source pointer advanced by 24 bytes at 0x1558e74) followed by a tail from 0x1558e94 to 0x1558ed4 that writes the last output frame and its zero words. The per-frame stores in the unrolled loop match the one-frame loop, and the routine makes no calls. The loop is software-pipelined: each of the three blocks in a pass stores one frame and converts the next frame's words, with early exits to the tail at 0x1558ef0 (after the first block, 0x1558e14), 0x1558ee0 (after the second, 0x1558e50) and by falling out at 0x1558e94 (after the third), all of which join at 0x1558ea0 to convert and store the final frame. Every count from 0 to 15 was run through the listing in a small interpreter (the instructions of the routine only, random signed words, gain 0.5): the stores were exactly 4 words per frame, each frame left times gain, right times gain, 0.0, 0.0, and nothing else was written.

## Degree trigonometry, octant angle and the ellipse tail bound read from whole listings

Each routine loads its constants from the table whose base is 0x172acc0 (the value of the slot at gp-28680, `$gp` = 0x173a9c0). The doubles are at that base plus the offset named, so 24664 is 0x1730d18 and holds 90.0. The GOT names are resolved with `scripts/resolve_got.py`.

- `miDsin..DO` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16ae8e0, 284 bytes): **established.**
  Sine of an angle given in degrees. It divides the argument by 90.0 (the double at pool offset 24664) and calls `floor` (the GOT slot at gp-28292) on the quotient at 0x16ae900. When the floor is not equal to the quotient (the `c.eq.d` at 0x16ae908 is false, and a NaN is false too), it tail-jumps to `sin` (GOT slot gp-28296) with the argument times pi (the double at pool offset 24672) divided by 180.0 (pool offset 7392). When the quotient is a whole number, the integer part n (from `trunc.w.d`) is reduced to an index: for n of zero or more the index is n and 3, for n below zero it is 4 minus the low two bits of -n. Index 0 returns 0.0 (0x16ae9cc), index 1 returns the double at pool offset 11856 (1.0), index 2 returns 0.0 (0x16ae9ec), and index 3 returns the double at pool offset 12648 (-1.0). Index 4, which occurs for a negative whole quotient whose magnitude is a multiple of 4 (for example -360 degrees), takes the `sin` path as well.
  Frame: 32 bytes, with the argument saved at sp+0, the caller's `$f20` saved at sp+8 and the return address at sp+16. The quotient is kept in `$f20` across the `floor` call (not in memory).

- `miDcos..CO` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x165eea0, 280 bytes): **established.**
  Cosine of an angle given in degrees, with the same structure as `miDsin..DO`: divide by 90.0 (pool offset 24664), `floor` on the quotient (the GOT load at 0x165eeb4 and the call at 0x165eebc), and a non-whole quotient or NaN tail-jumps to `cos` (GOT slot gp-28300) with the argument times pi over 180.0 (pool offsets 24672 and 7392). For a whole quotient the index is computed the same way (n and 3 for n of zero or more, 4 minus the low two bits of -n for n below zero). Index 0 returns the double at pool offset 11856 (1.0, through the branch at 0x165ef38), index 1 returns 0.0 (0x165efa0), index 2 returns the double at pool offset 12648 (-1.0, the `ldc1` in the delay slot at 0x165ef44 before the branch at 0x165efb0), index 3 returns 0.0 (the register loaded with zero at 0x165eed8), and index 4 takes the `cos` path (0x165ef74).

- `miDatan2..FO` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16ba850, 268 bytes): **established.**
  Angle in degrees of the point with y in the first argument (`$f12`) and x in the second (`$f13`), returning atan2(y, x) times 180/pi. For y equal to 0.0 it returns 0.0, or 180.0 (pool offset 7392) when x is below zero (`movf.d` on the test at fcc5). For x equal to 0.0 and y not zero it returns 90.0 (the double at pool offset 24664) when y is above zero and -90.0 (pool offset 24680) otherwise. When |y| equals |x| (the `c.eq.d` at 0x16ba8fc sets the condition tested by the branch at 0x16ba904) it returns one of four octant values: 45.0 (pool offset 24696) or 135.0 (pool offset 24704) for y above zero, by the sign of x, and 315.0 (pool offset 24712) or 225.0 (pool offset 24720) for y at or below zero. The sign of x comes from the single at pool offset 13512 from the table pointer at gp-32628, which is 1.0 (the first word of the `.rodata` section at 0x17234c8): the routine builds plus or minus that value from the sign of x and compares it with the constant, so the octant branch tests x above zero. In every other case it calls `atan2` (GOT slot gp-27876) with the same two arguments and returns the result times 57.29577951308232 (pool offset 24688).

- `tailEllipseY..BP` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x163cad0, 232 bytes): **established.**
  Computes one bound value for a record. The first argument points to three doubles P0 at +0, P1 at +8 and T at +16; the second argument points to a record whose double at +0 is the result, +8 is A and +40 is D. The result word at +0 is first set to 0.0 (0x163caf0). It stays 0.0 when P0 equals P1 (the branch at 0x163caf4), when P1 is below P0 and T times P0 is below A (the compare at 0x163cb08, branch at 0x163cb0c), or when P0 is below P1 and A is below T times P0 (the compare at 0x163cb20, branch at 0x163cb24). The test is on the product T times P0 in both cases (the `mul.d` in the delay slot at 0x163cb04 runs whether or not the branch is taken). Otherwise the routine computes M = P1 times T times P0, multiplies M by 2.0 (pool offset 1808) and squares that product (the `mul.d` in the delay slot of the call), so `cbrt` (GOT slot gp-28544) receives 4M squared. It then forms A times 2^(2/3) minus that cube root, with 1.5874010519681994 at pool offset 24752 and the `msub.d` at 0x163cb58, and divides by D. When the quotient is above zero (the compare at 0x163cb68) it takes `sqrt` of the quotient (GOT slot gp-28540) and stores that times P1 divided by 2^(1/3) (the constant 1.2599210498948732 at pool offset 24760) into the result word at 0x163cba8. Otherwise the result stays 0.0. A NaN quotient fails the test and leaves 0.0.
  Frame: 32 bytes, with P0's record address at sp+0 and the return address at sp+16.

## Tile fills, glyph set-up and private allocation read from whole listings

Each routine below was read from its full listing. The listings were dumped with `objdump -z`, because the plain dump elides runs of zero words (nops) with `...`. `$gp` is 0x173a9c0 in every routine here. The GOT slot names come from the `gp` offsets in the module's global offset table.

- `cfb16FillRectTileOdd` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x162ec90, 208 bytes): **established.**
  Picks one of four fill routines and tail-calls it. The first argument (a0) is the drawable, whose halfwords at +8 and +10 are its origin; the second (a1) is the graphics context; the third and fourth (a2, a3) are passed on. The routine saves gp and ra in a 16-byte frame (gp at sp+0, ra at sp+8) and returns the callee's v0 unchanged.
  The choice uses the halfword at +12 of the word at +32 of the context (t8) and the byte at +5 of the context (a6): the bit 0 test is at 0x162eca0 to 0x162ecb4, and the low halfword of the word at +20 (a5, `andi` at 0x162eccc) is compared with 0xffff at 0x162ecd0 and 0x162ed18.
  Bit 0 set and a5 not 0xffff: `cfb16FillBoxTileOddGeneral` (GOT slot gp-31152, loaded at 0x162ecd8). Bit 0 set and a5 0xffff: `cfb16FillBoxTileOddCopy` (gp-31076, loaded at 0x162ed2c) when a6 is 3, else the General routine (the `movz` at 0x162ed44).
  Bit 0 clear and a5 not 0xffff: `cfb16FillBoxTile32sGeneral` (gp-29040, loaded at 0x162ed20). Bit 0 clear and a5 0xffff: `cfb16FillBoxTile32sCopy` (gp-29784, loaded at 0x162ed48) when a6 is 3, else the 32s General routine (the `movz` at 0x162ed5c).
  The callee receives a0 unchanged, a1 = the third argument, a2 = the fourth argument, a3 = the word at +32 of the context, a4 = the drawable's halfword at +8 plus the context's halfword at +40, a5 = the drawable's halfword at +10 plus the context's halfword at +42, a6 = the byte at +5 and a7 = the word at +20.

- `cfbFillRectTileOdd` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1722d20, 208 bytes): **established.**
  The 8-bit form of `cfb16FillRectTileOdd`, with the same arguments, frame, call and return. The test uses the low two bits of the halfword at +12 of the word at +32 of the context (`andi` 3 at 0x1722d44) and tests the low byte of the word at +20 (`andi` 0xff at 0x1722d5c) against 255 (t0).
  Low bits not both zero and the byte not 255: `cfbFillBoxTileOddGeneral` (GOT slot gp-30096, loaded at 0x1722d68). Low bits not both zero and the byte 255: `cfbFillBoxTileOddCopy` (gp-29236, loaded at 0x1722dbc) when a6 is 3, else the General routine (`movz` at 0x1722dd4).
  Low bits both zero and the byte not 255: `cfbFillBoxTile32sGeneral` (gp-31520, loaded at 0x1722db0). Low bits both zero and the byte 255: `cfbFillBoxTile32sCopy` (gp-28988, loaded at 0x1722dd8) when a6 is 3, else the 32s General routine (`movz` at 0x1722dec).

- `mfbAllocatePrivates` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x167cd40, 236 bytes): **established.**
  Allocates the window and graphics-context private indexes of a screen and their private records (arguments: a0 the screen, a1 a pointer that receives the window index or is null, a2 a pointer that receives the GC index or is null). The routine saves s0, s1, s2 and ra in a 32-byte frame.
  The serverGeneration word (read through the GOT slot gp-28012 and then dereferenced at 0x167cd50) is compared at 0x167cd68 with the word at gp+19320. When they differ, the routine calls `AllocateWindowPrivateIndex` (GOT gp-27700) and stores its result at gp+19316 and `AllocateGCPrivateIndex` (gp-27708) and stores its result at gp+19788 and gp+19308, copies the serverGeneration word into gp+19320 (the store in the last delay slot at 0x167ce28), and rejoins the main path. The slow path passes a0 unchanged, the screen.
  Main path: when a1 is not null, `*a1` is set to the word at gp+19316 (the store in the delay slot at 0x167cd7c). When a2 is not null, `*a2` is set to the word at gp+19308 (0x167cd8c to 0x167cd90).
  It then calls `AllocateWindowPrivate` (gp-27704) with the screen, the window index (gp+19316, loaded at 0x167cd74 whether or not a1 was null) and 16 as the size. A zero result returns 0 (`move v0,zero` at 0x167cde8). Otherwise it calls `AllocateGCPrivate` (gp-27712) with the screen, the GC index (gp+19308) and 16. A zero result returns 0; a nonzero result returns 1 (`li v0,1` at 0x167cdcc).
  The routine makes no check on the two index calls. The listing ends at the store at 0x167ce28, and the nop at 0x167ce2c is padding.

- `cfb32ImageGlyphBlt8` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1623c80, 332 bytes): **established.**
  Glyph set-up for a 32-bit drawable: it fills a 40-byte block on the stack and then calls three routines through the context's function tables. Arguments: a0 the drawable pointer (kept in s2), a1 the context (s0), a2 the x coordinate (s3), a3 the y coordinate (s1), a4 (s8), a5 (saved at sp+120) and a6 (saved at sp+112).   Then sp+34 is the y coordinate minus the halfword at +68 of the word at +44 of the context, and sp+38 is that halfword plus the halfword at +70 of the same record. The record is the word at +44 of the context, read again at 0x1623d04.
  The word at +24 of the context is saved, and the word at +28 of the context is stored at +24 of the context and at +12 of the pointer P. P is the word at (the word at +76 of the context) plus 4 times the word at gp+19288 (0x1623d1c to 0x1623d34).
  Second call: the function pointer at +44 of the record at word +72 of the context, called with a0 = a0, a1 = the context, a2 = 1 and a3 = sp+32. Then the saved word at +24 of the context is restored, and P+12 is set to that saved value, not to its earlier contents (0x1623d60 to 0x1623d6c).

## Fill, span and clip routines read from whole listings

Each routine below was read from its full listing; the plain dump elides runs of nops, and each gap was checked with `objdump -z`. `$gp` is 0x173a9c0 in every routine here. Where a routine does not set gp, its entry says that it uses the caller's. The GOT slot names come from the module's global offset table.

- `RecomputeLayeredExposures..OG` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1711ae0, 212 bytes): **established.**
  Calls the same function pointer three times and returns 1, or 3 without a call when the record has no pointer. The first argument (a0, kept in s0) is the record S and the second (a1) is copied to a5. The frame is 48 bytes: the value a5 + 12N at sp+0, the object pointer O at sp+8 (t9), s0 at sp+16, gp at sp+24 and ra at sp+32.
  The guard is the word at +68 of S, P. When P is zero the routine returns 3 (`li v0,3` at 0x1711ae4 runs before the `beqz` at 0x1711b04, whose target is the epilogue at 0x1711ba4). Nothing else is done.
  Otherwise N is found in three loads. G is the word at gp-5736 (address 0x1739358, read at 0x1711b0c, with gp = 0x173a9c0). The pointer E is the element G of the pointer array at +132 of S (0x1711b10 to 0x1711b1c, index scaled by 4), and N is the word at +24 of E (0x1711b24). a2 = 12N is formed at 0x1711b30 to 0x1711b44 and a2 = a5 + 12N at 0x1711b48.
  O is the word at +16 of S (0x1711b20). The function pointer is the word at +364 of O (0x1711b2c), and it is reloaded from the frame before each call.
  Call 1 (`jalr` at 0x1711b4c): a0 = P, a1 = S+44 (`addiu` at 0x1711b34, an address), a2 = a5 + 12N, a3 = N.
  Call 2 (`jalr` at 0x1711b68): a0 = P+12, where P is the word at +68 of S read again at 0x1711b5c and the `addiu a0,a0,12` sits in the delay slot; a1 = S+56; a2 = S+72. The routine does not set a3, so a3 is what call 1 left in it.
  Call 3 (`jalr` at 0x1711b84): a0 = P+12 (re-read at 0x1711b74, plus 12 at 0x1711b7c), a1 = P+12 (`move a1,a0` in the delay slot at 0x1711b88), a2 = the value saved at sp+0, a3 as call 2 left it.

## Save-under, span-list and region copy routines read from whole listings

Each routine below was read from its full listing, with its calls, branches and exits taken from the listing itself. The window offsets are the ones the save-under entry above uses: parent +24, next sibling +28, first child +36, backing-store record +116 and flag word +124 (bit 24 and bits 27:26 are the flag bits this section tests).

- `miAppendSpans` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16c8e90, 336 bytes): **established.**
  Appends one span list N (a2) to an accumulator A (a0), keeps the least first y and the greatest last y of the accumulated list, and subtracts N from the list T (a1) when the y ranges overlap. The routine does not set gp: it uses the caller's, 0x173a9c0 in this module, for its three GOT slots. The frame is 48 bytes: s0 at sp+8, s1 at sp+16, s2 at sp+0 and ra at sp+32.
  A is a record with the capacity at +0, the count at +4, the array pointer at +8 (12-byte entries), the least first y at +12 and the greatest last y at +16. N is a record with the point count at +0, the points pointer at +4 (x and y halfword pairs, 4 bytes each) and the widths pointer at +8.
  Empty N. When the count of N is zero or negative (`blez` at 0x16c8eb4), the routine calls Xfree (GOT slot gp-28196) with the word at N+4 (0x16c8fc4) and then with the word at N+8 (0x16c8fd0), and returns the second call's result.
  Grow. When the capacity equals the count C (`beq` at 0x16c8ec4), the capacity becomes 2C+16 (0x16c8f64, 0x16c8f6c), stored at A+0 before the call. Xrealloc (GOT slot gp-28592) is called with the array pointer at A+8 and 12 times (2C+16) bytes (0x16c8f80), and its result is stored at A+8. The routine then resumes the append below with C again.
  Append. The entry at index C of the array (12 bytes at array + 12C) takes the count of N (word 0), the points pointer of N (word 1) and the widths pointer of N (word 2), from N+0, N+4 and N+8 (0x16c8ed8 to 0x16c8ef0). A+4 becomes C+1 (`sw` at 0x16c8f08, in the delay slot of the branch at 0x16c8f04, so it is stored on both paths).
  Extent. a1 is the halfword at +2 of the first point of N and a0 the halfword at +2 of its last point (0x16c8ef4, 0x16c8f14). A+12 becomes a1 when a1 is less than A+12 (0x16c8ef8, 0x16c8f0c), and A+16 becomes a0 when A+16 is less than a0 (0x16c8f18, 0x16c8f28).
  Overlap. When T is nonzero, T+12 is compared with a0 and T+16 with a1 (0x16c8f34 to 0x16c8f44). When T+12 is less than a0 and a1 is less than T+16, miSubtractSpans (local GOT slot 541, gp-30588) is called with a0 = T and a1 = N (0x16c8fa0 to 0x16c8fac). Nothing in that sequence sets a2: it is still the 1 that the `slt` at 0x16c8f44 left there, since the branch at 0x16c8f48 is taken only when that result is nonzero.
  Return. v0 is C+1, the new count, unless a call ran: then it is the result of miSubtractSpans, or on the empty path the result of the second Xfree.
- `miRegionCopy` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x171da50, 380 bytes): **established.**
  Copies region R (a1) into region D (a0): the bounding box, then the rectangle list, reusing or reallocating D's list. It returns 1 on every path. gp is 0x173a9c0 (set from t9 at 0x171da78). The frame is 32 bytes: gp at sp+0, s1 at sp+8, s0 at sp+16 and ra at sp+24.
  A region record has the four halfwords of its bounding box at +0, +2, +4 and +6 and the list pointer at +8. A list block has a word at +0 that the code treats as its capacity, a word at +4 that is its rectangle count, and 8-byte rectangles from +8.
  Same region. When D equals R (`beq` at 0x171da74), the routine returns 1 at once.
  Box. The four halfwords of R's box are copied to D at +0, +2, +4 and +6 (0x171da7c to 0x171da98).
  Empty source. The source list SL is the word at R+8, and DL the word at D+8. When SL is zero (0x171daa4) or the word at SL+0 is zero (0x171dab0), the routine frees DL with Xfree (GOT slot gp-28196) when DL is nonzero and its word at +0 is nonzero, stores SL at D+8 (0x171dbb4) and returns 1.
  Otherwise N is the word at SL+4 (the count). When DL is zero, or the word at DL+0 is not less than N (`slt` and `beq` at 0x171dac4, 0x171dac8), DL is kept: its word at +4 becomes N (0x171db2c). Otherwise, when DL is nonzero and its word at +0 is nonzero, DL is freed with Xfree (0x171dadc). A new block of 8N+8 bytes is then allocated with Xalloc (GOT slot gp-28180, the size set at 0x171daf8 and 0x171dafc), with the word at gp-28596 (address 0x1733a0c) set to 1 before the call and cleared after it (0x171daf4, 0x171db04, 0x171db14). D+8 becomes the new block, and its words at +0 and +4 both become N (0x171db1c, 0x171db2c).
  Copy. memmove (GOT slot gp-27928) copies 8 times the word at block+4 bytes from SL+8 to block+8 (0x171db30 to 0x171db4c), where block is the list kept or allocated above.
  Return. v0 is 1 (`addiu` at 0x171db58 after the copy, at 0x171dbb8 on the empty path, and at 0x171db74 for the same-region case).

## Arc, line and span routines of the 2D layer

- `arcSpan0..NP` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1619c00, 348 bytes): **established.**
  Clips one span against two records and makes one call to `arcSpan..MP` (0x171f640, GOT slot 1008 at gp-28720), passing the clipped values. Frame 64 bytes; s0, s1, s2, s8 and ra are saved at sp+24, sp+32, sp+16, sp+40 and sp+48. Arguments: a0 is the first argument (s1), a1 is the second (s2), a2 is the third (t8), a3 is the fourth (t1), a4 is the fifth (s0) (a record read at +0 and +16 as doubles), a5 is t3 (a record read at +64 and +72 as doubles and at +80 and +84 as words), a6 is t2 (a record read at +64 and +88 as doubles and at +96 as a word) and a7 is s8, which goes to the callee's ninth argument as a word at 4(sp).
  Pass-through: the call goes out with the arguments as they came in when any of these holds: the word at +80 of a5 is above 0 (branch at 0x1619c38), the word at +84 of a5 is below 0 (0x1619c4c), the double at +64 of a5 is above 0 (0x1619c6c), the double at +72 of a5 is below 0 (0x1619c74), the word at +96 of a6 is 0 (0x1619c64), or the double at +88 of a6 is not above 0 (0x1619c7c). On this path the callee gets a0 = 0, a1 = s1 (the caller's a0), a2 = s2 (the caller's a1), a3 = t8 (the caller's a2), a4 = t1 (the caller's a3), a5 = s0 (the caller's a4), a6 = t3 (the caller's a5), a7 = t2 (the caller's a6) and the ninth argument s8 (the caller's a7).
  Clip, when every test passes. m is the smaller of the double at +88 of a6 and the difference of the doubles at +0 and +16 of a4 (the `movf.d` at 0x1619c98). f1 = (double at +64 of a6) − m and f5 = (double at +64 of a6) + m. The callee's a2 is ceil(f1) − s1 (the value is taken from trunc(f1), plus one when f1 is positive and not whole, at 0x1619d44). The callee's a3 is ceil(f5). The callee's a4 is (caller a2 + caller a3) − ceil(f5). The clip path also stores trunc(f5), the integer word of f4 at 0x1619cac, to the global word at gp+19324 (address 0x173f53c, delay slot of the branch at 0x1619ce8). The callee's a0 is 0 and its other arguments are the same as on the pass-through path.
  Return: v0 is the callee's result on every path (`jr ra` at 0x1619d3c after the callee's call); this routine does nothing with it.

- `miLineOnePoint..LBB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1655d10, 348 bytes): **established.**
  Calls through a function-pointer table on the second argument and checks a word of that argument. Frame 112 bytes; ra, s0, s1, s2 and s3 are saved at sp+48, sp+32, sp+40, sp+24 and sp+16, and the third argument a2 is saved as a word at sp+84. Arguments: a0 is s1 (the first argument), a1 is s0 (the second), a2 is saved at sp+84, a4 is s3, and a5 is s2.
  The word at +24 of a1 is compared with a2 (branch at 0x1655d40). The word at +24 is also stored at sp+0 on both paths (the delay slot at 0x1655d44). When the two differ, the routine calls `DoChangeGC` (GOT slot 1171 at gp-28068; the X server's routine of that name is at Xsgi 0x102588b0, and the module imports it) with a0 = s0, a1 = 4, a2 = the address sp+84 and a3 = 0, then calls `ValidateGC` (GOT slot 1172 at gp-28064; Xsgi 0x100aecf0) with a0 = s1 and a1 = s0, and continues at 0x1655d4c.
  At 0x1655d4c the word at +16 of a1, bits 25:24 (`srl 24`, `andi 3`), decides the call. Zero goes to 0x1655e3c. Non-zero stores 1 at sp+8 and tests bit 17 of the same word (`sll 14`, `bltz`); when bit 17 is set, the halfword at +10 of s1 is added to s2 and the halfword at +8 of s1 to s3 (0x1655e28 to 0x1655e38). Both paths store s2 at sp+6 and s3 at sp+4 as halfwords.
  Non-zero call: the function whose address is the first word of the table that the word at +72 of a1 points to is called with a0 = s1, a1 = s0, a2 = 1, a3 = sp+4, a4 = sp+8 and a5 = 1.
  Zero call (0x1655e3c): the function whose address is word +20 of the table that the word at +72 of a1 points to is called with a0 = s1, a1 = s0, a2 = 0, a3 = 1 and a4 = sp+4, a5 unchanged.
  After either call, the word at sp+0 is compared with the saved a2 at sp+84 (0x1655da4 to 0x1655db0). When they are equal the routine returns the word at sp+0, which is the value of a2. When they differ it calls `DoChangeGC` again with a0 = s0, a1 = 4, a2 = sp+0 and a3 = 0, then `ValidateGC` with a0 = s1 and a1 = s0, and returns the result of `ValidateGC`.
  The first `DoChangeGC` and `ValidateGC` pair runs only when the entry test found a difference, and the second pair runs on the same condition after the call, so the pair runs twice on that path and never on the equal path.

## Stereo draw wrappers read from whole listings

- `nfbStereoPolyPoint` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16acd90, 448 bytes): **established.**
  Runs the table routine twice, once per eye, and moves the window words by the eye offset around each call. Frame 96 bytes. Saved: s2 at sp+0, s3 at sp+8, s6 at sp+16, s4 at sp+24, the caller's s5 at sp+32, the word at +8 of s0 at sp+40, the word at +72 of a1 (in a5 on entry) at sp+48, gp at sp+64, s0 at sp+56, s1 at sp+72 and ra at sp+80. Arguments: a0 is the record whose word at +16 is read (kept in s2), a1 is the GC (kept in s1), and a2, a3 and a4 are kept in s3, s5 and s6 and passed to each call unchanged.
  The GC's private record is `s0`, the word loaded from the address (the word at a1+76) plus 4 times `nfbGCPrivateIndex` (a small-data word, loaded at 0x16acdbc, not a GOT slot). `s4` is the unsigned halfword at +226 of the word at +116 of the word at +16 of a0 (0x16acdd8). On entry the routine sets the word at +104 of s0 to 1 (0x16acdec) and replaces the word at +72 of a1 with the word at +92 of s0 (0x16acde4). The calls use that replaced word as their table pointer. The word at +104 of s0 is cleared to 0 on return.
  Eye test: the word at +220 of the word at +116 of the word at +16 of a0 is compared with 1 (0x16ace20). Equal goes to path B at 0x16aced8. Any other value is path A at 0x16ace28.
  Path A: the first call is preceded by s0+8 := the word at +100 of s0, a0+10 += s4 and the halfword at +90 of s0 += s4 (0x16ace28 to 0x16ace44). The second call follows s0+8 := the word at +96 of s0, a0+10 -= s4 and s0+90 -= s4 (0x16ace58 to 0x16ace74).
  Path B: the first call is preceded by s0+8 := the word at +96 of s0, a0+10 -= s4 and s0+90 -= s4 (0x16aced8 to 0x16acef4). The second call follows s0+8 := the word at +100 of s0, a0+10 += s4 and s0+90 += s4 (0x16acf08 to 0x16acf24).
  Each call is `lw t9,72(a1)` then `lw t9,20(t9)`: the function at word +20 of the table that the word at +72 of a1 points to, called with a0 = s2, a1 = s1, a2 = s3, a3 = s5 and a4 = s6. The first call is at 0x16ace50 on path A and 0x16acf00 on path B; the second is at 0x16ace90 on path A and 0x16acf40 on path B.
  Return: both paths restore the word at +8 of s0 from sp+40 and the word at +72 of a1 from sp+48 (0x16ace98 to 0x16aced0), then clear the word at +104 of s0. v0 is what the second table call returned, since no instruction writes v0 after it.

- `nfbStereoPolyline` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16f4c80, 448 bytes): **established.**

The one difference is the table slot: each of the four calls loads `lw t9,24(t9)` (at 0x16f4d3c, 0x16f4d6c, 0x16f4dec and 0x16f4e1c), so the function is the one at slot 24 of the table, where `nfbStereoPolyPoint` reads slot 20. Path A (any other value): the first call is preceded by s1+8 := the word at +100 of s1, a2+10 += s7, the halfword at +90 of s1 += s7, and a6 += s7 (0x16c2f00).
