## Window-tree, raster-op table, event and scratch-GC routines read from whole listings

- `mfbReduceRop` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1688a60, 392 bytes): **established.**
  Maps a raster-op number in a0 to one of the values 0, 5, 10 or 15, which v0 returns. Only bit 0 of a1 is read. When that bit is set, a0 indexes a 16-word table at 0x172a1f8 (the base is the word at gp-32576, 0x1730000, minus 24072) and the value is 5 times (a0 & 3). When it is clear, a0 indexes a table at 0x172a1b8 (the same base minus 24136) and the value is 5 times (a0 >> 2). Each table is dispatched with `jr v1` (0x1688a88 and 0x1688ac0). Most targets set v0 in a `li` or `move` in the delay slot of a branch to the common return at 0x1688a9c, and five targets of the first table (indexes 4 to 8, at 0x1688b44, 0x1688b38, 0x1688b2c, 0x1688b20 and 0x1688b14) set v0 and return directly. Index 10 of the first table (0x1688b04) sets nothing: it keeps the 10 that the `li v0,10` at 0x1688a8c already put there; it sits in the delay slot of the `jr v1` at 0x1688a88. An a0 of 16 or more (an unsigned `sltiu` test, so a negative a0 too) returns the word at 0(sp) of the 16-byte frame, which the routine never writes. The routine makes no calls.
- `nfbTellGainedMap` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15e6380, 404 bytes): **established.**
  Returns 1 on every path. Byte 1 of the window record a0 is tested against 2 first. For a value of 2 the routine goes on only when the word at +8 of the second argument a1 is zero (0x15e64bc); otherwise it returns 1. For any other value it takes the word at +120 of a0 and, when that is zero, calls FindWindowWithOptional(a0, 0) and uses the word at +120 of its result. The word at +8 of that object (the word at +120 of the result, then +8) must equal the word at +8 of a1 (0x15e63c0 to 0x15e63c8), or the routine returns 1.
  On the main path it fills a 16-byte record at sp: byte 32 at +0, the word at +4 of a0 at +4, the word at +8 of a1 at +8, zero at +12 and one at +13. It then calls DeliverEvents (GOT slot gp-27924) with a0 = a0, a1 = sp, a2 = 1, a3 = 0, a4 = the word at +8 of a1, a5 = the word at +4 of a0, a6 = 32 and a7 = the word at +4 of W (0x15e646c). W is the window private record, the word at [[a0+132] + 4 * nfbWindowPrivateIndex], where the index is the small-data word at gp-5736. The screen record is the word at +16 of a0, and X is the word at +116 of that record.
  After the call, the flags word at +32 of W decides the rest. Bit 1 clear returns 1. Bit 1 set with bit 0 clear calls the word at +200 of X with a0 = the window record and a1 = the second argument (0x15e64fc to 0x15e6504). Both bits set (0x15e6494 to 0x15e64e0) computes V = the word at (Y + 28 * I + 16), where Y is the word at +116 of X and I is the word at the address held at +68 of a1. If V equals the word at +4 of W the routine returns 1; otherwise it stores V at +4 of W and calls the word at +72 of P with a0 (0x15e64d0 to 0x15e64d8). P is the screen private record, the word at ([[a0+16]+436] + 4 * rrmScreenPrivateIndex).
- `nfbUnmapTree` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1627f10, 404 bytes): **established.**
  Clears bits 2 and 3 (mask 0xfffffff3, from `li a5,-13`) of the word at +32 of the window private record W, for every window it visits. Each window's W is the word at [[window+132] + 4 * nfbWindowPrivateIndex]. The walk goes down through the first child at +36, then across the next sibling at +28, and back up through the parent at +24. The root is the window in a0. A1 selects the scope. With a1 clear (0x1628008 to 0x1628054) the walk covers the root's subtree only. With a1 set (0x1627f20 to 0x1627fe0) it covers the subtree and then each later sibling of the root, the chain at +28 of the root, ending when that word is zero (0x1627f40 to 0x1627f48).
  At the end the routine reads the word at +24 of a0 (the original a0, at 0x1627fe4). If that is nonzero it calls nfbSetDidFlags (0x167bcb0) with it as a0 (0x1627ff4). Only the a1-set path tests a0 for zero (`beqz` at 0x1627f1c), and it jumps to the same final read at 0x1627fe4 on zero, so a zero a0 loads the word at +24 of address zero on either path. The routine does not set gp itself; it uses the caller's gp for the nfbWindowPrivateIndex word (gp-5736) and the nfbSetDidFlags slot (gp-30072, local GOT entry 670). Returns the value of the nfbSetDidFlags call when it makes one, and otherwise the entry value of v0.
- `nfbFreeHWCmapArrays` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x162d300, 416 bytes): **established.**
  Frees the storage pointers held by two tables. The first loop frees the pointer at +4 of each of the 23 24-byte entries that start at a0 (0x162d360, one Xfree per entry with no null test). The second loop runs over the a1 records of 28 bytes that start at a2 (0x162d3c0 to 0x162d444). For each nonzero word at +8 it frees the pointer only when the same pointer has not already been freed by this loop. The freed pointers are recorded in a stack array of a1 words, allocated as (a1 * 4 + 15) & ~15 bytes, and the array count s3 grows with each new pointer. Then the routine frees a0 (the original, 0x162d448) and a2 (0x162d46c), and returns the value of the last Xfree call. Xfree is the GOT slot at gp-28196.

## Scratch-GC put and pixmap get-image routines read from whole listings

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`. GOT slots are named by their dynamic symbol and the global-offset-table formula, and each callee is named by its address in this module where the module defines it.

- `nfbShmPutXYImage..KJ` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16d9e00, 404 bytes): **established.**
  Puts an image into a drawable through a scratch graphics context. Arguments by register: a0 the drawable, a1 a record that is only saved at sp+16 and used for its table at +72 in the second call (the word read at +16 at entry, 0x16d9e24, is the drawable's, not a1's), a2 a depth-like value, a3 the format, a4 to a7 and the stack words at entry +4, +12, +20, +28 and +36 are passed on. The routine stores a2 to a7 and a1 in its 176-byte frame (sd at 0x16d9e04 to 0x16d9e20; the frame offsets are 88, 80, 48, 56, 32, 40 and 16) and a0 at +64 (0x16d9e30).
  It calls GetScratchGC (GOT global slot 1173, formula PLTGOT + 4*(1038 + 2160 - 2025)) with a0 = a2 and a1 = the word at +16 of the drawable (0x16d9e38). A zero result returns at once with v0 = 0 (the `bne` at 0x16d9e44 falls through to the return at 0x16d9e50).
  Otherwise it calls the function at +236 of the drawable's screen record (screen = the word at +16 of a0, 0x16d9e5c to 0x16d9e6c) with a0 = screen, a1 = entry +4, a2 = entry +12 and a3 = the saved a2. A zero result jumps to FreeScratchGC (GOT global slot 1157) with a0 = the scratch GC and returns through it (0x16d9f50 to 0x16d9f64). A nonzero result is kept at +72 and ValidateGC (GOT global slot 1172, 0x16d9e7c) runs with a0 = that drawable and a1 = the scratch GC.
  The format word is set from the saved a3: 1 when a3 equals 1 and 2 otherwise (`xor`, `sltiu` and `movz` at 0x16d9e8c to 0x16d9eac), stored at frame +4. The word at entry +36 is copied to frame +12 (0x16d9eb0 to 0x16d9eb4).
  It calls the function at +8 of the table at +72 of the scratch GC (0x16d9eb8 to 0x16d9edc) with a0 = the drawable, a1 = the scratch GC, a2 = the saved a2, a3 = minus the saved a6, a4 = minus the saved a7, a5 = the saved a4, a6 = the saved a5 and a7 = 0. Then it calls FreeScratchGC with the scratch GC (0x16d9ee4 to 0x16d9eec).
  Second call. The saved a3 selects the function that follows. Both calls pass a0 = the drawable returned by the screen call (kept at sp+72), a1 = entry a0 (reloaded from sp+64), a2 = entry a1 (reloaded from sp+16), a3 = 0, a4 = 0, a5 = entry +4, a6 = entry +12 and a7 = entry +20 (0x16d9ef4 to 0x16d9f00), and frame +4 = entry +28. A nonzero a3 calls the function at +12 of the table at +72 of entry a1 (0x16d9f10 to 0x16d9f28), leaving frame +12 as the entry +36 word copied earlier. A zero a3 first stores 1 in frame +12, then calls the function at +16 of the same table with the same registers (0x16d9f68 to 0x16d9f88). Both paths rejoin at 0x16d9f34.
  Tail. The drawable from the screen call is loaded again, and the routine jumps through the function at +240 of its screen record with a0 = that drawable after restoring s0, ra and the stack (0x16d9f34 to 0x16d9f48). The routine has no loop and no branch-likely instruction, and it makes no stores through a pointer other than the frame.
- `cfb32GetImage` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x162e940, 412 bytes): **established.**
  Reads a rectangle of pixels from a drawable into memory. Arguments by register: a0 the drawable, a1 x, a2 y, a3 width, a4 height, a5 the format, a6 the plane mask, a7 the destination. The routine copies a7 into v0 first (0x162e94c), so every early return leaves v0 equal to the entry a7.
  A zero width (0x162e95c) or a zero height (0x162e964) returns at once. Byte +3 of the drawable is compared with 1 (0x162e96c, 0x162e974): when it is 1 the routine calls mfbGetImage (GOT local slot 279, address 0x159fbb0, 0x162eacc) with a0 = the drawable and a4 = the height, and the other registers unchanged. When the format is not 2 (0x162e97c) it calls miGetImage (GOT local slot 770, address 0x16abb10, 0x162eaa8) with all eight arguments unchanged (a4 is restored from s0 in the call's delay slot, and a0 from s1). Both of these paths return the callee's v0 (the epilogue at 0x162eab0 and 0x162ead8 does not write v0).
  Format 2 (ZPixmap) path. The word at +16 of the drawable is the screen S (0x162e978). The routine loads three words from the table PixmapWidthPaddingInfo (GOT global slot 1081) at the offset 12 times the drawable's depth byte (+2), and computes a5 = ((word 0 + width) shifted right arithmetically by word 4) shifted left by word 8 (0x162e9b8 to 0x162e9e0). It calls GetScratchPixmapHeader (GOT global slot 1224, 0x162e9dc) with a0 = S, a1 = width, a2 = height, a3 = the depth byte, a4 = byte +3 of the drawable, a5 = the value above and a6 = entry a7.
  A zero header returns with v0 = 0 (0x162e9e8). When the plane mask is not minus one (0x162e9ec, 0x162e9f0) the routine calls bzero (GOT global slot 1220, 0x162ea0c) with a0 = entry a7 and a1 = the word at +28 of the header times the height (`mult` and `mflo` at 0x162ea00 to 0x162ea08). A plane mask of minus one skips the clear.
  It then builds two rectangles on the stack. The halfwords at frame +0 and +2 are the drawable's halfwords at +8 and +10 plus a1 and a2. The halfwords at +8 and +10 are zero and at +12 and +14 are the width and the height. It calls the function at +340 of S (0x162ea4c to 0x162ea58) with a0 = frame +16 (the output), a1 = frame +8 and a2 = 1. - `cfb16GetImage` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1667e40, 416 bytes): **established.**

The row stride comes from the 12-byte entry of PixmapWidthPaddingInfo (GOT global slot 1081) at index 12 times byte +2 of the drawable, the depth. The value passed on is a5 = ((word 0 + width) shifted right arithmetically by word 4) shifted left by word 8 (0x1667e9c to 0x1667ed0, with the final `sllv` in the call's delay slot at 0x1667ee0).
  It calls GetScratchPixmapHeader (GOT global slot 1224, call at 0x1667edc) with a0 = S, a1 = width, a2 = height, a3 = the depth byte, a4 = byte +3 of the drawable, a5 = the stride value above and a6 = entry a7. The header is kept at sp+32. A zero header returns 0 (0x1667ee8).

Unless the result is 0xffff (`beq` at 0x1667ef4) the routine calls bzero (GOT global slot 1220, call at 0x1667f10) with a0 = entry a7 and a1 = the word at header+28 times the height (`mult` at 0x1667f04, `mflo` at 0x1667f0c).   It builds two rectangles on the stack. The halfword stores at 0x1667f30 to 0x1667f4c are, in issue order: frame +0 = the drawable's halfword at +8 plus x, frame +14 = height, frame +12 = width, frame +10 = 0, frame +8 = 0, frame +2 = the drawable's halfword at +10 plus y. The width is not held in a register of its own here: it is loaded into ra from sp+64 (0x1667f18) and stored from ra, and ra is restored from sp+104 before the return. The one backward branch (0x1667fd8) goes to the epilogue, not a loop, and there is no branch-likely instruction. The routine stores through no pointer other than the frame.

## Span-array lookup, clip validation and pixmap creation read from whole listings

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`. GOT slots are named by their dynamic symbol (the local or global kind is given where it matters), and addresses are in this module.

- `realFindSpan..BAB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16cdb00, 412 bytes): **established.**
  Returns the element for key a0 in a growable word array, and grows the array at one end when a0 lies outside it. Four small-data words hold the state: the word at 0x173f6f4 (gp+19764) is the lowest key X, the word at 0x1738d24 (gp-7324) is the highest key Y, the word at 0x173f6f8 (gp+19768) is the element count Z, and the word at 0x173f6f0 (gp+19760) is the array pointer P. The array holds the keys X through Y inclusive, so an empty array has Y = X - 1 and Z = 0.
  When X <= a0 <= Y the routine changes nothing and returns P + 4*(a0 - X). The test is `slt` at 0x16cdb0c (a0 < X, and the `bnez` at 0x16cdb18 then goes straight to growth at 0x16cdb24), then `slt` at 0x16cdb1c in the branch delay slot (Y < a0) and `beq` at 0x16cdb20 (to the return at 0x16cdc64 when Y is not below a0). The return is the `addu` in the delay slot of the `jr ra` at 0x16cdc5c, after `sll` by 2 at 0x16cdc58; it uses the saved s0 restored at 0x16cdc50, and ra and s1 are not saved on this path.
  Otherwise D is the distance outside the range: X - a0 when a0 < X, and a0 - Y when a0 > Y (0x16cdb34 to 0x16cdb40). The count added is 100 when D < 100 and D + 100 when D >= 100 (`slti` at 0x16cdb48 and `movz` at 0x16cdb54), so the count goes from 100 to 200 at D = 100. An empty array (Z = 0, tested at 0x16cdb28) first stores X = a0 and Y = a0 - 1 into the globals (0x16cdc88 and 0x16cdc90) and then takes the growth path. The a0 < X result in v0 was computed before that store, so D is 1 when a0 was not below the old X and 0 when it was; the count is 100 either way.
  The routine calls Xalloc (global GOT slot 1143, 0x16cdb68) with 4*(count + Z) bytes. A zero return ends the routine at 0x16cdc6c with v0 = 0. P, Y, X and Z keep their old values, except that an empty array has already had X and Y set to a0 and a0 - 1 by the stores above.
  On success the old P is copied and freed only when P is nonzero (the branch at 0x16cdba0 skips both). With a0 < X the Z old words go to element count of the new array (memmove, global GOT slot 1206, destination new + 4*count, byte count 4*Z, 0x16cdbc0), and the first count words are zeroed (bzero, global GOT slot 1220, 0x16cdbf0). With a0 > Y the old words go to element 0 (destination new + 0) and the last count words, elements Z to Z + count - 1, are zeroed (bzero at 0x16cdc1c). The old pointer is then freed with Xfree (global GOT slot 1139, 0x16cdbcc). A zero front clear is skipped when X does not move (the `blez` at 0x16cdbe0).
  Globals after success: P = the new array, X = X - count when a0 < X (otherwise unchanged), Y = Y + count when a0 >= X (otherwise unchanged), and Z = Z + count (0x16cdc28 to 0x16cdc48). The return value is new P + 4*(a0 - X_new), computed at 0x16cdc58 to 0x16cdc60.
  Return values by path: P + 4*(a0 - X) for a key already in the array; the new P + 4*(a0 - X_new) after growth; 0 when Xalloc fails (in the empty-array case X and Y are left set to a0 and a0 - 1, with Z still 0 and P unchanged).

- `ValidateClip..CF` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16df630, 412 bytes): **established.**
  Validates the clip of one window and then queues or runs the screen's hook for it. a0 is the window record, saved at sp+8.
  It first calls `DelayValidateClip..EF` (local GOT slot 685, 0x16df64c) with a0, and returns that value at once when it is nonzero (`bne` at 0x16df654).
  Otherwise it finds the window's private record W = the word at [[a0+132] + 4*rrmWindowPrivateIndex] (the index is read through global GOT slot 1104, 0x16df658 to 0x16df670). When W is zero, or bit 0 of the halfword at W+0 is clear (tests at 0x16df678 and 0x16df694), it calls ErrorF (global GOT slot 1142, 0x16df750) with the format at 0x172eb20, a1 = the word at +4 of the window, and a2 = W, and returns ErrorF's result.
  Otherwise it sets bit 1 of the byte at W+6 (`ori` and `sb` at 0x16df6ac and 0x16df6b0). It loads the screen private record SP = the word at [[a0+16] + 436 + 4*rrmScreenPrivateIndex] (the index is read through global GOT slot 1151 at 0x16df67c, and the word is loaded at 0x16df69c to 0x16df6a8). It then shifts the word at +124 of the window left by 20 and branches on the sign (`bltz` at 0x16df6bc), which is bit 11 of that word.
  Bit 11 clear: when bit 2 of the halfword at W+0 is clear, it sets bit 1 of the byte at W+5 (0x16df6ec to 0x16df6f4) and returns 0 (the v0 left by the first call). When bit 2 is set it calls the word at +28 of SP with a0 = the window (`jalr` at 0x16df6d4) and returns that result.
  Bit 11 set: when the word at +4 of SP is zero, it calls FindFreeID (local GOT slot 1004, 0x16df764) with a0 = the window. A zero result calls appendRequestQueue..AF with a0 = the window and a1 = 2 (local GOT slot 396, 0x16df7bc). A nonzero result calls the word at +28 of SP with a0 = the window (0x16df77c). When the word at +4 of SP is nonzero, the word at W+24 is tested: zero calls appendRequestQueue..AF with a1 = 2 (0x16df7a8). Nonzero with bit 1 of the byte at W+4 clear calls rrmValidateCID (local GOT slot 361, 0x16df790) with a0 = the window and then the word at +28 of SP. Nonzero with that bit set calls the word at +28 of SP directly (0x16df728 to 0x16df738).
  The return value is the result of the last call on each path; the result of appendRequestQueue..AF, or of the function at +28 of SP, is returned; the rrmValidateCID result is not used.

- `cfbCreatePixmap` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x163d6a0, 416 bytes): **established.**
  Allocates a colour frame-buffer pixmap for screen a0 with width a1, height a2 and depth a3, and fills its header. The stride comes from the 12-byte entry of PixmapWidthPaddingInfo (global GOT slot 1081) at index a3: the entry's word 0 plus the width, shifted right arithmetically by word 1 and left by word 2 (0x163d6b8 to 0x163d700). The routine keeps the stride at frame+32 and the entry pointer at frame+8.
  Allocation. When panoramiXMode (global GOT slot 1100, loaded at 0x163d6dc) is zero (0x163d704), or the panoramiXSharePixmap word (global GOT slot 1101, 0x163d70c) is zero (0x163d714), it calls AllocatePixmap (global GOT slot 1227) with a0 = the screen and a1 = stride * height (`mult` at 0x163d80c, `mflo` at 0x163d814). Otherwise it calls AllocatePixmap with a0 = the screen and a1 = 0 (0x163d724 to 0x163d72c), so the shared pixmap is made without data. A zero result from AllocatePixmap returns 0 (0x163d828 to 0x163d838).
  Header fields, all stored from the allocation's returned pixmap P: byte +0 = 1, byte +1 = 0, byte +2 = the depth a3, byte +3 = trunc(8 * 2^(word 2) / (word 0 + 1)) as a double division (`div.d` at 0x163d760, `trunc.w.d` at 0x163d7c0), where `teq` at 0x163d7d4 traps (code 7) when the divisor word 0 + 1 is zero; it comes after the division is issued. Halfword +8 and +10 = 0, halfword +12 = the width, halfword +14 = the height, word +4 = 0, word +16 = the screen, word +20 = the serial number, word +24 = 1, word +28 = the stride, and word +32 = P + the word at +460 of the screen record when stride * height (`mult` at 0x163d79c, `mflo` at 0x163d7cc) is nonzero, otherwise 0 (0x163d7e4 to 0x163d7f4). That product is formed on every path, including the shared pixmap allocated with a1 = 0.
  Serial number. It is globalSerialNumber (global GOT slot 1156) plus one, and when that is above 0x10000000 the new value is 1 (`sltu` and `movn` at 0x163d7bc to 0x163d7c8). The new value is stored both at P+20 and in globalSerialNumber.
  Return: P on success, 0 when AllocatePixmap returns 0.

## Audio-library lookup and arc-cap routines read from whole listings

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`. GOT slots are named by their dynamic symbol, and addresses are in this module.

- `alIsSubtype` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1563010, 420 bytes): **established.**
  Tests whether the value a0 matches a subtype entry of the object a1, using two `alGetParams` calls and a scan of an 82-word table. The routine saves a0 and a1 in s0 and s1 (sp+56 and sp+64) and keeps ra at sp+72 for its calls. Its only stores are to the 80-byte frame. Both calls are `bgezall zero` (0x156313c and 0x1563180), which always link, so their delay-slot stores run as part of the call.
  Setup. `dsll32 v0,a0,2` (0x1563010) leaves a0 shifted left by 34 in v0, so the sign of v0 is bit 29 of a0. If a0 equals a1 (`beq` at 0x1563034) the routine returns 1. Otherwise, a clear bit 29 of a0 sends it to path B (0x1563168). A set bit 29 of a0 falls through to the test of bit 29 of a1 (`and` with 0x20000000 at 0x1563044): a clear bit sends it to path C (0x1563124), a set bit falls to path A.
  Path A (bit 29 set in both K and a1, where K is s0). The `beq` at 0x1563050 compares s0 with s1 and returns 1 when they are equal (0x1563160). Entered straight from the setup, it cannot be taken, because the test at 0x1563034 already ruled out equal arguments. Entered after path B, K is the word loaded from sp+8 and can equal s1, so it can be taken. Its delay slot sets a1 to -2, and the routine enters the table scan.
  Path B (bit 29 of a0 clear). It calls alGetParams (GOT local slot 182, address 0x1562af0) with a0 = the entry a0, a1 = sp+0 and a2 = 1. The word 0x10000012 is stored at sp+0 in the call's delay slot (0x1563184). A return of zero or less, or a halfword at sp+20 of zero or less, returns 0 (0x15631a4). Otherwise K is loaded from the word at sp+8 (0x15631a0, in the delay slot of the branch at 0x156319c), and the routine continues at the bit 29 test of a1 (0x1563044). s1 is unchanged on this path.
  Path C (bit 29 of a1 clear). It calls alGetParams with a0 = the entry a1, a1 = sp+24 and a2 = 1. The word 0x10000012 is stored at sp+24 in the call's delay slot (0x1563140). A return of zero or less, or a halfword at sp+44 of zero or less, returns 0 (0x15631ac). Otherwise s1 is loaded from the word at sp+32 (0x1563154). If K equals the new s1 (`bne` at 0x1563158 falls through) it returns 1 (0x1563160). Otherwise it runs the table scan with K and the new s1.
  Table scan. v0 starts at 0x1732888, which is the word at gp-32576 (0x1730000, GOT local slot 44) plus 10376 (0x1563058 to 0x1563068). a6 = v0 + 328 = 0x17329d0 is the end, so the table has 82 words. a0 = -1, a1 = -2, a5 = 0 and a7 = 0 are set before the scan. For each word w, in address order:
   - w equal to K sets a5 to 1 (0x1563090, then 0x1563080). This test comes first.
   - While a5 is 0, any other w is skipped (0x1563098).
   - w equal to -1 adds 1 to a7 (0x15630a0, 0x15630d0). w equal to -2 subtracts 1 from a7 (0x15630a8, 0x15630d8).
   - Any other w, with a7 zero or less, returns 0 (0x15630b0 to 0x1563110). With a7 above zero, w equal to s1 returns 1 (0x15630b8 to 0x15630c0), and any other w goes on to the next word.
   The scan returns 0 when the end is reached (0x15630e0). The table is only read.
  Return values by path: 1 for equal arguments (0x15630f8), for a table match (0x15630c0), for path C with K equal to the new s1 (0x1563160) and for path A with K equal to s1 after path B (0x1563050 to 0x1563160). 0 for the end of the table (0x15630e0), for a7 zero or less at a word that is not K, -1 or -2 (0x1563110), and for the failed alGetParams checks on paths B and C (0x15631a4 and 0x15631ac).
- `miRoundCap..BO` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x169d6c0, 420 bytes): **established.**
  Builds an arc record on the stack from a line end, two doubles and a halfword width, and then runs the arc-point routine and the span fill on it. Arguments by register: a0 and a1 are saved at sp+88 and sp+80, and a1 is the record whose unsigned halfword at +6 is the width H. The doubles f14 and f15 are kept as f20 and f22 (saved at sp+56 and sp+64), f16 is saved at sp+72 and f17 at sp+96, and f18 and f19 are read where they are. The frame is 176 bytes and ra is saved at sp+104. The caller's stack words are read at frame offsets 176, 184, 196, 204, 208 and 216.
  Width. When H is zero (`beq` at 0x169d6e4) the routine takes the double 1.0 from the constant at 0x172db10 (0x169d850) and joins the common path at 0x169d6fc. Otherwise it converts H to a double (`cvt.d.w` at 0x169d6f4). Either way w is stored at sp+16 and sp+24 (0x169d708, 0x169d710).
  Record start. The constant 0.5 (from 0x172d5d0) is loaded at 0x169d700. f1 = f14 - w*0.5 (`nmsub.d` at 0x169d704) is stored at sp+0, and f0 = f15 - w*0.5 (`nmsub.d` at 0x169d70c) is stored at sp+8 by the delay slot of the next call (0x169d72c), before the call. The pointer a2 = the word at gp-28680 (0x172acc0) is the base of the constants: 0.5 at +10512, 1.0 at +11856, 1e-6 at +24600, -180.0 at +24632 and 360.0 at +24640.
  Angle 1. miDatan2 (GOT entry at gp-29524, address 0x16ba850, call at 0x169d728) is called with f12 = f19 - f15, f13 = f18 - f14 and a2 = 0x172acc0. Its result in f0 is angle 1, and -angle 1 is stored at sp+32 (0x169d734, 0x169d744).
  Tests. With d1 = f14 - f16 and d2 = f15 - f17, the routine tests |d1| against 1e-6 (`c.le.d` at 0x169d768) and then |d2| against 1e-6 (`c.le.d` at 0x169d788), taking the absolute values with `neg.d` and `mov.d` at 0x169d760 to 0x169d784.
  If either test fails, the routine goes to the angle path below: the `bc1f` at 0x169d774 lands at 0x169d808, and the `bc1f` at 0x169d78c lands at 0x169d80c, after the f13 load, which its own delay slot (0x169d790) already made. If both pass, it stores -180.0 at sp+40 (0x169d794), stores 0 at sp+48 (0x169d7ac) and calls miGetArcPts (GOT entry at gp-31548, address 0x15b2670, call at 0x169d7a8) with a0 = sp+0, a1 = 0 and a2 = sp+48. The routine's own call sets no floating-point argument register.
  Angle path (0x169d808). It calls miDatan2 with f12 = (the double at frame offset 184, the caller's sp+8) - f15 and f13 = (the double at frame offset 176, the caller's sp+0) - f14, using the same a2 (0x169d818). Then f5 = angle 1 - angle 2, read as -(-angle 1 + angle 2) (0x169d824, 0x169d830). The store of f5 at sp+40 (0x169d83c) runs before the branch on every path. With f5 not less than zero (`c.lt.d` at 0x169d838 with 0.0 in f7), `bc1f` at 0x169d840 goes straight to 0x169d798. With f5 less than zero the add of 360.0 (0x169d844, the delay slot of that `bc1f`) is kept, and the `b` at 0x169d848 stores f5 + 360.0 over the first store in its delay slot (0x169d84c). The comparison is false for NaN, so NaN takes the non-negative store. Both paths then continue at the miGetArcPts sequence that starts at 0x169d798 (the t9 load), with the same arguments as above.
  After miGetArcPts. If its return v0 is zero (0x169d7b0) the routine skips to the final free. Otherwise it calls miFillSppPoly (GOT local slot 323, address 0x15c3f90, call at 0x169d7e0) with a0 and a1 from sp+88 and sp+80, a2 = v0 (the count), a3 = the word at sp+48 (the points pointer that miGetArcPts stored), a4 = minus the word at frame offset 196 (the caller's sp+20), a5 = minus the word at frame offset 204 (the caller's sp+28), f18 = the double at frame offset 208 (the caller's sp+32) and f19 = the double at frame offset 216 (the caller's sp+40).
  Final free. Both paths call Xfree (global GOT slot at gp-28196) with a0 = the word at sp+48 (0x169d7f4), restore f20 and f22, and return Xfree's v0 (0x169d800). Return value: the v0 of Xfree on every path. The routine has no loop, no branch-likely branch and no store through a pointer other than its frame.

## Ellipse angle and mono-to-stereo routines read from whole listings

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`. GOT slots are named by their dynamic symbol, and addresses are in this module. Pool constants are named by the word's own address (the table at 0x172acc0 is the value of the slot at gp-28680).

- `miEllipseAngleToSlope` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16e71b0, 420 bytes): **established.**
  Converts an angle a0 in 1/64 of a degree (23040 units to a turn) and two signed radii a1 and a2 into two signed ratios, each stored as a word through a pointer. Arguments by register: a0 is the angle, a1 and a2 are the two words that scale the results, a3 and a4 are the output pointers. The frame is 80 bytes: ra at sp+64, the caller's a2 at sp+56, a4 at sp+48 and a3 at sp+40.
  Axis angles. a0 equal to 0 (`beqz` at 0x16e71b8), 5760 (`beq` at 0x16e71c0), 11520 (`beq` at 0x16e71c8) or 17280 (`beq` at 0x16e71d0) leaves the general path. Angle 0 stores -1 at a3 and 0 at a4 (0x16e7324). Angle 5760 stores 0 at a3 and 1 at a4 (0x16e7334, which also sets v0 to 1). Angle 11520 stores 1 at a3 and 0 at a4 (0x16e7344). Angle 17280 stores 0 at a3 and -1 at a4 (0x16e7314). The `sd a2,56(sp)` in the delay slot of the 17280 test (0x16e71d0) runs only on the 17280 path and the general path, not on the zero, 5760 or 11520 paths. Each axis path ends at the common return (0x16e730c).
  General path. The body stores a4 at sp+48 and a3 at sp+40, converts a0 and a1 to doubles, and forms f12 = (double) a0 times the double at 0x1730d88 (0.000272707695624114, which is 2π/23040). It calls the routine at GOT slot gp-27860 (global `__dcis`, dynamic symbol 2210) with that argument, with (double) a1 saved at sp+8. The two results of that call are f0 and f2. The body forms x = (double) a1 times f0 and y = (double) a2 times f2.
  Signs and magnitudes. The flag at sp+24 is 1 when x is below 0.0 and 0 otherwise, and |x| is kept in f6. The flag at sp+32 is 1 when y is below 0.0 and 0 otherwise, and |y| is stored at sp+16. The sign tests are `c.lt.d` against 0.0, so a NaN reads as non-negative.
  Ratios. m = max(|x|, |y|) is taken with `c.lt.d` and `movt.d` at 0x16e727c and 0x16e7280, and stored at sp+0. The body computes the double 0.5 (0x172d5d0) plus 32768 times |x| divided by m, calls `floor` (GOT slot gp-28292, dynamic symbol 2102, call at 0x16e7298), and converts the result with `trunc.w.d` into X. Y is computed the same way from |y|, with a second `floor` call at 0x16e72dc. The 32768 is the double at 0x1730d90 and 0.5 is the double at 0x172d5d0. The rounding argument is formed in each `floor` call's delay slot.
  Stores. X is negated when the x flag is 1 (`movz` at 0x16e72d4), and the word is stored at 0x16e72d8 through the caller's a3 (reloaded from sp+40). Y is negated when the y flag is 1 (`movz` at 0x16e72fc), and the word is stored in the delay slot of the return (0x16e7308) through the caller's a4 (reloaded from sp+48). Each output is one word. The other stores are to the frame.
  Degenerate input. The body has no test for m equal to zero. With a1 and a2 both zero the divisions are 0/0, and the stored words are whatever `trunc.w.d` gives for a NaN.
  Return values. On the general path v0 is not written after the second `floor` call, so the return value is whatever that call left in v0 (exit at 0x16e7304). On the axis paths v0 is the caller's value, except on the angle 5760 path, where it is 1.
  Frame: ra is restored before the return, and the frame is popped at 0x16e7300.
- `_AL2WriteMonoToStereo_8v` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x155ff10, 424 bytes): **established.**
  Expands n signed 8-bit mono samples into n stereo frames. Each frame is two 32-bit words at the destination, both holding the sample shifted left 16 bits (the low halfword is zero). Arguments by register: a0 is a record R whose word at +0 is the source base S, a1 is a record Q whose word at +0 is the stride t0 in bytes between samples, a2 is the destination D and a3 is the frame count n. The frame is 16 bytes: gp at sp+0, and ra at sp+8 on the zero path only (`sd` at 0x1560068).
  Zero path. When S is zero (`beqz a7` at 0x155ff38, branch to 0x1560060), the body calls `bzero` (GOT slot gp-27872, dynamic symbol 2207, call at 0x1560070) with a0 = D and a1 = 8n, and returns the value bzero returned. R is not changed.
  Count of zero or less. When n is 0 or below (`blez` at 0x155ff44, to 0x1560080), a3 is set to 0 and R is set to S unchanged.
  Odd count. When n is odd, the first sample S[0] is written first: two words at D+0 and D+4 (0x155ff58 to 0x155ff70). Then a2 = D+8 and a3 = 1. The next sample is read at S + t0, and the rest of the body works from there.
  Pairs. The body makes p = n >> 1 (arithmetic shift, 0x155ff74). When p is 0, it goes to the exit. When p is 1 (the `slti` and `bnez` at 0x155ff84 and 0x155ff88), it goes to 0x1560088, which writes two more frames for the next two samples (words at a2+0, a2+4, a2+8 and a2+12) and sets a3 to a3 + 2.
  Loop. When p is 2 or more, a first block (0x155ff90 to 0x155ffa4) writes the current sample at a2+4 and a2+0 (a2 is D, or D+8 after the odd first frame). Two zero words at 0x155ffa8 and 0x155ffac (objdump prints them as "...") sit before the loop head, so the fall-through runs them as nops. The loop head is 0x155ffb0 and the back branch is `bne` at 0x1560010. Each pass has two half-passes. Each half-pass writes two frames, adds 2 to a3 and adds 16 to a2. A `beq` at 0x155ffdc leaves the loop after the first half-pass when a2 equals a0, where a0 = D + 8n - 16 (set at 0x155ff78; the argument a0 was saved in t2 at 0x155ff1c). The `bne` at 0x1560010 repeats the pass otherwise. A pass therefore writes four frames.
  Exits. Both exits write one more frame at a2+8 (the sample at the stride, from the `lb` at 0x156001c, with a2 = end minus 16), and set a3 to n. The `beq` exit (0x155ffdc, to 0x1560058) and the `bne` fall-through (0x1560018) use the same store sequence.
  Source update. At 0x1560030 the body sets the new source base to S + a3 times t0, with `multu`, `mflo` and `addu` against the saved S in a7. It stores that word through t2 = R in the delay slot of the return (0x1560048). This runs on every path that reaches 0x1560030, including the count-of-zero path, which stores S back.
  Return values. The routine returns no value of its own. On the zero path v0 is what bzero returned. On the count-of-zero path and the even pair-of-one path v0 is the caller's value. On the odd paths with p of 0 or 1, v0 is the first shifted sample S[0] << 16. On the loop exits v0 is the last shifted sample the loop loaded: s(k+2) << 16 from 0x155ffcc on the `beq` exit, and s(k+4) << 16 from 0x1560000 on the `bne` exit.
  Frame: gp is restored at 0x1560034 and the frame is popped at 0x1560038.

## Mono-to-stereo, fill-spans and Inf-layer wrapper routines read from whole listings

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`. GOT slots are named by their dynamic symbol, and addresses are in this module. Pool constants are named by the word's own address. Records are named by their offsets from the register that holds them, as the code shows.

- `_AL2WriteMonoToStereo_fv` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15603e0, 428 bytes): **established.**
  Expands n single-precision mono samples into n stereo frames. Each frame is two 32-bit words at the destination, both holding the same value: the sample times a gain, clamped to the 24-bit signed range and truncated to an integer. Arguments by register: a0 is a record R whose word at +0 is the source base S; a1 is a record Q whose word at +0 is the stride t2 in float elements between samples; a2 is the destination D; a3 is the frame count n; a4 is a record whose single-precision word at +144 is the gain g. The frame is 16 bytes: the caller's gp at sp+0, and ra at sp+8 on the zero path only (`sd` at 0x156056c).
  Sample value. Each sample s is multiplied by g in single precision (`mul.s` at 0x1560438, 0x15604b8, 0x15604dc, 0x1560524 and 0x1560530, the last two in branch delay slots). Then: s greater than the single 8388607.0 (the word at 0x173f850, gp+20112) gives 0x007fffff. Otherwise s less than the single -8388608.0 (the word at 0x173f854, gp+20116) gives 0xff800000. Otherwise the word is `trunc.w.s` of s. The high test is `c.lt.s` followed by `bc1f`/`bc1t` branches. A NaN fails both tests, so it takes the truncation, and the body has no separate NaN test. The values are not shifted; the 8-bit routine `_AL2WriteMonoToStereo_8v` shifts its samples left 16 bits.
  Zero path. When S is zero (`beqz` at 0x1560404) the body calls bzero (global GOT slot gp-27872, dynamic symbol 2207, `jalr` at 0x1560574) with a0 = D and a1 = 8n (`addu` at 0x1560570, `sll` by 2 in the delay slot). It passes 8n as it is, without a sign check, and returns the value bzero returned. R is not written on this path.
  Odd count. The body sets a6 = n & 1 (0x15603e0). When n is odd, the first sample S[0] is written first: its value goes to D+0 and D+4 (0x1560450 to 0x1560458, or the paths from 0x1560540). Then a2 = D+8 (0x1560440) and a5 = S + 4*t2 (0x1560444).
  Pairs. The pair count p = n >> 1 (arithmetic, `sra` at 0x1560418). When p is 0 (`bnez` at 0x156045c not taken) the body goes to the exit. Otherwise a loop head at 0x15604b8 handles two samples per pass: the sample at a5 (for frame A) and the sample a stride later (for frame B). a5 advances by two strides (0x15604c8, 0x15604d0), and a2 advances by 16 (0x15604c0). Frame A is stored at a2-16 and a2-12, frame B at a2-8 and a2-4. Each frame is clamped on its own sample, with the same three cases as above. The end pointer t0 = D + 8n is set at 0x1560424, and the pass ends with `beq a2,t0` at 0x15604ac, whose delay slot stores frame B's first word. The fall-through goes on to the next pass at 0x15604b4.
  Exit. At 0x1560464 the body keeps a3 as n when n is positive, and sets it to zero when n is negative (`move` at 0x156046c, taken only when n is below zero; n equal to zero already holds zero). It then computes S + 4 * t2 * a3 (`multu`, `mflo`, `sll` by 2, `addu` at 0x1560470 to 0x1560484) and stores that word to R+0 in the delay slot of the return (0x1560488). So R+0 is advanced by n samples, and by nothing when n is zero or below.
  Return values. The body never writes v0 on the main path, so the main exit returns the caller's v0. The zero path returns the v0 that bzero returned (0x1560578 and 0x1560494). The word at 0x156049c is unreached.
  Frame: gp is restored from sp+0 on both exits (0x1560478 and 0x1560490), ra from sp+8 on the zero path (0x1560580), and the frame is popped at 0x156047c and 0x1560498.
- `nfbFillSpans` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x161b900, 428 bytes): **established.**
  Fill-spans wrapper: clips a list of spans against the clip of the private record that the second argument selects, then passes the clipped spans to a fill routine held in the same record. Arguments by register: a0 is the first argument (saved at -80(s8)), a1 is the second (saved at -72(s8), and its word at +76 is the private-array pointer), a2 is the span count nInit (kept in s1), a3 is the point array (saved at -128(s8)), a4 is the width array (saved at -136(s8)), and a5 is the sort flag (saved at -152(s8)). The frame is 160 bytes below s8, which is the sp on entry, and the offsets below are the ones the code uses with s8 as the base. The caller's s8 is copied into a6 at entry and saved at -112(s8); the clip pointer Q (see below) is saved at -160(s8); gp is saved at -104(s8) and ra at -144(s8). The routine also saves s0 at -120(s8), s1 at -96(s8) and the fill routine pointer at -88(s8), and stores the second allocation's base (sp) at -64(s8).
  Private record. The index i is the word at gp-6768 (0x1738f50), and i times 4 is added to the word at a1+76 (0x161b91c to 0x161b930). The word there is the record Pv. From Pv: the word at +8 is the clip Q (a6, 0x161b93c), the word at +36 is X, and the word at X+4 is the fill routine pointer F (0x161b950, saved at -88(s8)). From Q, the word at +8 is the box list T (0x161b944), and the word at T+4 is the box count nb (0x161b960). When T is zero the clip is the single box at Q, with nb = 1 (0x161ba94).
  Band measure. The routine walks the boxes in list order. A box's y1 is the signed halfword at +2 of its 8-byte entry. A band start sets the band key t3 to that box's y1 (0x161b9ac) and zeroes t1 (0x161b9b4). Each pass at 0x161b980 counts down the boxes left (t2), advances t0 by 8 and adds one to t1; the add is in the delay slot of the `blez` at 0x161b988, so it also runs on the pass that finds no boxes left. A box whose y1 equals the key is in the same band (`beq` at 0x161b994). A different y1 ends the band (0x161b99c): t1 is then the size of the band just ended, because the box being examined has not been counted, and that box starts the next band. At each band end, and at the loop exit (0x161b9c4), the routine keeps the larger value (`slt` at 0x161b9a0 and `movn` at 0x161b9a8, the `movn` being in the delay slot of the `blez` at 0x161b9a4, so it also runs for the last band). So ra ends as the size of the largest band, the longest run of consecutive boxes with the same y1, and every band including the last is counted at its full size. When nb is 0 or below, ra is 0 (0x161baa4). When the clip has no box list, the single box at Q is one band of size 1 and ra is 1 (0x161ba94, then the pass at 0x161b980 once). A small model of this loop, run on box lists of 1, 2 and 5 boxes, gives the same band sizes.
  Scratch areas. The routine computes t1 = 4 * nInit * ra (32-bit `mult`, `mflo`, `sll` by 2 at 0x161b9dc to 0x161b9e4), adds 15 and clears the low four bits (0x161b9ec, 0x161b9f4, 0x161b9f8). That size is taken from the stack twice: area1 is the first allocation, and its base is s0 (0x161b9fc, 0x161ba00). Area2 is the second allocation, below area1, and its base is sp (0x161ba04). If sp or s0 is zero, the routine skips to 0x161ba2c and returns with v0 = 4 times i. The routine does not return early for nInit of zero or below: with ra of 0 the areas are zero bytes, and both calls still run.
  Clip call. The clip routine miClipSpans (local GOT slot gp-31140, which holds 0x15fe2a0, `jalr` at 0x161ba68) is called with a0 = Q, a1 = saved a3 (the points), a2 = saved a4 (the widths), a3 = nInit, a4 = area2, a5 = area1 and a6 = saved a5 (the sort flag, loaded in the delay slot at 0x161ba6c). Its return value (v0) is moved to a4 at 0x161ba78.
  Forward call. The fill routine F is called (`jalr` at 0x161ba84) with a0 = saved a1 (the second argument), a1 = saved a0 (the first argument), a2 = area2, a3 = area1 (in the delay slot at 0x161ba88), and a4 = the count from miClipSpans.
  Return values. The forward call's result is v0 on the main path, because nothing after the call writes v0 (0x161ba8c and the exit at 0x161ba44). On the failed-allocation path v0 is 4 times i (0x161b91c, 0x161b928, 0x161ba2c). The routine has no loop with a store through a pointer other than its frame, and no branch-likely branch.
- `nfbInfPolyFillRect..AL` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1566090, 432 bytes): **established.**
  Rectangle-fill wrapper of the "Inf" layer. It either hands the call to a fill routine in the drawable's private record (the "B" path below) or runs a first routine, nfbInfRender..EK, under a re-entry guard, and then, when a second flag is set, a routine held in the GC's private record. Arguments by register: a0 is the drawable (its word at +132 is a private array), a1 is the GC (its word at +76 is a private array, and its word at +16 has bit 21, 0x200000, cleared during the first call), a2 and a3 are passed on (a2 is also kept in a5, a3 in a6), a4 is passed on unchanged, and a7 is not an incoming argument: the routine loads it with the clip Q (Pv+8, 0x15660e4) before either call. The frame is 64 bytes: s1 at sp+16, s2 at sp+32, s0 at sp+24, gp at sp+40, ra at sp+48, Q at sp+8, and the ninth argument at sp+0.
  Records. The index i is the word at gp-6768 (0x1738f50). The word at a1+76 plus 4i is the GC record Pv (0x15660cc). Pv+8 is the clip Q, Pv+24 is a bit mask, Pv+28 is a routine pointer P2, and the word at Pv+32 is a record Z whose word at +44 is a routine pointer F3. The index of the drawable's private array is the word at gp-5736 (0x1739358), so the drawable's word for it is at a0+132 plus 4 times that index (0x1566124 to 0x1566140, the word `at`).
  Enable test. The re-entry guard G1 is the word at gp+19112 (0x173f468). The routine takes the first path only when G1 is zero and bit (the byte at a0+2, low five bits) of the word at Pv+24 is set (`bnez` at 0x15660e0, `beqz` at 0x15660f8).
  Fall-back path. Otherwise it calls F3 (`jalr` at 0x1566230) with a0 = a0, a1 = a1, a2 = saved a2, a3 = saved a3, a4 unchanged, a5 = saved a2, a6 = saved a3 and a7 = Q, and returns F3's v0 (0x1566238 to 0x1566214).
  First path. It sets G1 to 1 (0x1566100), stores a2 and a3 in the words at gp+19080 and gp+19084 (0x1566104, 0x1566110) and clears bit 21 of a1+16 (0x1566108 to 0x156611c). It then calls nfbInfRender..EK (local GOT slot gp-29908, which holds 0x1690aa0; `jalr` at 0x1566148) with a0 = a0, a1 = a1, a2 = a0, a3 = the word at Z+44, a4 unchanged, a5 = saved a2, a6 = saved a3, a7 = Q, and the ninth argument (sp+0) = the drawable's private word `at`. After the call it restores Q to Pv+8 (0x1566154, 0x1566158), sets bit 21 of a1+16 again (0x156615c to 0x156616c), clears G1 (0x1566174) and stores `at` back into the drawable's private word (0x1566188).
  Second flag. The word at gp+19116 (0x173f46c, flag G2) is read after the call (0x1566150). If it is zero (`beqz` at 0x1566184) the routine returns nfbInfRender's v0 (0x1566200). Otherwise it copies a1+24 into Pv+40 and Pv+64, a1+28 into Pv+44 and Pv+68, a1+20 into Pv+52 and Pv+76, and the byte at a1+5 into Pv+48 and Pv+72 (0x15661a0 to 0x15661d0). It then calls P2 (the word at Pv+28, `jalr` at 0x15661d8) with a0 = a1, a1 = a0 (the drawable), a2 = the drawable's private array word address (a0+132 plus 4 times the index), a3 = G2's value, a4 = 4 times the index, a5 = (a1+16) | 0x200000, a6 = 0x200000 and a7 = Q. The delay slot (0x15661dc) stores the byte at Pv+72. After the call G2 is cleared (0x15661e0) and the routine returns P2's v0.
  Return values. On the first path with G2 zero, nfbInfRender's v0. With G2 nonzero, P2's v0. On the fall-back path, F3's v0. Nothing after each call writes v0.
  No branch-likely branch. No unreached code.
- `nfbInfPolySegment..MK` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15833c0, 432 bytes): **established.**
  The same body as `nfbInfPolyFillRect..AL` (0x1566090, above), instruction for instruction apart from the address-relative gp immediates below, with the same records, guards, flags and calls, except for these addresses. The first call (nfbInfRender..EK, `jalr` at 0x1583478) takes its a3 from the word at Z+28 (`lw` at 0x1583464), where Z is the word at Pv+32, not Z+44. The fall-back routine F3 is the word at Z+28 (`lw` at 0x1583550), and the fall-back code starts at 0x158354c, the target of the branches at 0x1583410 and 0x1583428. The second-flag test is `beqz` at 0x15834b4 with its branch to 0x1583530, and the `b` at 0x1583568 goes to 0x1583534. The `lui` at 0x15833c8 and `addiu` at 0x15833d0 hold different immediates (0x1b and 30208 against 0x1d and 18736 in the template) because they are relative to this routine's address; they produce the same gp, so the gp slots are the same as in the template. Return values are as above: nfbInfRender..EK's v0 on the zero-flag exit, P2's v0 on the other exit, and F3's v0 on the fall-back.
- `nfbInfPolyArc..OK` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15929d0, 432 bytes): **established.**
  The same body as `nfbInfPolyFillRect..AL` (0x1566090), instruction for instruction, except for the offset taken from Z (the word at Pv+32): it is +36 instead of +44, and the `lui` at 0x15929d8 and `addiu` at 0x15929e0 hold address-relative immediates (0x1a and 32752) that give the same gp. The first call (nfbInfRender..EK, `jalr` at 0x1592a88) takes its a3 from the word at Z+36 (`lw` at 0x1592a74). The fall-back routine F3 is the word at Z+36 (`lw` at 0x1592b60), and the fall-back code starts at 0x1592b5c, the target of the branches at 0x1592a20 and 0x1592a38. The second-flag test is `beqz` at 0x1592ac4, with its branch to 0x1592b40, and the `b` at 0x1592b78 goes to 0x1592b44. Otherwise the body and the return values are as in `nfbInfPolyFillRect..AL`.
- `nfbInfPolyRectangle..NK` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x166b4e0, 432 bytes): **established.**
  The same body as `nfbInfPolyFillRect..AL` (0x1566090), instruction for instruction, except for the offset taken from Z (the word at Pv+32): it is +32 instead of +44, and the `lui` at 0x166b4e8 and `addiu` at 0x166b4f0 hold address-relative immediates (0xd and -2848) that give the same gp. The fall-back routine F3 is the word at Z+32 (`lw` at 0x166b670), and the fall-back code starts at 0x166b66c, the target of the branches at 0x166b530 and 0x166b548. The second-flag test is `beqz` at 0x166b5d4, with its branch to 0x166b650, and the `b` at 0x166b688 goes to 0x166b654. Otherwise the body and the return values are as in `nfbInfPolyFillRect..AL`.
- `nfbInfPolyFillArc..BL` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16cabc0, 432 bytes): **established.**
  The same body as `nfbInfPolyFillRect..AL` (0x1566090), instruction for instruction, except for the offset taken from Z (the word at Pv+32): it is +48 instead of +44, and the `lui` at 0x16cabc8 and `addiu` at 0x16cabd0 hold address-relative immediates (0x7 and -512) that give the same gp. The first call (nfbInfRender..EK, `jalr` at 0x16cac78) takes its a3 from the word at Z+48 (`lw` at 0x16cac64). The fall-back routine F3 is the word at Z+48 (`lw` at 0x16cad50), and the fall-back code starts at 0x16cad4c, the target of the branches at 0x16cac10 and 0x16cac28. The second-flag test is `beqz` at 0x16cacb4, with its branch to 0x16cad30, and the `b` at 0x16cad68 goes to 0x16cad34. Otherwise the body and the return values are as in `nfbInfPolyFillRect..AL`.

## Colormap, window-visual, shared-memory pixmap and stereo wrapper routines read from whole listings

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so` (N32). Every routine below was read from its whole listing next to its fact sheet, so each statement comes from the binary alone. Offsets are bytes into the record the code addresses; no structure name is claimed beyond what the code proves, and a name in parentheses (screen, window) says only what the surrounding routines treat the pointer as.

- `nfbInstallHWColormap` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1592630, 916 bytes): **established.**
  Makes a colormap the occupant of a hardware colormap slot, evicting the least recently used occupant when it has none (argument a0, called C; V is the word at C+12, the screen, and R the word at V+116). The record A for the colormap's layer is the 24-byte entry k of the table at the word at R+124, where k is the word at +4 of the 20-byte entry of the table at R+88 selected by the word at +0 of the word at C+0 minus the word at R+80 (loads at 0x1592634 to 0x1592684). A holds a count n at +0, a pointer B at +4 to n pairs of words and an index L at +8; the pairs' second words link them into a chain by pair number. The colormap's slot number s is the word at +0 of the record at C+68.
  Already installed (s not negative, tested at 0x15926a0): it finds the pair i whose first word is s (loop at 0x15926c0), then the pair j whose second word is i (loop at 0x15926f0), and returns when L equals i (0x1592708). Otherwise it takes i out of the chain (pair j receives i's second word), puts it after pair L (i takes L's second word, L's second word becomes i) and stores i in A+8; the last store is in the delay slot of the return at 0x1592754. A miss in the first search leaves the index to a reload of the stack word at 0(sp), which the routine never stores. The routine sets no result on this path.
  Not installed (branch at 0x15926a0 to 0x1592780): the victim is the second word of pair L, e; A+8 becomes e and the victim's first word, a slot number, is stored in the record at C+68 so that C takes that slot. Q is the 28-byte record for that slot in the table at the word at R+116; its word at +0 names the previous occupant O. When O is nonzero the routine calls `WalkTree` with V, `TellLostMap` and O+8 (`jalr` at 0x15927e8) and stores -1 in the word at +0 of O's record at +68. It then repeats that for each other occupant listed in Q: the word at Q+4 counts them and the pointer at Q+8 lists their slot numbers (loop from 0x1592828). For each occupant the slot's 28-byte entry names it calls `WalkTree` again (`jalr` at 0x1592894), stores -1 in the word at +0 of its record at +68, stores 0 in the word at +0 of the slot's entry and, when the pointer at +20 of the 24-byte layer record selected for that occupant (by the same lookup as A) is nonzero, calls it with the occupant (`jalr` at 0x1592900). After that, when the word at +48 of the screen's record (the word at V+436 plus 4 times `rrmScreenPrivateIndex`, loaded at 0x1592938) is nonzero and the word at Q+20 is zero, it calls the local routine `SetReservedID` with V and the word at Q+16 (`jalr` at 0x15929ac) and stores 1 at Q+20. Last it calls the function at +12 of A with C (`jalr` at 0x1592970), stores C in the word at Q+0 (0x1592988) and calls `WalkTree` with V, the local routine `nfbTellGainedMap` (at 0x15e6380) and C (`jalr` at 0x159298c). Its result is that call's result.

- `nfbHWStoreColors` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15a08c0, 660 bytes): **established.**
  Records which colour cells a store touches, then hands the store to the layer's own routine (arguments: a0 the colormap C, a1 the item count n, a2 the array of 12-byte items). The routine called is the pointer at +16 of the 24-byte record of the table at the word at R+124, where R is the word at +116 of the screen at C+12 and the record is chosen as in `nfbInstallHWColormap` (loads at 0x15a08c8 to 0x15a0958); it is saved at 48(sp) and called last with a0 to a2 unchanged, a5 set to the address D (the word at C+68 plus 4) and a7 set to 1 (`jalr` at 0x15a0a54, the result returned). D is a bitmap of changed cells. When the word at C+68 is zero, or n is not above 0, the bitmap is left alone. The halfword at +4 of the word at C+0 selects the layout: when it equals 5 each item's first word is split by three masks, the words at +12, +16 and +20 of that record, each shifted right by the word at +24, +28 or +32, and the three resulting cell numbers set one bit apiece in three consecutive bitmaps of w words each (red first at D, then green at D plus 4w and blue at D plus 8w), where w is ((the halfword at +8 of that record plus 7) >> 3, plus 3) >> 2 with signed shifts, the number of words that hold one bit per value of that halfword (8 when it is 256); this loop is not unrolled. Otherwise it treats the first word of each item as the cell number and sets bit (number mod 32) of word (number >> 5) of D, in a loop for the n mod 4 leading items followed by a four-item unrolled loop that advances 48 bytes a pass and issues `pref` hints.

- `nfbValidateWindowVisual` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15ba2a0, 432 bytes): **established.**
  Re-asserts a window's visual after its attributes changed (argument a0, the window W). It returns without work when the word at W+52 is nonzero and the word at +4 of the record it names is zero (0x15ba2dc). Otherwise it takes F as the word at +4 of the window private found at the word at W+132 plus 4 times the word at address 0x1739358, V as the word at W+16 and R as the word at V+116. When the halfword at W+100 is nonzero, or the word at W+120 and the word at +44 of the record it names are both nonzero, it calls three functions of the screen record V in turn on the 12-byte entry F of the table at R+92: the one at +344 with the entry, W+56 and the word at W+52 (0x15ba33c), the one at +364 with the entry twice and W+72 (0x15ba354) and the one at +360 with the entry twice and W+44 (0x15ba36c). Otherwise it calls only the one at +344 with the entry and W+44 (0x15ba440). It then zeroes the words of the array at R+60, the count being the word at R+40 (a two-way unrolled loop), stores 1 in the word of that array selected by the word of the table at R+48 indexed by F (0x15ba3f4), and returns the result of the function at +108 of R called with V (0x15ba3fc); the early return leaves 0.

- `nfbCreateStereoGC` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16f6a50, 660 bytes): **established.**
  Initialises a graphics context for a stereo window (argument a0, the GC). It selects by the byte at GC+4 through a 31-entry jump table at 0x1729e44: entry 1 goes to the one-bit case at 0x16f6ab8, entries 2, 4, 8, 12, 15, 16, 24 and 30 to the case at 0x16f6b50, and every other value, or a byte above 30, logs "nfbCreateGC: unsupported depth: %d" with `ErrorF` and returns 0. Both cases store 0 in GC+56, store two addresses formed from gp-27584 in GC+68 and GC+72 (offsets +19848 and +19880 in the first case, +18760 and +18232 in the second) and set the word at GC+16 to its value with bits 0xc0000 cleared and 0x20000 set. P is the word of the array at the word at GC+76 selected by a global index word: the word at address 0x173f52c in the one-bit case and the word at 0x1738f50 in the other. The one-bit case calls `mfbReduceRop` with the byte at GC+5, the word at GC+24 and P, stores the result as a byte at P+0, clears the word at P+4, stores the address of `mfbSolidWhiteArea` (gp slot -31284) at P+12, sets bit 7 (0x80) and clears bit 6 of the word at P+0, and returns 1. The other case copies 28 words (112 bytes, in seven groups of four) from the table at the word at +132 of the record at +116 of the word at GC+0 into P and returns 1.

- `nfbShmCreatePixmap` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15f7b60, 424 bytes): **established.**
  Builds a pixmap record around memory the caller supplies (arguments: a0 the screen, a1 the width, a2 the height, a3 the depth, a4 the data pointer). The depth selects the bits per pixel through a 31-entry jump table at 0x1729f80: depth 1 gives 1, depths 2, 4 and 8 give 8, depths 12, 15 and 16 give 16, depths 24 and 30 give 32, and every other depth logs "nfbShmCreatePixmap, unsupported depth %d" with `ErrorF` and returns 0. It allocates 40 bytes with `Xalloc` and returns 0 when that fails. It stores a4 at +32, a0 at +16, the width at +12 and the height at +14 (halfwords), the depth at +2 and the bits per pixel at +3 (bytes), 1 at +0 (byte) and 1 at +24 (word), and zeros at +1 (byte), +4 (word), +8 and +10 (halfwords). The word at +20 is the incremented `globalSerialNumber`, or 1 once that would exceed 0x10000000 (stored back through the same pointer). The word at +28 is the row size: the entry of `PixmapWidthPaddingInfo` for the depth (12 bytes per depth) gives an added constant, a right shift and a left shift, applied to the width as ((width plus constant) >> shift1) << shift2. It returns the pixmap.

- `nfbStereoPolyText16` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15b2ed0, 468 bytes): **established.**
  Draws a 16-bit text string once for each stereo eye through the GC's saved drawing operations (arguments: a0 the drawable, a1 the GC, a2 to a5 passed on unchanged). G is the word of the array at the word at a1+76 selected by the word at address 0x1738f50, and Y is the halfword at +226 of R, the word at +116 of the screen at a0+16. It saves a1+72 and the word at G+8, replaces a1+72 with the word at G+92 (store at 0x15b2f28), stores 1 at G+104 and tests the word at R+220. When that word is not 1 it stores the word at G+100 in G+8, adds Y to the halfword at a0+10 and to the halfword at G+90, calls the function at +56 of the table at a1+72 (`jalr` at 0x15b2f98), stores the word at G+96 in G+8, subtracts Y from both halfwords and calls the same function again (0x15b2fdc). When that word is 1 the same two calls happen in the other order, G+96 with Y subtracted first (call at 0x15b3050) and G+100 with Y added second (0x15b3094). It restores G+8 and a1+72, clears G+104 and returns the second call's result.

- `nfbStereoPolyText8` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1603000, 468 bytes): **established.**
  The 8-bit text twin of the 16-bit routine at 0x15b2ed0. Its 117 instructions differ from that routine in the gp-forming immediates, the address of its alternate branch (to 0x1603158) and one slot number: both draw calls go through the function at +52 of the table at a1+72, not +56 (the four `jalr` are at 0x16030c8, 0x160310c, 0x1603180 and 0x16031c4). The saves, the use of the words at G+92, G+96, G+100, G+104 and G+8, the halfwords at a0+10 and G+90, the shift Y and the ordering by the word at R+220 are otherwise as in that routine, and so is the result.

- `nfbStereoImageText16` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x156e390, 468 bytes): **established.**
  The image-text counterpart of the 16-bit routine at 0x15b2ed0: the same 117 instructions except the gp-forming immediates, the alternate-branch target (0x156e4e8) and the slot, which is +64 of the table at a1+72 for all four calls (`jalr` at 0x156e458, 0x156e49c, 0x156e510 and 0x156e554). ## Stereo wrappers for the rectangle, rectangle-outline and segment draws

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so` (N32). Each routine was read from its whole listing next to its fact sheet and compared against the other two wrappers of this shape, so each statement comes from the binary alone.

- `nfbStereoPolyFillRect` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16baa30, 676 bytes): **established.**
  Draws filled rectangles once for each stereo eye through the GC's saved drawing operations. Arguments: a0 the drawable D, a1 the GC, a2 the count n, a3 the array P of rectangles, 8 bytes each. G is the word of the array at the word at a1+76 selected by the word at address 0x1738f50 (loaded at 0x16baa78); Y is the halfword at +226 of R, the word at +116 of the screen at D+16. It saves a1+72 at 48(sp), 8*n at 56(sp) and the word at G+8 at 32(sp), replaces a1+72 with the word at G+92 (store at 0x16baab8) and calls `Xalloc` with 8*n (the `jalr` at 0x16baac0; the store of 1 into G+104 is in its delay slot). The result of `Xalloc` is not tested for null.
  When n is above 0 (`blez` at 0x16baad4) it copies P into the new buffer. If n is odd (the `beqz` at 0x16baae8 skips this when it is even) it copies one record first, with a write prefetch at +256 of the new buffer and a read prefetch at +256 of P. Then n>>1 passes of 16 bytes (loop head 0x16bab30, back branch 0x16bab74) copy two records each. Each record is copied halfword by halfword and the stores go in the order +6, +4, +2, +0 for the first record of a pass, then +14, +12, +10, +8 for the second in the pair loop; the loads are the unsigned halfword at +6 and +4 and the signed halfword at +2 and +0 (+14, +12 unsigned, +10 and +8 signed in the second record).
  It then compares the word at R+220 with 1 (`bne` at 0x16bab88). When it is 1, it stores the word at G+96 in G+8, subtracts Y from the halfwords at D+10 and G+90 and calls the function at +44 of the table at a1+72 with D, the GC, n and the copy (`jalr` at 0x16babc4); it then stores the word at G+100 in G+8, adds Y to both halfwords and makes the same call with P itself (0x16bac00). Otherwise the order is reversed: G+100 and Y added with the copy first (0x16bac44), then G+96 and Y subtracted with P (0x16bac80). Last it calls `Xfree` on the copy (`jalr` at 0x16baca4; the delay slot stores the saved word back into G+8), restores a1+72, clears G+104 (store at 0x16bacc0) and returns what `Xfree` returned.

- `nfbStereoPolyRectangle` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16acf50, 676 bytes): **established.**

If n is odd (the `beqz` at 0x16ad008 skips this when it is even) it copies one record first, with a write prefetch at +256 of the new buffer and a read prefetch at +256 of P. - `nfbComputeDidFlag..IG` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x166b990, 448 bytes): **established.**
  Sets or clears bit 2 (value 4) of the flags word at +32 of the second argument by comparing two windows' records (arguments: a0 the window W, a1 the object O whose flags word is written, a3 a second object T; a2 is not read). N is the word at W+24. The new bit is `v0`, 0 or 4, and the routine returns it; the store is `(old & ~4) | v0` into O+32 (stores at 0x166ba38, 0x166ba5c).
  Clear case: when bit 10 (0x400) of the word at W+124 is clear (the shift by 21 and `bgez` at 0x166b9a4), or N is zero (`beqz` at 0x166b9b4), `v0` is 0 and the bit is cleared (code at 0x166ba48).

The first is the one at W+120 and the second the one at N+120; the word at +0 of each is taken and `v0` is 0 when they are equal and 4 when they differ (`xor` at 0x166ba90 and `movz` at 0x166ba98, the delay slot of the branch at 0x166ba94). A missing record at W+120 calls the same slot with W (0x166bab4) and one at N+120 calls it with N (0x166bae0).

## 32-bit block-transfer routines read from the listing

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so` (N32). Read from the whole listing next to the fact sheet, so each statement comes from the binary alone. The 32-bit layer's block-transfer routines are the generated forms of one routine, one per raster operation.

- `cfb32DoBitbltOr` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16920f0, 2540 bytes): **inferred.**
  Copies a list of rectangles between two 32-bit-per-pixel drawables, combining each source word into the destination with a bitwise or (arguments: a0 the source drawable, a1 the second drawable, whose pixmap base is the one the words are combined into, a3 a region record, a4 an array of source points; a2 is saved as the caller's frame pointer, not used as an argument). Read in full for set-up, rectangle selection, overlap handling and the exit; the word loops were read for their loads, ors, stores and table dispatch, but the pointer arithmetic that picks each row's start and the group counts was not traced instruction by instruction, which is why the grade is inferred. Frame 192 bytes, with s0 to s7, gp and ra saved by `sd` below a frame pointer in s8; the only exit is the `jr ra` at 0x1692478, which restores them, and the routine returns no value (v0 is 1 on the three early exits and otherwise whatever the last instruction left).
  Drawable lookup: the byte at +0 of each drawable is compared with 1 (0x1692118, 0x1692150); a 1 means the drawable is itself the pixmap. Otherwise the pixmap is the entry of the array at the word at (screen + 436), indexed by the global word at 0x173f51c, where the screen is the word at drawable+16 (loads at 0x1692120 to 0x1692138 and 0x1692158 to 0x1692174). From each pixmap it takes the word at +28, the row pitch in bytes, shifted right two to give words (s6 for the source, s5 for the destination), and the word at +32, the pixel base (stored at sp-side slot -184 from s8 for the source, held in gp for the destination).
  Rectangles: the region's word at +8 is a data pointer; when it is nonzero the count is the word at data+4 (stopping at once when it is not positive) and the rectangles are 8-byte records of four halfwords from data+8, and when it is zero the one rectangle is the region record itself (0x16923d4, 0x16923dc). The point array a4 has one 4-byte record (x then y halfword) per rectangle, and its end is kept at -88 from s8.
  Direction: the copy runs top to bottom and left to right (the words at -176 and -168 from s8, both 1) unless source and destination can overlap. That is when the two drawable pointers are equal (0x1692180) or both bytes are 0, windows of the one screen (0x1692188 to 0x16921f4); then the first rectangle's top (halfword at data+10) is compared with the first source point's y (0x1692200 to 0x1692210) and, when the rectangles are bands of equal top, the x of the first rectangle with the first point's x (0x16922b8 to 0x16922c0). Two stack buffers of eight and four bytes per rectangle are carved with `subu sp` (a zero result from that exits through 0x1692448) and the loops at 0x16924b8 and 0x1692550 copy the rectangles and points into them with whole bands of equal top reversed in order, the loops at 0x1692364 and 0x1692510 finding the band ends by the halfword at +2; the direction words become -1 where the bands are reversed or the x copy must run right to left.
  Per rectangle (0x1692800): the width is x2 minus x1 (halfwords at +4 and +0), the height y2 minus y1; the source and destination first-row addresses are formed from the row pitches in words with `mult` by the row (the last row for upward copies, selected with `movz` on the direction word) and the x origins; a width of 2 or more sets the word count s7 to the width and the end mask t1 to 0 (`slti`, then `movz s7` and `movz t1` at 0x1692854 and 0x1692874), while a width below 2 leaves s7 at 0 (no word loop) and t1 at its per-rectangle value of -1, so that one word is merged under the all-ones mask. Ascending rows go to the loop at 0x16925d8, descending rows to 0x16928e0. The number of leading words is the width and 7 (`andi s0,s7,0x7`); they are copied through the jump table at 0x1728dd4 (eight entries, 0x16926d8 for none down to 0x1692668 for seven, jump at 0x16927d8), each step `lw` source, `lw` destination, `or`, `sw` at negative offsets from the biased pointers; the loop at 0x16926f0 then does eight such words per pass (32 bytes, the `or` at 0x16926fc to 0x1692770). A nonzero end mask t1 then merges one more word, destination = destination or (source and t1) (0x1692784 to 0x1692794). Descending rows use the mirror image: the table at 0x1728df4 (jump at 0x1692ab0, entries 0x16929b0 down to 0x1692940) and the loop at 0x16929c0 stepping -32. Each row ends by adding the pitches, and the rectangle loop ends when the point pointer reaches the end at -88 from s8 (0x16927f4 and 0x1692a58).

- `cfb32DoBitbltXor` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1669340, 2540 bytes): **inferred.**
  The exclusive-or form of the routine at 0x16920f0. A raw diff of the two listings shows 103 lines of the other routine changed. Compared with branch targets taken as offsets from the routine start, they are of four kinds only: the address immediates (`lui v1,0xd` and `addiu v1,v1,5760` where the other has 0xb and -30512, and the table offsets -28368 and -28336 where it has -29228 and -29196); the branch targets, shifted by 8 from 0x1669590 on; every combining `or` (the 32 in the other routine, including the ones in the eight-word loop at 0x16698c4 to 0x1669924, the unrolled entries and the end-mask merge, which is the `xor` at 0x1669cd4 here) is an `xor`; the 33rd `xor` in this routine is the direction-word flip at 0x1669ae4, which the other routine has at 0x1692894, so the counts are 32 combining operations in each, giving destination = destination xor (source and mask); and, fourth, one `li a7,1` sits elsewhere: in the other routine it is the delay slot of the branch at 0x16922c4 (at 0x16922c8, so it runs whether or not that branch is taken), here it is at the branch target 0x1669820, which the corresponding branch at 0x1669514 reaches and which then jumps on to 0x166967c. The drawable lookup, the rectangle walk, the overlap handling, the direction words and the buffers are as in the other routine. Its tables are the leading-words table at 0x1729130 (jump at 0x1669a28, entries 0x166992c down to 0x16698bc) and the descending table at 0x1729150 (jump at 0x1669d00, entries 0x1669c00 down to 0x1669b90); the exit is the `jr ra` at 0x16696c0; the 32 `or` against 33 `xor` instruction counts differ only by that shared direction-word `xor`, not in structure. Inferred for the same reason as that routine.

## Span-group and polygon fill routines read from the listing

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so` (N32), read from the whole listing next to the fact sheet. The fact sheet labels some calls through `s0` by the slot of the nearest GOT load; the callees below were taken from the GOT loads themselves (`Xfree` is global slot 1139, loaded into s0 at 0x15d07cc, 0x15d0a18, 0x15d0d98 and 0x15d0e00).

- `miFillUniqueSpanGroup` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15d06e0, 2540 bytes): **inferred.**
  Turns a group of span lists that may overlap into one list in which no span overlaps another in its row, then draws it with the graphics context's fill-spans entry (arguments: a0 the drawable and a1 the graphics context, both passed on to the draw call unchanged; a2 a group record: word +4 the number of span lists, +8 a pointer to an array of 12-byte list records (count, points, widths), +12 the smallest y, +16 the largest y). Read in full for the control flow, allocation, failure and exit paths; the unrolled row-initialisation loops (0x15d0868 and 0x15d08d0 to 0x15d09a8) and the merge loop at 0x15d0fd0 were read for their stores and tests, not traced register by register, which is why the grade is inferred. Frame 320 bytes.
  Group of no lists (the word at a2+4 is 0): the branch at 0x15d0720 goes straight to the register-restore epilogue at 0x15d0804 (its `jr ra` is at 0x15d0830), touching nothing; v0 still holds the zero count loaded at 0x15d0708, so it returns 0. Group of one list (a2+4 equals 1, 0x15d072c): the draw call is made at once through the table at the context word +72, its first entry (loads at 0x15d0d78 to 0x15d0d80), with a2 the count, a3 the points and a4 the widths of the one list (words 0, 4 and 8 of the record) and a5 = 1; then `Xfree` (slot 1139) is called on that list's points and widths and the group is reset: the word +4 is zeroed, +12 set to 32767 and +16 to -32767, and the routine returns with v0 = -32767 (the `li` at 0x15d0ddc, not a computed result).
  General case: rows = largest y minus smallest y plus 1 (s2). Four scratch areas are carved from the stack with `subu sp` rounded to 16 bytes: 12 bytes per row (a record of count, points pointer and widths pointer), 4 bytes per row (the capacity), and 64 bytes per row twice (initial points and widths, sixteen entries of four bytes each). If any of the four comes back zero the fallback at 0x15d07c0 calls `Xfree` on every list's points and widths (loop at 0x15d07d0 to 0x15d07fc over 12-byte records) and goes to the epilogue at 0x15d0830. Otherwise each row's record is set to count 0, points at its 64-byte slice, widths at its 64-byte slice, and its capacity word to 16, unrolled four rows per pass (the remainder, rows and 3, first, at 0x15d0868).
  Distribution: for each list (record step 12; a list with count 0 is only freed) and for each point in it, y minus the smallest y selects the row; points outside 0 to rows-1 are skipped. When the row's count differs from its capacity the point's two halfwords and the width word are appended at index count and the count is incremented (0x15d0a50 to 0x15d0a78). When it is full the capacity becomes 2*capacity+16 (t8 at 0x15d0acc and 0x15d0ad8). If the old capacity is still 16, the row is on the stack slice: two `Xalloc` calls (global slot 1143, at 0x15d0c78 and 0x15d0c94) for the new points and widths, eight doubleword copies with `ldl` and `ldr` pairs for the 64 bytes of points (0x15d0cc8 to 0x15d0d44) and a `memcpy` (slot 1250, 64 bytes) for the widths. Otherwise two `Xrealloc` calls (global slot 1040, at 0x15d0b00 and 0x15d0b20) grow the heap buffers. A zero result from either allocation goes to the clean-up at 0x15d0bcc: `Xfree` of the points and widths of each row whose capacity is not 16, then of each list's buffers, then the epilogue at 0x15d0808, without drawing. After the points of a list are consumed, its points and widths are freed (0x15d0b74 and 0x15d0b90) and its count and widths pointer zeroed (0x15d0b8c, 0x15d0b9c), and the number of points taken is added to a running total kept at -136 from s8.
  Output: two more stack areas of 4 bytes per counted point (points at -112 from s8, widths at -128). If either is zero the rows' heap buffers are freed and the routine ends at 0x15d0808 without drawing (0x15d0e44 to 0x15d0e94). Otherwise each row is walked in order: a row of one point is copied to the output (0x15d0ed4), a longer row is sorted with `QuickSortSpansX..CCB` (local GOT entry 448, 0x1617710, `jalr` at 0x15d0f64; a0 the points, a1 the widths, a2 the count) and then merged: the running span's right end is its x plus width, and the next point extends it to the larger right end when the next x is not greater than that end (`slt` at 0x15d0fdc, `movn` at 0x15d0ffc), otherwise the running span is emitted and a new one starts; the last span of the row is emitted at 0x15d1000 to 0x15d1018. Every row whose capacity is not 16 then has its two heap buffers freed (0x15d0ef0 to 0x15d0f10). Finally the draw call is made through the context's table at +72, first entry (0x15d1034 to 0x15d1044): a0 and a1 the original drawable and context, a2 the output count, a3 the output points, a4 the output widths, a5 = 1. The group is reset as in the one-list case (0x15d0dd0 to 0x15d0ddc) and the routine returns with v0 = -32767.
