The three depth branches, the single-pixel shortcut and the exits were read in full; the masked word-merge sequences inside the two long loops were read for their table loads and logic and not traced instruction by instruction, so the grade is inferred. Its error string at 0x1730ab0 reads "cfbGetSpans: invalid depth", the shared message of the cfb layers, not a 16-bit-specific one.
  Depth dispatch: the byte at drawable+3 (bits per pixel) is compared with 1 (0x16e1800, to the one-bit path at 0x16e1dd0) and 16 (0x16e1808, to 0x16e1834). Anything else calls `FatalError` (global GOT slot 1127, `jalr` at 0x16e1820, a0 the string at the local-GOT page plus 24048) and, if that returns, falls into the 16-bit path.
  Sixteen-bit path (0x16e1834): the drawable is used directly when its byte at +0 is 1 (a pixmap, 0x16e1850) and otherwise its pixmap comes from the array at the word at (screen + 436), indexed by the global word at gp-relative 19304 (0x16e183c to 0x16e1868); the row pitch is the word at +28 shifted right two (words, kept in s8) and the pixel base the word at +32 (t2). A call with exactly one span (s2 equal to 1, 0x16e1878) whose width word equals 1 (0x16e1d30, 0x16e1d34) takes the shortcut at 0x16e1d3c to 0x16e1d94: it multiplies y by the pitch in words, adds x as halfwords, loads the one 16-bit pixel with `lhu`, shifts it left 16 and stores it as the first word of the destination buffer, then returns with the frame restored. A count of zero or less goes to the epilogue at 0x16e1dc0 (0x16e1880 to 0x16e1d98). Otherwise each span (loop at 0x16e18f8) forms its first source word as base + pitch*y + (x >> 1) words and clips the end to the pixel limit in t3 (twice the pitch, the `slt` and `movn` at 0x16e1910 and 0x16e191c); an odd x starts in the middle of a word. Two spans' worth of mask-table entries are read from the global table whose page is the local-GOT word at gp-relative -28680 (offsets 24112 to 24144) and the span is packed into the destination with the three shapes the code distinguishes by the parity of start and length: a span of one or two pixels (`slti ... 3`, 0x16e1950 and 0x16e1974) merged into one or two destination words (0x16e197c to 0x16e19f0); a longer span starting on a word boundary, copied word by word and finished with a masked partial word; and a longer span starting mid-word, in which each pair of source words is shifted by 16 in each direction to realign (the variable shifts left and right at 0x16e1aa8 to 0x16e1c34). The loop ends when the widths pointer in a3, advanced by four per span, is no longer below the widths array end in s7 (`sltu` at 0x16e1928, tested at 0x16e18ec); the destination advances by whole words (4 times the word count of each span).
  One-bit path (0x16e1dd0): the same drawable lookup (0x16e1dd8 to 0x16e1dec; a zero type byte goes to 0x16e218c, the window lookup at 0x16e1de8), the pitch in words at -120 from s8 and the pixel base at -112, and a loop at 0x16e1f8c with its own mask tables at gp-relative -28680 offsets 19216 to 19952: each span's bits are collected into words of the destination at bit positions of the destination's own alignment, with the leading, whole-word and trailing cases split at the 32-pixel boundary (`andi 0x1f` and `sra 5`, 0x16e1fb4, 0x16e1fdc). A widths pointer (a3) that has reached the widths end in s7 ends the loop (`sltu` at 0x16e1fa8, tested at 0x16e1f84), and exits go to the epilogue at 0x16e1d98.

- `miFillGeneralPoly` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16155c0, 2504 bytes): **inferred.**

It is the usual scan-conversion routine with the standard 44-byte edge records. The set-up, allocation, flush and exit paths were read in full; the two edge-list update and sort bodies (even-odd at 0x16156fc to 0x1615998, winding at 0x1615b40 to 0x1615f84) were read for their loads, stores, comparisons and loop shape, not instruction by instruction, so the grade is inferred.
  Entry: a count below 3 (`slti` at 0x16155d0, branch at 0x16155e8) returns 1 without touching anything (exit 0x1615a80). Otherwise it takes 44 bytes per point from the stack (count times 44, rounded to 16, built by shifts and subtractions at 0x16155f0 to 0x1615610); a zero result returns 0 (0x1615614, exit 0x1615a9c). `miCreateETandAET` (local GOT entry 989, 0x171b010, `jalr` at 0x161563c; a0 the count, a1 the points, a2 the edge-table record at -112 from s8, a3 the active-list record at -88, a4 the stack edge records, a5 the block header at -416) builds the tables; a zero result returns 0 (0x1615644 to 0x1615f40, exit 0x1615f5c). Two buffers sit on the frame: 200 points of four bytes at -2016 from s8 and 200 widths at -1216, the constant 200 held in s4.
  Rule choice: bit 23 of the context word +16 (`sll` 8 then `bltz` at 0x161566c) selects winding, with the loop at 0x1615aa4; clear selects even-odd, the loop at 0x1615684. Each iterates the scanline from the table's smallest y (-108 from s8) to its largest (-112), pulling in the scanline list entries whose y matches (the list at -96, each node: y, edge list, next), and walking the active list from the word at -60.

New edges from the scanline list are merged into the active list by an insertion pass on the word at +4 (0x1615920 to 0x1615998), and after the walk the active list is sorted by the same key with the loop at 0x1615810 to 0x1615868.

Last, the block list from -116 from s8 is freed (`Xfree`, global slot 1139, call at 0x1615a24, next block from the word at +300), and the routine returns 1 (exit 0x1615a64).

## One-bit solid fill routines read from the listing

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so` (N32), read from the listing and fact sheet. The binary alone is the source for these statements.

- `mfbSolidBlackArea` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1672fa0, 2260 bytes): **inferred.**
  Clears the pixels of a list of boxes in a one-bit-per-pixel drawable (arguments: a0 the drawable, a1 the box count, a2 an array of 8-byte boxes of four halfwords x1, y1, x2, y2; the box array end is kept at sp+88; frame 112 bytes, s0 to s8, gp and ra saved by `sd`). The set-up, mask selection, row loops and exit were read; the unrolled whole-word store runs (the eight-word passes ending in `sw zero` at 0x167373c to 0x1673760) and the dozens of tail variants were matched by their stores, not read one by one, so the grade is inferred.
  Drawable: when the byte at +0 is zero (a window, 0x1672fc8) the pixmap is the word at +116 of the word at +16 (0x167379c to 0x16737a8); otherwise the drawable itself. The row pitch in words is the word at +28 shifted right two (s8; ra holds it times four) and the pixel base the word at +32 (stored at sp+96). A count of zero or less returns at 0x1672ff4.
  Box: the first bit is x1 and 31 (v0), the word is x1 shifted right five, the width is x2 minus x1 (t2) and the height y2 minus y1 (t9); the first word address is the base plus 4 times (that word index plus y1 times the pitch in words) (`mult` at 0x16730fc). When v0 plus the width is below 32 (`slti` at 0x1673120, the branch at 0x1673144), the box lies inside one word and its mask is the word of the 32-by-32-entry table at the page named by the local-GOT word at gp-relative -28680, offset 19952, entry (x1 and 31) times 32 plus the width (index from `sll` at 0x1673104 and the add at 0x1673024, scaled by 4 at 0x167302c); the loop at 0x1673060 clears those bits in each row by ANDing with the complement (`nor` at 0x1673058), stepping rows by the pitch. A wider box takes a left mask from the table at offset 19816 indexed by x1 and 31 and a right mask from the table at offset 19344 indexed by x2 and 31 (0x167314c to 0x167316c); a zero left mask means the box starts on a word boundary and a zero right mask means it ends on one, and those partial words are skipped. The partial words are cleared with an AND with the complement, the whole words between them are stored as zero in passes of eight words with the remainder handled first by the 1-to-3 word fix-ups, and each row advances by the pitch; the loop ends at the row count in t9 and then the next box, until the box pointer reaches the end at sp+88 (0x1673864).
  Exit: the `jr ra` at 0x1673794 after restoring the saved registers; no result is set.

- `mfbSolidWhiteArea` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15e5ac0, 2240 bytes): **inferred.**
  The set-the-bits twin of the routine at 0x1672fa0 (same arguments; frame 112 bytes). A line-by-line comparison of the two bodies shows the same structure with these differences: it sets bits (an OR with the mask word taken directly, with no `nor`) where the other clears them; its whole-word stores write -1 (a7, loaded by `li` at 0x15e5b28) where the other writes zero; the pitch in words is held in the pushed value at sp+104 and the base at sp+96 (loaded at 0x15e5b08 and 0x15e5b0c) where the other keeps the pitch in s8 and ra; the box pointer end is at sp+88 as in the other; and the register assignments, the slots the callee-saved registers are saved in (the registers are stored in a different order, so each slot holds a different register from the other routine's) and the placement of the table-page load differ, so its 560 instruction words (28 of them `nop`) line up with the other's 565 (33 `nop`) only in shape; both have 137 `sw` instructions. The window case (0x15e62ac), the box loop (the `b` at 0x15e5b40 into 0x15e6370), the single-word case and the left and right masks follow the same tables (offsets 19952, 19816 and 19344 of the local-GOT page, the loads at 0x15e5b70 and 0x15e5c7c) as in the other routine. Its single exit is the `jr ra` at 0x15e62a4. Inferred for the reason given for the other routine, and because the match of the tail variants is by shape.

## Inf-layer drawing wrappers with a saved-argument block, read from whole listings

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`. These eight wrappers share the skeleton of `nfbInfPolyFillRect..AL` (0x1566090): the GC private record Pv is the word at a1+76 plus 4 times the index at gp-6768, the drawable private index is at gp-5736, the re-entry flag G1 is the word at 0x173f468 and the follow-up flag G2 the word at 0x173f46c (both gp-relative data words), and the record Z is the word at Pv+32. They differ in which Z slot they use, which argument registers they park in the words from 0x173f448 to 0x173f464 (4 bytes apart), and what they hand to the follow-up routine. Each entry says what its own listing does. The wrappers hold no board access; the drawing routines they dispatch to are the ones that write the command window.

- `nfbInfFillSpans..FK` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x156ec80, 440 bytes): **established.**
  Wrapper for the fill-spans request. The inner routine is the word at Z+0 (`lw` at 0x156ed2c, and at 0x156ee18 on the fall-back path). The guard is the usual pair: `bne` at 0x156ecd0 when G1 is set and `beq` at 0x156ece8 when bit (byte a0+2, low five bits) of the word at Pv+24 is clear, both going to the fall-back at 0x156ee14. On the main path it stores 1 into G1 (0x156ecf0), then a5, a4, a2 and a3 into 0x173f454, 19088, 19080 and 19084 (0x156ecf4, 0x156ecf8, 0x156ecfc, 0x156ed08), clears bit 21 of a1+16 (0x156ed00 to 0x156ed14), and calls `nfbInfRender..EK` (GOT slot gp-29908; `jalr` at 0x156ed40) with a0 and a1 unchanged, a2 = a0, a3 = the Z+0 word, a4 and a5 as received and a7 = the caller's a3; the drawable's private word goes to sp+0 and Pv+8 is kept at sp+8. After the call it writes Pv+8 back (0x156ed50), sets bit 21 of a1+16 again, restores the drawable private word from sp+0 (0x156ed74) and clears G1 (0x156ed6c). If G2 is zero (`beq` at 0x156ed7c to 0x156edf8) it returns what `nfbInfRender..EK` returned; otherwise it copies a1+24, a1+28 and a1+20 into Pv+40/64, Pv+44/68 and Pv+52/76, the byte a1+5 into Pv+48 and Pv+72, calls the word at Pv+28 (`jalr` at 0x156edd0, with a0 = a1, a1 = a0, a2 = the address of the drawable private word, a3 = G2, a4 = 4 times the index, a5 = a1+16 with bit 21 set and a6 = 0x200000), clears G2 (0x156edd8) and returns that call's v0. The fall-back (0x156ee14 on) calls the Z+0 word (`jalr` at 0x156ee28) with a2 and a3 as received and returns its v0.
- `nfbInfSetSpans..GK` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1633af0, 440 bytes): **established.**
  Wrapper for the set-spans request, 440 bytes like its fill-spans sibling but with a different register plan: it takes the inner routine from Z+4 (`lw` at 0x1633ba0 and 0x1633c88) and parks six words, a2, a3, a6, a4 and a5 in 0x173f448, 19084, 19096, 19088 and 19092 (0x1633b64 to 0x1633b8c) after setting G1 at 0x1633b60. The guard branches are at 0x1633b40 and 0x1633b58, both to the fall-back at 0x1633c84. The `nfbInfRender..EK` call (`jalr` at 0x1633bb0) gets a2 = a0, a3 = the Z+4 word, a4 = the drawable's private word and a5 = 4 times the drawable index, and the same private word is stored at sp+0 in the delay slot; a6 still holds the flags word of a1+16 with bit 21 cleared. After it, Pv+8 is restored (0x1633bc0) and G1 cleared (0x1633bdc). With G2 zero (`beq` at 0x1633bec to 0x1633c68) it returns `nfbInfRender..EK`'s v0. With G2 set it does the standard copy of a1+24, +28, +20 and +5 into Pv+40/64, +44/68, +52/76 and +48/72; the routine then calls the word at Pv+28 (`jalr` at 0x1633c40) with only a0 = a1 and a1 = a0, and clears G2 at 0x1633c48; the return is that call's v0. - `nfbInfImageGlyphBlt..GL` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16221e0, 440 bytes): **established.**

It saves a2, a3, a6, a4 and a5 in 0x173f448, 19084, 19096, 19088 and 19092 (0x1622254 to 0x162227c) after setting G1 at 0x1622250. Guard branches: 0x1622230 (G1 set) and 0x1622248 (mask bit clear), both to 0x1622374. The `nfbInfRender..EK` call is at 0x16222a0, with a3 = the Z+68 word, a4 = the drawable private word and a5 = 4 times the index. Parked words: a2, a3, a6, a4, a5 into 0x173f448, 19084, 19096, 19088, 19092 (0x171f4f4 to 0x171f51c); G1 is set at 0x171f4f0 and cleared at 0x171f56c. The fall-back `jalr` is at 0x171f628 and its `b` at 0x171f630 lands on 0x171f5fc. ## Solid rectangle and span fills that write the command window

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`. The command-window pointer record B is the word at +16 of the window's device record, indexed by the module's board index (gp-5732), then taken from the table behind +436; the pointer through which every word is sent is the word at B+0, reloaded before each store. The graphics context record C is the word that gp-6776 indexes through B+92. Command words are given as the 32-bit values stored. Most of the colour and window-descriptor code is shared with the libGLcore driver, which sends the same words (`docs/opengl.md`); this section records only what these two listings do.

- `odsyDrawSolidRectsSingleBuffer..HE` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1580da0, 7072 bytes): **inferred.** The prologue, the descriptor refresh, the buffer-select decision, the dispatch, the common tail and the vertex loop were read as listings; the colour paths were read as command-word sequences with the arithmetic checked at the stores named below, and what the state words mean for the board is inferred from `docs/opengl.md`.
  Draws a list of filled rectangles in a window with one colour buffer. Arguments by register: a0 the rectangle array (8 bytes per entry, four signed halfwords x1, y1, x2, y2 at +0, +2, +4, +6), a1 the rectangle count, a2 the pixel value, a3 the raster operation, a4 the plane mask (inferred: it is split into the colour-field bit groups of block 0x11023), a5 the window; a6 and a7 are not arguments. A zero count returns at once (`beq` at 0x1580e34 to 0x15817f4), with C+132 cleared in the delay slot.
  Window descriptor (0x1580e44 to 0x1580f8c). The clip box held in B (halfwords +96 to +102) is compared with the copy at +108 to +114. When it differs the routine copies it over and rebuilds the eight descriptor words at C+11072 to C+11100: x0 shifted left 12 with y0 in the low 12 bits, x1 minus 1 shifted left 12 with y1 minus 1, then 0xffffff, 0, 0xffffff, 0, 0xffffff, 0 for the three unused boxes. It sends the settle group (0x10443, 250, 0x10046, 0x10046) first when C+7352 is nonzero, clearing it, and then block 0x1204b with the eight words.
  Buffer select (0x1580f94 to 0x15813a8). The byte at +1 of the window's private-array slot that `dbcWindowPrivateIndex` selects (the slot word itself, not a record it points to) chooses the operand cached at C+11112. Byte -2 or 0, and any value not named here, give 3, or 12 when the window's pixel mode is 7; byte -3 or 1 gives 12 whatever the mode; byte -1 gives 15 when the word at +12 of the window's 68-byte table entry is 2 and 3 otherwise, then 12 when the pixel mode is 7. The branches are at 0x1580fac, 0x1580fb4, 0x1580fbc, 0x1580fc4, 0x1580fdc and 0x1580ff0. When the chosen value equals C+11112 nothing is sent. Otherwise it is stored and the routine sends a fixed run of settle groups with block 0x10424 once (operand C+11112, 0x15810a4) and block 0x11017 four times (0x1581198, 0x1581228, 0x158125c, 0x1581320; the third operand of the second is zero and of the third a computed value, the others come from C+6948 to C+6960), the same sequence `docs/opengl.md` records for a draw-buffer change.
  Pixel-mode dispatch. The pixel mode (s3) is the word at +20 of the window's private record chosen by the index at gp-5736. A mode of 12 or more calls ErrorF with "Unrecognized mode: 0x%x" (string 0x172ae60, `jalr` at 0x1581460) and then goes straight to the common tail with a5 = 1, a0 = 3 and a7 = 0, so none of the colour paths runs. Otherwise a bit-15 field is merged into C+132 (0x15813e0 to 0x15813fc) and the 12-entry table at 0x172a3d8 is used (`jr` at 0x1581404): modes 0, 1 and 7 go to 0x1581814, 2 to 0x15826d4, 3 to 0x158253c, 4 to 0x15823a8, 5 to 0x1582208, 6 to 0x1582034, 8 to 0x1581eb4, 9 to 0x1581d30, 10 to 0x1581bb0 and 11 to 0x1581a30. Each path ORs 0x80000 (modes 0, 1, 7) or 0x100000 (the others) into the word it stores at C+132 and sends block 0x10404 with it, after the settle group when C+7352 is set.
  Colour sending. Index paths (modes 0, 1, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11) store 1 and 3 in C+9828 and C+9832 and send 0x22008001 and 0x23008003 only when the first cached word differs from a5, then 0xce80cc08 with three zero operands and 0xc580c406 with one operand: a2 for modes 0, 1, 7, 10 and 11, a2 shifted left 4 for modes 8 and 9 (0x1581f88, 0x1581e04). RGB paths (modes 2 to 6) cache 0 and 2 and send 0x22008000 and 0x23008002, then three operands after the colour word: mode 2 (0xc580cc08) sends (a2 and 0xf) << 4, a2 and 0xf0, (a2 >> 4) and 0xf0; mode 3 (0xc580cc08) sends (a2 and 0x1f) << 3, (a2 >> 2) and 0xf8, (a2 >> 7) and 0xf8; mode 4 (0xc580cc08) sends a2 and 0xff, (a2 >> 8) and 0xff, (a2 >> 16) and 0xff; modes 5 and 6 send 0xc580cc09 with 10-bit fields cut out at 0x15822b4 to 0x15822e4 and 0x15820e0 to 0x1582130. Every path then builds two words from a4 (C+232: a4 << 12 for modes 0, 1, 7, 10, 11, a4 << 16 for 8 and 9, nibble, five-bit, byte or ten-bit groups of a4 for the RGB modes; C+236: 1 on the index paths, or the repacked upper bits of a4 ORed with 0xf00, 0x800, 0xff0, 0xc00 and 0xfff for modes 2, 3, 4, 5 and 6). When either differs from the cache it is stored and block 0x11023 is sent with C+232, C+236, C+240 and C+244 (0x1581954 and its twins, after the settle group when C+7352 is set).
  Common tail (0x1581494 on). For mode 7 it takes a3 from B+8 and a2 = 0 (0x1581990, 0x1581998); otherwise a3 = B+4 and a2 = the word at B+16 shifted left 13 and masked with 0xe000. Bit 0x20000000 is added to a2 when the raster operation is 0, 3, 12 or 15 (`beq` at 0x15814ac, 0x15814b4, 0x15814c0 and `bne` at 0x15814cc); those are the X raster operations that ignore the destination (inferred from the X numbering). The raster operation is stored at C+200 (0x1581510) and sent as block 0x10422, preceded by the settle group when C+7352 is set. If the halfword at B+106 is nonzero the words C+6948 (= a3) and C+6956 (a word built from fields of the window's flag bytes) are rewritten and the settle runs and block 0x11017 with C+6948 to C+6960 are sent; when it is zero they are rewritten and resent only if they differ from the cached values (0x15819e0, 0x15819ec). B+106 and B+104 are then cleared (0x15816d8, 0x15816e4).
  Rectangle loop. It sends 0x00014400 with operand 7 (0x15816ec, 0x1581708), then for each rectangle four vertices, each the word 0x8080c800 followed by x and y: (x1, y1), (x1, y2), (x2, y2), (x2, y1) (loop 0x1581710 to 0x1581780, 8 bytes per entry, 48 bytes stored per pass, back branch `bne` at 0x1581780 with the last y store in its delay slot). It ends with 0x00014001 and, when C+7036 is nonzero, clears C+7352 and sends 0x10443, 250, 0x10046, 0x10046; it then sets C+7352 and the halfword at B+104 to 1 and returns.
  Return value. No instruction sets v0 for the caller on the main path; the value returned is whatever the body last left there. No branch-likely instruction.
- `odsySolidSpans` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x160f0a0, 7104 bytes): **inferred.** The prologue, the span loop and the exits were read as listings; the descriptor, buffer-select and colour paths were matched to the rectangle fill above by their dispatch table and entry points, not read path by path.
  Draws solid spans as pairs of vertices. Arguments by register: a0 the graphics context, a1 the window, a2 the points (two signed halfwords each, x at +0 and y at +2), a3 the widths (one word each), a4 the span count. The pixel value is the word at Pv+40, the raster operation the byte at Pv+48 and the plane mask the word at Pv+52, where Pv is the context's private record (loads at 0x160f0d0 to 0x160f0d8; Pv+40 is saved at sp+24 and Pv+52 at sp+16). A count of zero or less returns (`blez` at 0x160f174 to 0x160faf4) with the word at Pv+52 as v0 and C+132 cleared in the delay slot. The module-private pixel mode word selects among the 12 table entries at 0x172a6b0 (`jr` at 0x160f744; the targets named in the table are 0x160fb18, 0x160fce8, 0x160fe70, 0x160fffc, 0x161018c, 0x1610318, 0x1610500, 0x16106ac, 0x161084c and 0x16109f8); a mode of 12 or more calls ErrorF with "Unrecognized mode: 0x%x" (`jalr` at 0x160f774). The raster operation is stored at C+200 (0x160f828) and sent as block 0x10422.
  Span loop (0x160fa30 to 0x160fa7c, 4 bytes consumed from each input array per pass, 24 bytes stored). It sends 0x00014400 with operand 1 (0x160fa04, 0x160fa24); for each span it sends two vertices, each 0x8080c800 followed by two words: x then y for the first, and x plus the span's width then y for the second. It ends with 0x00014001 (0x160fa90). When C+7036 is nonzero it clears C+7352 and sends 0x10443, 250, 0x10046, 0x10046; it then sets C+7352 and the halfword at B+104 to 1 and returns through 0x160faec with v0 left as the body last set it.
  Inferred: a two-vertex primitive with begin operand 1 draws a line one pixel high; the rectangle routine's four vertices with begin operand 7 draw a quad.

## Window border and background painters

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`. Both routines treat the window's private-array slot chosen by `dbcWindowPrivateIndex` as an inline word and read and write its bytes +1 and +3; the nfb private record P is the word the array holds at the index in gp-5736, and the table of drawing operations is the word at P+0.

- `nfbPaintWindowBorder` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1621010, 568 bytes): **established.**
  Paints the border region of a window, either as a solid pixel or as a tile. Arguments by register: a0 the window W, a1 the region R to paint, a2 the paint kind, which must be 1: any other value calls ErrorF with the string "nfbPaintWindowBorder: what != PW_BORDER" (0x172f658; `jalr` at 0x1621080) and carries on. The routine calls `nmbxCanSupportBuffers` (`jalr` at 0x162108c); when its result is at least 2 it saves the byte at +1 of the slot in a5 and writes -1 there (0x16210ac, 0x16210b0), and otherwise saves zero, so the byte is marked while painting and put back afterwards.
  Style test. The word at W+124 decides the path. If its bit 29 is set (`bltz` at 0x16210c0) the border is a solid pixel (path at 0x16211d4); otherwise it is a tile. With the top two bits of the word equal to 1 the routine first walks to an ancestor, repeating `a3 = [a3+24]` while the top two bits of that ancestor's word at +124 are still 1 (loop 0x16210d0 to 0x16210e0), and uses that ancestor's origin for the tile.
  Solid path. The box list comes from the word at R+8: when it is zero the region itself is one box (a1 = 1, a0 = R); otherwise a0 is that word plus 8 and a1 is the count at +4 of it. It calls the table entry at +4 (`jalr` at 0x1621208) with a0 the boxes, a1 the count, a2 the word at P+16, a3 = 3, a4 the word at P+8 and a5 = W.
  Tile path. It makes the tile phase from the ancestor's origin and the tile size held in the record at W+112 (width at +12 and height at +14, both unsigned halfwords): x is the signed `lh` load at 0x16210e4 (ancestor +8) and y the one at 0x1621118 (+10), each reduced to the remainder after division by the tile size (the quotient is computed in double precision and truncated toward zero, the product subtracted: 0x16210e4 to 0x1621160), and the two results are stored as halfwords at sp+16 and sp+18. Division by a zero tile size traps (`teq` code 7 at 0x1621150 and 0x162115c). It then calls the table entry at +28 (`jalr` at 0x16211a0) with a0 the boxes, a1 the count, a2 the word at +32 and a3 the word at +28 of the W+112 record, a4 and a5 the tile width and height, a6 the address of the phase pair at sp+16, a7 = 3, and on the stack the word at P+8 and W.
  Exit. The saved byte is restored when the slot's byte +1 still holds -1 (`beq` at 0x16211ec to 0x1621218, `sb` in the return delay slot at 0x1621238); the value restored is the one saved in a5, so a slot that held -1 when nmbx reported fewer than 2 buffers ends as 0. The result in v0 is whatever the last table call left.
- `nfbPaintWindowBackground` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x166c3c0, 636 bytes): **established.**
  Paints the background of a window as nothing, a solid pixel or a tile, after resolving parent-relative backgrounds. Arguments by register: a0 the window, a1 the region (saved at sp+32), a2 the paint kind, which must be 0: otherwise ErrorF prints "nfbPaintWindowBackground: what != PW_BACKGROUND" (0x172f688; `jalr` at 0x166c428).
  Style and ancestor. The top two bits of the word at +124 give the background kind: 0 paints nothing, 1 means parent-relative, 2 a solid pixel, 3 a pixmap tile. For kind 1 it walks to the first ancestor whose kind is not 1 (loop 0x166c454 to 0x166c460, `[s0+24]` each time) and works from that ancestor.
  Slot marking. It reads the bytes at +3 and +1 of the slot. When byte +3 is not -1 and byte +1 is not negative it saves byte +1 in a4 and writes -1 there (0x166c48c to 0x166c494) and then re-reads the kind; a negative byte +1 saves zero without writing. When byte +3 is -1 it saves zero and skips the write. Kinds 0 and 1 end here; kind 2 goes to 0x166c504, kind 3 to 0x166c548.
  Solid path. It uses the box list of the region as the border painter does, then calls the table entry at +4 (`jalr` at 0x166c530) with a2 the word at +12 of the ancestor's nfb record, a3 = 3, a4 the word at +8 of that record and a5 = the original window.
  Tile path. The tile is the record at ancestor+108 (width at +12, height at +14). The phase is computed exactly as in the border painter from the ancestor's origin (stores at sp+16 and sp+18, `teq` code 7 on zero size), and the table entry at +28 (`jalr` at 0x166c604) receives a0 the boxes, a1 the count, a2 the word at +32 and a3 the word at +28 of the tile record, a4 and a5 its width and height, a6 the phase pair, a7 = 3 and on the stack the record word at +8 and the original window.
  Exit (0x166c4bc on). When the slot's byte +1 is -1 the saved value in a4 is written back; v0 is whatever the last call left.

## Segment, glyph, fill and copy routines of the X module read from whole listings

Release IRIX 6.5.30m, `usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so` (N32), read from the whole listing and fact sheet of each routine. The binary alone is the source for these statements.

- `cfb8SegmentSS1RectCopy` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1692ae0, 2532 bytes): **established.**
  Draws a list of independent line segments one pixel wide into an 8-bit-per-pixel drawable, storing the foreground pixel word with `sb`, and stops at the first segment that is not wholly inside the single clip box. `cfb8SegmentSS1Rect` (0x16dbcb0) selects it for mode 3. Arguments: a0 the drawable, a1 the graphics context, a2 the segment count (kept in v0 and at sp+0), a3 the array of 8-byte segments (two packed halfword points each; kept in v1 and at sp+8 and sp+80). Frame 176 bytes, gp 0x173a9c0.
  Set-up. The word at gp+19072 (read at 0x1692b10) indexes the table at +436 of the word at drawable+16: a negative index (`bltz` at 0x1692b28) leaves the zero-bias value at sp+16 (store at 0x169347c), otherwise the table word is the bias kept at sp+16. The byte at drawable+0 equal to 1 selects the drawable itself (0x1692b74, branch to 0x1693480); any other value uses the word at +116 of the word at drawable+16. The row pitch is that record's word at +28 (kept at sp+64), the signed halfword at drawable+10 is multiplied by it (`mult` at 0x1692b9c) and added to the record's word at +32 and the halfword at drawable+8 to give the origin address kept at sp+32. Bits 28 and 29 of the word at gc+16 are kept at sp+40 and nonzero means the last pixel of each segment is drawn. The word at gc+76 indexed by the word at gp+19120 gives the per-context record; its word at +8 points at the clip box (two packed points, loaded at 0x1692bac and 0x1692bb4), its word at +12 is the pixel word kept in s6. The clip box is moved by the drawable origin (the low halfword sign-extended by the `andi`/`subu` pair at 0x1692ba0 to 0x1692bb0), and the upper corner is reduced by one in each halfword (the constant formed by `lui 0xfffe` and `ori 0xffff` at 0x1692bcc to 0x1692bd4, added at 0x1692bdc), giving sp+48 and sp+56. A zero count (`beq` at 0x1692bec) goes to 0x1693434 and returns -1 (0x1693470).
  Per segment (loop head 0x1692dc4). Both points are loaded from the current segment (sp+80). Each point is tested against the two clip corners by subtracting in packed form, OR-ing the four differences and masking with 0x80008000 (t3 set at 0x1692bfc); any sign bit set is outside, and `bne` at 0x1692e1c leaves for 0x16933e4 before the segment counter at sp+72 or the pointer a2 move on. Inside, the deltas dx and dy come from the unpacked halfwords, the start address is the origin plus y1 times the pitch plus x1 (`mult` at 0x1692e28), s4 is the x step of +1 or -1 and s5 the y step of plus or minus the pitch (dy below zero selects -pitch; the octant word v0 is 2 or 0 for the y sign, plus 4 for a negative dx).
  Cases. dy equal to zero (`beq` at 0x1692e78 or 0x1693170) is a horizontal run: the length is |dx|, plus one when the cap flag is set, and a negative dx first moves the start address left by |dx| (0x16933b8 to 0x16933cc, which also skips the first pixel when the cap flag is clear). The run is stored a word at a time: short runs (under five bytes counting the start offset) combine one start mask from the table at offset 19184 and one end mask from offset 19200 of the page at local GOT 1018 (gp-28680) into a single read-modify-write (0x16931b8 to 0x16931ec); longer runs merge a left partial word using the mask at offset 19152, store whole words of the pixel word (a count loop of 1 to 7 first at 0x1693254, then eight-word passes at 0x1693290 to 0x16932b0, with a straight run of eight stores at 0x169348c taken when exactly one group of eight words is left), and merge a right partial word with the mask at offset 19168. Otherwise the major axis is the larger of |dx| and |dy| (the `slt` at 0x1692e84; the two step registers swap when |dx| is the smaller), the error term starts at minus the major length minus the bias bit (the bias word shifted right by the octant, a variable right shift at 0x1692ec4), and each pixel adds twice the minor length to the error and, when the sum is not negative, subtracts twice the major length and also takes the minor step (the `slti`/`movz` pairs). The pixel count is the major length, or one less when the cap flag is clear. Whole passes of 16 pixels (loop 0x1692f00 to 0x1693100, gp passes) compute sixteen addresses and then issue sixteen `sb` stores; the remainder below 16 (sltiu at 0x169312c) jumps through the 15-entry table at 0x1729388 (`jr` at 0x1693144) to an entry of the unrolled chain at 0x1692c18 to 0x1692d98, and the last pixel is the `sb` at 0x1692db0.
  Exit. After the last segment the pointer a2 is compared with the end (sp+24, 0x1692db8) and 0x16933d4 sets a2 to the start plus 8 times the counter; the result is -1 (0x1693470) when the count minus the counter (sp+0 minus sp+72) is not positive, otherwise the number of 8-byte steps from the start to a2 (the `subu`/`sra` at 0x1693428 to 0x1693430), which for an early stop is the index of the failing segment plus one. The routine calls nothing.
- `cfbGetSpans` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x171dd30, 2500 bytes): **inferred.**
  Copies horizontal spans out of a drawable into a packed destination, one run of whole words per span. Arguments: a0 the drawable (saved at sp+16), a1 not read, a2 the array of halfword points (x, y) (copied to a7), a3 the array of span widths (copied to s0), a4 the span count (copied to s2) and a5 the destination (copied to a6). Frame 208 bytes, gp 0x173a9c0. The byte at drawable+3 selects the layout: 1 goes to 0x171e32c (bit-per-pixel), 8 goes to 0x171e324 and on to 0x171dda4 (byte-per-pixel), and any other value calls `FatalError` (global GOT slot 1127, `jalr` at 0x171dd90, a0 the string at the local-GOT page 1018 plus 24048, with a2 and a5 passed on in a7 and a6) and then, if it returns, falls into the byte path. In each path a drawable whose byte at +0 is zero uses the word at +116 of the word at +16 (0x171e6ec and 0x171ddc4), and the row pitch in words is the word at +28 shifted right two and the base the word at +32.
  Byte path. A count of one with a width word of 1 (`beq s2,t3` at 0x171ddd8 and `bne a2,t3` at 0x171e294) is a single pixel: it loads the byte at base plus 4 times y times the word pitch plus x, shifts it left 24 and stores it as the word at the destination (the store in the delay slot of the `jr ra` at 0x171e2ec, to the address held in a6), returning with nothing else written. A count of zero or less returns at 0x171e2f4. Otherwise each span (loop 0x171de58): x and y are the two halfwords, the end is clamped to the row's byte count (`slt`/`movn` at 0x171de70 to 0x171de7c), the start byte offset is x and 3, and the first source word is at base plus 4 times (y times the word pitch plus x shifted right two). The partial-word masks come from the tables at offsets 19136, 19152, 19168, 19184 and 19200 of the page at local GOT 1018 (loaded into s1 at 0x171dd84). A span that fits two words (start offset plus width below 5, `slti` at 0x171deb0) is merged into one or two destination words with a read-modify-write (0x171ded0 to 0x171df50); a longer span merges a partial first destination word when the start mask at 19152 is nonzero, then moves whole destination words, each built from two adjacent source words with a right shift by 8 times the start offset (a variable right shift) and a left shift by the complement (`sllv`), two words per pass of the loop 0x171e120 to 0x171e164 with the single-word step at 0x171e170, and ends with a merged partial word using the end mask at 19168 (0x171e084 to 0x171e21c). The destination pointer then advances by the words written and the next span starts.
  Bit path (0x171e4ec): the row width in bits is the word pitch times 32 (sp+104), the end of each span is clamped to it (0x171e520 to 0x171e528), the start bit is x and 31 and the source address is base plus 4 times (y times the word pitch plus x shifted right five). A span that ends in the first source word (start bit plus width below 33, `sltiu` at 0x171e550) is one merged destination word using the mask pair at offsets 19344 and 19816 indexed by the width (0x171e570 to 0x171e598). Longer spans merge a first partial word (masks at 19816 and 19344 indexed by the start bit and its complement, 0x171e390 to 0x171e3d8), move whole words by funnel shifts of two source words (loop 0x171e5a0 to 0x171e614), and finish with the tail masks from the table at 19952 (indexed by 32 times the start bit plus the end bit, 0x171e4ac to 0x171e4d4) or the pair at 19688 and 19216 (0x171e65c to 0x171e6a4).
  Every path returns with no result set, through the restores at 0x171e2f8 to 0x171e31c, except the single-pixel exit above. Graded inferred because the funnel-shift passes and the mask-table indexes were matched by shape and not worked out word for word.
- `cfb32LineSS1RectCopy` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x165a180, 2492 bytes): **established.**
  Draws a connected run of one-pixel-wide lines through a list of points into a 32-bit-per-pixel drawable, storing the foreground pixel word with `sw`, and stops at the first point outside the single clip box. Arguments: a0 the drawable, a1 the graphics context (saved at sp+176), a2 not read, a3 the point count (saved at sp+8), a4 the array of 4-byte packed points (x in the high halfword, y in the low; saved at sp+0 and sp+64), a5 a second pointer to the original point array (saved at sp+168, used only in the closing test below). Frame 288 bytes, gp 0x173a9c0. The result is in v0, set to 1 at 0x165a194 before anything else.
  Set-up. As in `cfb8SegmentSS1RectCopy` (0x1692ae0), the word at gp+19072 indexes the table at +436 of the word at drawable+16 to give the bias (sp+24; zero when the index is negative, 0x165aa74). The pixmap is the drawable itself when its byte at +0 is 1 (0x165aa78) and otherwise the word at +436 of the word at drawable+16 indexed by the word at gp+19292 (loads at 0x165a1cc and 0x165a1e8); the 8-bit routine reads the word at offset 116 instead. The per-context record is the word at gc+76 indexed by the word at gp+19288, the clip box is that record's word at +8, and the pixel word (gp) is its word at +12. The clip box is moved by the drawable origin (halfword-corrected word at drawable+8) and its upper corner reduced by one in each halfword (the constant 0xfffeffff built by the `lui` at 0x165a1d0 and the `ori` at 0x165a1d8), giving sp+32 and sp+40. The row pitch in words is the pixmap's word at +28 shifted right two (sp+48); the start address is the pixmap word at +32 plus 4 times (drawable origin y times pitch plus origin x) plus 4 times (y1 times pitch plus x1) of the first point (0x165a26c to 0x165a2d8).
  First point. The same packed subtract-and-mask test with 0x80008000 (s1) is applied to the first point (0x165a230 to 0x165a248); if it fails, the routine returns 1 at 0x165a264 without drawing. A count of one (`beq` at 0x165a2d4) skips the loop and goes to the closing test.
  Per segment (0x165a574). The next point is the word at +4 of the pointer at sp+64, tested against the clip box the same way; a failure goes to 0x165aa18. Otherwise the deltas, the direction signs and the major and minor lengths are formed as in the segment routine (pixel steps of plus or minus 1 word and plus or minus the pitch), the error starts at minus the major length minus the bias bit (a variable right shift at 0x165a630), and each pixel stores the pixel word, adds twice the minor length to the error and takes the extra minor step when it is not negative, with the last pixel of the segment left for the next segment to start on. The drawable's byte at +0 equal to 1 selects itself (0x16ed410), otherwise the pixmap is the word at +436 of the word at drawable+16 indexed by the word at gp+19292 (0x16ed41c to 0x16ed430); the pitch word it loads at 0x16ed434, shifted right two (signed, 0x16ed444 to 0x16ed45c), is the row pitch in words (s8) and its word at +32 the base (a3). The pixel is the context record's word at +12 (a6). A count of zero returns. For each glyph (0x16ed4f4) the first row address is the base plus 4 times (pitch times (y minus ascent) plus x plus the left bearing), the row count is ascent plus descent (zero skips the glyph, 0x16ed538), the row width in bits is right minus left bearing, the bitmap row stride is ((width plus 7) shifted right three, plus one) rounded down to even (0x16ed554, 0x16ed5b0 to 0x16ed5c4), and x advances by the advance width.
